===Original===
To 

 **John Emerson**

===Chinese===
献给

**约翰·埃默森**（John Emerson）

## I Gentlemen Prefer Blondes / 一 绅士爱美人

===Original===
March 16th:
A gentleman friend and I were dining at the Ritz last evening and he said that if I took a pencil and a paper and put down all of my thoughts it would make a book. This almost made me smile as what it would really make would be a whole row of encyclopediacs. I mean I seem to be thinking practically all of the time. I mean it is my favorite recreation and sometimes I sit for hours and do not seem to do anything else but think. So this gentleman said a girl with brains ought to do something else with them besides think. And he said he ought to know brains when he sees them, because he is in the senate and he spends quite a great deal of time in Washington, d. c., and when he comes into contract with brains he always notices it. So it might have all blown over but this morning he sent me a book. And so when my maid brought it to me, I said to her, “Well, Lulu, here is another book and we have not read half the ones we have got yet.” But when I opened it and saw that it was all a blank I remembered what my gentleman acquaintance said, and so then I realized that it was a diary. So here I am writing a book instead of reading one.

===Chinese===
3月16日：
昨晚一位绅士朋友和我在丽兹酒店（the Ritz）吃饭，他说，我要是拿一支铅笔一张纸，把我所有的想法都写下来，那就能成一本书了。这话差点让我笑出来，因为真写下来的话，那可不止是一本书，得是整整一排百科全书了。我是说，我好像几乎无时无刻不在思考。我是说，思考就是我最喜欢的消遣，有时候我一坐好几个钟头，除了思考以外好像什么都没干。于是这位绅士就说，一个有头脑的姑娘，总该拿头脑干点思考以外的什么事。他还说他一见到头脑就认得出来，因为他在参议院里，大部分时间都待在华盛顿，凡是跟头脑打上交道，他总能注意到。这事本来可能就这么过去了，可是今天早上他送了我一本书。女仆把书拿来的时候，我跟她说：「哎，露露（Lulu），又来一本书，咱们手里这些还没读到一半呢。」可是我打开一看，里面全是空白页，我才想起我那位绅士相识说过的话，这才明白过来，原来这是一本日记。于是我如今就在写书了，而不是读书。

===Original===
But now it is the 16th of March and of course it is to late to begin with January, but it does not matter as my gentleman friend, Mr. Eisman, was in town practically all of January and February, and when he is in town one day seems to be practically the same as the next day.

===Chinese===
不过现在已经是3月16日了，要从1月写起当然太迟，不过没关系，因为我的绅士朋友艾斯曼先生（Mr. Eisman）1月和2月几乎全程都在城里，而他在城里的时候，今天跟明天几乎没什么两样。

===Original===
I mean Mr. Eisman is in the wholesale button profession in Chicago and he is the gentleman who is known practically all over Chicago as Gus Eisman the Button King. And he is the gentleman who is interested in educating me, so of course he is always coming down to New York to see how my brains have improved since the last time. But when Mr. Eisman is in New York we always seem to do the same thing and if I wrote down one day in my diary, all I would have to do would be to put quotation marks for all other days. I mean we always seem to have dinner at the Colony and see a show and go to the Trocadero and then Mr. Eisman shows me to my apartment. So of course when a gentleman is interested in educating a girl, he likes to stay and talk about the topics of the day until quite late, so I am quite fatigued the next day and I do not really get up until it is time to dress for dinner at the Colony.

===Chinese===
我是说，艾斯曼先生在芝加哥做的是纽扣批发生意，整个芝加哥几乎人人都管他叫「纽扣大王」格斯·艾斯曼（Gus Eisman）。他就是那位热衷于教育我的绅士，所以他老是往纽约跑，来看看我的头脑自上次以来有了多少长进。可是艾斯曼先生在纽约的时候，我们做的事总是一模一样，我要是把其中一天写进日记，其余的日子只要打上引号就行了。我是说，我们总是在科洛尼餐厅（the Colony）吃晚饭，看一场演出，再去特罗卡德罗（the Trocadero），然后艾斯曼先生送我回公寓。当然啦，一位绅士既然有心教育一个姑娘，就喜欢留下来谈谈时事，一直谈到很晚，于是第二天我就相当疲惫，直到该换衣服去科洛尼吃晚饭的时候才真正起床。

===Original===
It would be strange if I turn out to be an authoress. I mean at my home near Little Rock, Arkansas, my family all wanted me to do something about my music. Because all of my friends said I had talent and they all kept after me and kept after me about practising. But some way I never seemed to care so much about practising. I mean I simply could not sit for hours and hours at a time practising just for the sake of a career. So one day I got quite tempermental and threw the old mandolin clear across the room and I have really never touched it since. But writing is different because you do not have to learn or practise and it is more tempermental because practising seems to take all the temperment out of me. So now I really almost have to smile because I have just noticed that I have written clear across two pages onto March 18th, so this will do for today and tomorrow. And it just shows how tempermental I am when I get started.

===Chinese===
要是我最后成了个女作家，那可就怪了。我是说，在阿肯色州小石城附近的老家，我们全家都巴望我在音乐上搞出点名堂。因为我所有的朋友都说我有天赋，他们不停地催我练习、催我练习。可是不知怎么，我对练习从来就没那么上心。我是说，我实在没法为了一项事业，一坐就是好几个钟头地练。于是有一天我艺术家脾气大发，把那把旧曼陀林整个儿扔到了房间那头，从那以后我真就再没碰过它。可是写作就不一样了，因为不用学也不用练，而且更有艺术家脾气，因为练习好像会把我的艺术家脾气全都练没了。所以现在我几乎真要笑出来了，因为我刚发现自己一口气写满了整整两页，一直写到了3月18日，这样今天和明天的份就都写完了。这正好说明我一动起笔来，艺术家脾气有多厉害。

===Original===
March 19th:
Well last evening Dorothy called up and Dorothy said she has met a gentleman who gave himself an introduction to her in the lobby of the Ritz. So then they went to luncheon and tea and dinner and then they went to a show and then they went to the Trocadero. So Dorothy said his name was Lord Cooksleigh but what she really calls him is Coocoo. So Dorothy said why don’t you and I and Coocoo go to the Follies tonight and bring Gus along if he is in town? So then Dorothy and I had quite a little quarrel because every time that Dorothy mentions the subject of Mr. Eisman she calls Mr. Eisman by his first name, and she does not seem to realize that when a gentleman who is as important as Mr. Eisman, spends quite a lot of money educating a girl, it really does not show reverance to call a gentleman by his first name. I mean I never even think of calling Mr. Eisman by his first name, but if I want to call him anything at all, I call him “Daddy” and I do not even call him “Daddy” if a place seems to be public. So I told Dorothy that Mr. Eisman would not be in town until day after tomorrow. So then Dorothy and Coocoo came up and we went to the Follies.

===Chinese===
3月19日：
昨天晚上多萝西（Dorothy）打来电话，说她在丽兹酒店的大堂里结识了一位自己凑上来作自我介绍的绅士。然后他们一起去吃了午饭、喝了下午茶、吃了晚饭，又去看了场演出，然后去了特罗卡德罗。多萝西说他的名字是库克斯利勋爵（Lord Cooksleigh），不过她实际管他叫「咕咕」（Coocoo）。于是多萝西说，今晚你、我加上咕咕一起去看《福利斯》歌舞团（the Follies）吧，要是格斯在城里，把他也带上？于是多萝西和我就小小地吵了一架，因为多萝西每次一提起艾斯曼先生，就直呼他的教名，她好像意识不到，一位像艾斯曼先生这么有身份的绅士，花了那么多钱教育一个姑娘，直呼人家的教名实在显得不够尊敬。我是说，我从来连想都没想过直呼艾斯曼先生的教名，我要是想叫他什么的话，我就叫他「爸爸」，而且要是场合显得人太多，我连「爸爸」都不叫。于是我告诉多萝西，艾斯曼先生后天才会到。然后多萝西和咕咕就上来了，我们一起去看了《福利斯》。

===Original===
So this morning Coocoo called up and he wanted me to luncheon at the Ritz. I mean these foreigners really have quite a nerve. Just because Coocoo is an Englishman and a Lord he thinks a girl can waste hours on him just for a luncheon at the Ritz, when all he does is talk about some exposition he went on to a place called Tibet and after talking for hours I found out that all they were was a lot of Chinamen. So I will be quite glad to see Mr. Eisman when he gets in. Because he always has something quite interesting to talk about, as for instants the last time he was here he presented me with quite a beautiful emerald bracelet. So next week is my birthday and he always has some delightful surprise on holidays.

===Chinese===
今天早上咕咕打来电话，想约我去丽兹吃午饭。我是说，这些外国人脸皮可真够厚的。就因为咕咕是个英国人、是个勋爵，他就觉得一个姑娘可以为他浪费好几个钟头，就为在丽兹吃顿午饭，而他满嘴谈的不过是他在一个叫西藏的地方赶上的什么博览会，聊了几个钟头我才发现，那儿的人不过是好多中国人。所以艾斯曼先生到的时候，我一定会很乐意见到他。因为他总有些相当有趣的东西可谈，比如说上次他来的时候，送了我一只相当漂亮的祖母绿手镯。下周就是我的生日了，逢年过节他总有些讨人喜欢的小惊喜。

===Original===
I did intend to luncheon at the Ritz with Dorothy today and of course Coocoo had to spoil it, as I told him that I could not luncheon with him today, because my brother was in town on business and had the mumps, so I really could not leave him alone. Because of course if I went to the Ritz now I would bump into Coocoo. But I sometimes almost have to smile at my own imagination, because of course I have not got any brother and I have not even thought of the mumps for years. I mean it is no wonder that I can write.

===Chinese===
我本来今天是打算和多萝西在丽兹吃午饭的，咕咕当然非要来搅局，我告诉他今天不能跟他吃午饭，因为我哥哥进城办事，得了腮腺炎，我实在不能丢下他一个人不管。因为要是现在去丽兹，我准会撞上咕咕。不过我有时候几乎要被自己的想象力逗笑，因为我压根就没有什么哥哥，腮腺炎这种事我更是好多年没想过了。我是说，我能写作一点也不奇怪。

===Original===
So the reason I thought I would take luncheon at the Ritz was because Mr. Chaplin is at the Ritz and I always like to renew old acquaintances, because I met Mr. Chaplin once when we were both working on the same lot in Hollywood and I am sure he would remember me. Gentlemen always seem to remember blondes. I mean the only career I would like to be besides an authoress is a cinema star and I was doing quite well in the cinema when Mr. Eisman made me give it all up. Because of course when a gentleman takes such a friendly interest in educating a girl as Mr. Eisman does, you like to show that you appreciate it, and he is against a girl being in the cinema because his mother is authrodox.

===Chinese===
我原本想在丽兹吃午饭，是因为卓别林先生（Mr. Chaplin）眼下就住在丽兹，我一向喜欢跟老相识叙叙旧，因为我跟卓别林先生见过一面，那时我们俩都在好莱坞同一家片场干活，我肯定他还记得我。绅士们好像总是记得金发女郎。我是说，除了当女作家，我唯一想干的事业就是当电影明星，而且我在电影界本来干得相当不错，是艾斯曼先生让我把这一切都放弃的。因为当然啦，一位绅士像艾斯曼先生这样对教育一个姑娘怀着如此友好的兴趣，你总得表示领情，而他反对姑娘混电影界，因为他母亲是个古板正统的人。

===Original===
March 20th:
Mr. Eisman gets in tomorrow to be here in time for my birthday. So I thought it would really be delightful to have at least one good time before Mr. Eisman got in, so last evening I had some literary gentlemen in to spend the evening because Mr. Eisman always likes me to have literary people in and out of the apartment. I mean he is quite anxious for a girl to improve her mind and his greatest interest in me is because I always seem to want to improve my mind and not waste any time. And Mr. Eisman likes me to have what the French people call a “salo” which means that people all get together in the evening and improve their minds. So I invited all of the brainy gentlemen I could think up. So I thought up a gentleman who is the proffessor of all of the economics up at Columbia College, and the editor who is the famous editor of the New York Transcript and another gentleman who is a famous playright who writes very, very famous plays that are all about Life. I mean anybody would recognize his name but it always seems to slip my memory because all of we real friends of his only call him Sam. So Sam asked if he could bring a gentleman who writes novels from England, so I said yes, so he brought him. And then we all got together and I called up Gloria and Dorothy and the gentleman brought their own liquor. So of course the place was a wreck this morning and Lulu and I worked like proverbial dogs to get it cleaned up, but Heaven knows how long it will take to get the chandelier fixed.

===Chinese===
3月20日：
艾斯曼先生明天就到，好赶上我的生日。所以我想，趁艾斯曼先生还没到，先好好痛快一回才叫妙呢，于是昨晚我请了几位文学绅士到公寓里消磨晚间时光，因为艾斯曼先生一向喜欢我让文人进出公寓。我是说，他巴不得一个姑娘增进心智，而他对我最大的兴趣就在于，我好像总想着增进心智，一点时间也不肯浪费。艾斯曼先生还喜欢我搞法国人管它叫「沙龙」的那种聚会，意思就是大家晚上都聚到一起，增进彼此的心智。于是我把能想起来的有头脑的绅士都请了来。我头一个想起的是哥伦比亚学院里教全部经济学的教授，还有《纽约实录报》那位大名鼎鼎的主编，还有另一位绅士，是个著名剧作家，写的都是非常非常出名的戏，全是关于「人生」的。我是说，谁都认得出他的名字，可我老是想不起来，因为我们这些他真正的老朋友都只管他叫「山姆」。山姆问能不能再带一位从英国来的写小说的绅士，我说好，他就把人带来了。然后我们大家聚在一起，我给格洛丽亚（Gloria）和多萝西打了电话，绅士们自带了酒水。于是今天早上公寓自然是一片狼藉，我和露露干得像老话说的狗一样卖力才收拾干净，不过老天爷才知道，修好那盏枝形吊灯得花多久。

===Original===
March 22nd:
Well my birthday has come and gone but it was really quite depressing. I mean it seems to me a gentleman who has a friendly interest in educating a girl like Gus Eisman, would want her to have the biggest square cut diamond in New York. I mean I must say I was quite disappointed when he came to the apartment with a little thing you could hardly see. So I told him I thought it was quite cute, but I had quite a headache and I had better stay in a dark room all day and I told him I would see him the next day, perhaps. Because even Lulu thought it was quite small and she said, if she was I, she really would do something definite and she said she always believed in the old addage, “Leave them while you’re looking good.” But he came in at dinner time with really a very very beautiful bracelet of square cut diamonds so I was quite cheered up. So then we had dinner at the Colony and we went to a show and supper at the Trocadero as usual whenever he is in town. But I will give him credit that he realized how small it was. I mean he kept talking about how bad business was and the button profession was full of bolshevicks who make nothing but trouble. Because Mr. Eisman feels that the country is really on the verge of the bolshevicks and I become quite worried. I mean if the bolshevicks do get in, there is only one gentleman who could handle them and that is Mr. D. W. Griffith. Because I will never forget when Mr. Griffith was directing *Intolerance*. I mean it was my last cinema just before Mr. Eisman made me give up my career and I was playing one of the girls that fainted at the battle when all of the gentlemen fell off the tower. And when I saw how Mr. Griffith handled all of those mobs in *Intolerance* I realized that he could do anything, and I really think that the government of America ought to tell Mr. Griffith to get all ready if the bolshevicks start to do it.

===Chinese===
3月22日：
唉，我的生日来了又过去了，可过得实在叫人相当郁闷。我是说，要我说，像格斯·艾斯曼这样对教育一个姑娘怀着友好兴趣的绅士，就该送她全纽约最大的一颗方形切割钻石。我是说，我必须承认，他到公寓时拿着一件小得几乎看不见的东西，我相当失望。于是我告诉他，我觉得这东西相当可爱，不过我头疼得厉害，最好一整天都待在暗房间里，还说我第二天再见他，也许吧。因为连露露都觉得那颗小，她说，她要是我，就真来点狠的，还说她一向信那句老话：「趁你还没人老珠黄，赶紧抽身走人。」可到了晚饭时分，他拿着一串非常非常漂亮的方形切割钻石手镯又来了，于是我精神大振。然后我们照例在科洛尼吃晚饭、看演出、去特罗卡德罗吃夜宵——只要他在城里都是这一套。不过我得给他记一功，他自己也明白那颗有多小。我是说，他一直在念叨生意多不景气，纽扣行业里全是布尔什维克，净惹麻烦。因为艾斯曼先生觉得这个国家真的已经到了布尔什维克的边缘，我也跟着相当忧心。我是说，万一布尔什维克真进来了，全美国只有一位绅士治得了他们，那就是 D. W. 格里菲斯先生（Mr. D. W. Griffith）。因为我永远忘不了格里菲斯先生执导《党同伐异》的那段日子。我是说，那是我的最后一部电影，就在艾斯曼先生让我放弃事业之前，我演的是打仗时昏倒的姑娘之一，当时所有的绅士都从塔上摔了下去。当我看到格里菲斯先生在《党同伐异》里把那些大群大群的人调度得服服帖帖，我意识到他什么事都办得到，我真的认为美国政府应该通知格里菲斯先生随时做好准备，以防布尔什维克动手。

===Original===
Well I forgot to mention that the English gentleman who writes novels seems to have taken quite an interest in me, as soon as he found out that I was literary. I mean he has called up every day and I went to tea twice with him. So he has sent me a whole complete set of books for my birthday by a gentleman called Mr. Conrad. They all seem to be about ocean travel although I have not had time to more than glance through them. I have always liked novels about ocean travel ever since I posed for Mr. Christie for the front cover of a novel about ocean travel by McGrath because I always say that a girl never really looks as well as she does on board a steamship, or even a yacht.

===Chinese===
对了，我忘了提那位写小说的英国绅士，他一发现我有文学气质，好像就对我产生了相当大的兴趣。我是说，他天天打电话来，我还跟他喝了两次下午茶。他送了我一整套书做生日礼物，作者是一位叫康拉德先生的先生（Mr. Conrad）。那些书好像全讲的是海上旅行，不过我一直没时间，只草草翻了几眼。我一向喜欢海上旅行的小说，因为我曾为克里斯蒂先生当过模特，拍麦格拉思一本海上旅行小说的封面，因为我老说，一个姑娘只有在轮船上，哪怕游艇上也行，才显得那么好看。

===Original===
So the English gentleman’s name is Mr. Gerald Lamson as those who have read his novels would know. And he also sent me some of his own novels and they all seem to be about middle age English gentlemen who live in the country over in London and seem to ride bicycles, which seems quite different from America, except at Palm Beach. So I told Mr. Lamson how I write down all of my thoughts and he said he knew I had something to me from the first minute he saw me and when we become better acquainted I am going to let him read my diary. I mean I even told Mr. Eisman about him and he is quite pleased. Because of course Mr. Lamson is quite famous and it seems Mr. Eisman has read all of his novels going to and fro on the trains and Mr. Eisman is always anxious to meet famous people and take them to the Ritz to dinner on Saturday night. But of course I did not tell Mr. Eisman that I am really getting quite a little crush on Mr. Lamson, which I really believe I am, but Mr. Eisman thinks my interest in him is more literary.

===Chinese===
这位英国绅士名叫杰拉尔德·拉姆森先生（Gerald Lamson），读过他小说的人都知道。他还送了我几本他自己的小说，写的似乎全是人到中年、住在伦敦乡下的英国绅士，还都爱骑自行车，这跟美国好像很不一样，棕榈滩除外。于是我告诉拉姆森先生，我怎样把我所有的想法都写下来，他说打见到我的头一分钟起，就知道我这个人身上有东西，等我们再熟一点，我就打算让他读我的日记。我是说，我连他的事都告诉艾斯曼先生了，他还相当高兴。因为拉姆森先生当然很有名气，而艾斯曼先生好像坐火车来来回回的时候就读遍了他的小说，艾斯曼先生又总爱结交名人，周六晚上带他们去丽兹吃晚饭。不过我当然没告诉艾斯曼先生，我对拉姆森先生真有点动心了，我相信我确实是动心了，可艾斯曼先生以为我对他的兴趣更多是文学上的。

===Original===
March 30th:
At last Mr. Eisman has left on the *20th Century* and I must say I am quite fatigued and a little rest will be quite welcome. I mean I do not mind staying out late every night if I dance, but Mr. Eisman is really not such a good dancer so most of the time we just sit and drink some champagne or have a bite to eat and of course I do not dance with anyone else when I am out with Mr. Eisman. But Mr. Eisman and Gerry, as Mr. Lamson wants me to call him, became quite good friends and we had several evenings, all three together. So now that Mr. Eisman is out of town at last, Gerry and I are going out together this evening and Gerry said not to dress up, because Gerry seems to like me more for my soul. So I really had to tell Gerry that if all the gentlemen were like he seems to be, Madame Frances’ whole dress making establishment would have to go out of business. But Gerry does not like a girl to be nothing else but a doll, but he likes her to bring in her husband’s slippers every evening and make him forget what he has gone through.

===Chinese===
3月30日：
艾斯曼先生终于坐《20世纪》号列车走了，我必须说我相当疲惫，稍微歇一歇会是很受欢迎的。我是说，要是跳舞，每晚熬夜我并不在乎，可是艾斯曼先生实在算不上跳舞的好手，所以大部分时间我们只是坐着喝点香槟，或者吃点东西，而且跟艾斯曼先生出门时，我当然不跟别人跳。不过艾斯曼先生和格里——也就是拉姆森先生要我这么叫他——成了相当好的朋友，我们好几个晚上都是三个人一起过的。如今艾斯曼先生总算出城了，今晚格里和我要一起出门，格里说不用盛装打扮，因为格里好像更喜欢我的灵魂。于是我只好告诉格里，要是所有的绅士都像他这样，弗朗西斯夫人（Madame Frances）的整家裁缝铺就只好关门大吉了。不过格里不喜欢一个姑娘光当个洋娃娃，他喜欢她每天傍晚给丈夫递拖鞋，让他忘掉自己吃过的一切苦头。

===Original===
But before Mr. Eisman went to Chicago he told me that he is going to Paris this summer on professional business and I think he intends to present me with a trip to Paris as he says there is nothing so educational as traveling. I mean it did worlds of good to Dorothy when she went abroad last spring and I never get tired of hearing her telling how the merry-go-rounds in Paris have pigs instead of horses. But I really do not know whether to be thrilled or not because, of course, if I go to Paris I will have to leave Gerry and both Gerry and I have made up our minds not to be separated from one another from now on.

===Chinese===
不过在艾斯曼先生去芝加哥之前，他告诉我今年夏天他要因公务去巴黎，我想他是有意要送我一次巴黎之行，因为他说没有什么比旅行更能长见识的了。我是说，去年春天多萝西出国一趟可是受益匪浅，她讲巴黎的旋转木马上骑的不是木马而是小猪，我怎么听都听不腻。不过我还真不知道该不该激动，因为要是去巴黎，我就得离开格里，而格里和我都已经下定决心，从今往后再也不分开。

===Original===
March 31st:
Last night Gerry and I had dinner at quite a quaint place where we had roast beef and baked potato. I mean he always wants me to have food which is what he calls “nourishing” which most gentlemen never seem to think about. So then we took a hansom cab and drove for hours around the park because Gerry said the air would be good for me. It is really very sweet to have someone think of all those things that gentlemen hardly ever seem to think about. So then we talked quite a lot. I mean Gerry knows how to draw a girl out and I told him things that I really would not even put in my diary. So when he heard all about my life he became quite depressed and we both had tears in our eyes. Because he said he never dreamed a girl could go through so much as I, and come out so sweet and not made bitter by it all. I mean Gerry thinks that most gentlemen are brutes and hardly ever think about a girl’s soul.

===Chinese===
3月31日：
昨晚格里和我在一家相当古雅的地方吃饭，吃的是烤牛肉和烤土豆。我是说，他总要我吃他所谓的「滋补」食物，大多数绅士从来想不到这些。然后我们雇了一辆双轮马车，在公园里转了好几个钟头，因为格里说那儿的空气对我有好处。有个人肯替你想这些绅士们几乎从来想不到的事，真是非常贴心。然后我们聊了相当多。我是说，格里懂得怎么把一个姑娘的心里话引出来，我告诉了他一些我连日记里都绝不会写的事。他听完我这一生的经历，变得相当伤感，我们两个眼里都含着泪。因为他说他从没想到，一个姑娘能经历我这么多事，还能出落得这么甜美，一点没有被这一切变得刻薄。我是说，格里觉得大多数绅士都是粗人，几乎从不顾及一个姑娘的灵魂。

===Original===
So it seems that Gerry has had quite a lot of trouble himself and he can not even get married on account of his wife. He and she have never been in love with each other but she was a suffragette and asked him to marry her, so what could he do? So we rode all around the park until quite late talking and philosophizing quite a lot and I finally told him that I thought, after all, that bird life was the highest form of civilization. So Gerry calls me his little thinker and I really would not be surprised if all of my thoughts will give him quite a few ideas for his novels. Because Gerry says he has never seen a girl of my personal appearance with so many brains. And he had almost given up looking for his ideal when our paths seemed to cross each other and I told him I really thought a thing like that was nearly always the result of fate.

===Chinese===
这么说吧，格里自己好像也受过相当多的苦，他因为有妻子，连婚都结不成。他和她从来就没有相爱过，可她是个女权运动者，主动开口向他求的婚，他又能怎么办呢？于是我们坐车绕着公园转到很晚，聊了好多，还谈了好多哲理，最后我告诉他，我到底还是认为，鸟类的生活才是文明的最高形式。于是格里管我叫他的小思想家，我的这些想法真能给他小说提供不少灵感，我一点也不会奇怪。因为格里说，他从来没见过哪个姑娘长着我这样的容貌，还有这么多的头脑。他本来几乎已经放弃寻找他的理想了，我们的路却好像交叉到了一起，我告诉他，我真心觉得这种事几乎总是命运的结果。

===Original===
So Gerry says that I remind him quite a lot of Helen of Troy, who was of Greek extraction. But the only Greek I know is a Greek gentleman by the name of Mr. Georgopolis who is really quite wealthy and he is what Dorothy and I call a “Shopper” because you can always call him up at any hour and ask him to go shopping and he is always quite delighted, which very few gentlemen seem to be. And he never seems to care how much anything costs. I mean Mr. Georgopolis is also quite cultured, as I know quite a few gentlemen who can speak to a waiter in French but Mr. Georgopolis can also speak to a waiter in Greek which very few gentlemen seem to be able to do.

===Chinese===
于是格里说，我让他大大地想起了特洛伊的海伦，海伦是希腊血统。可是我认识的唯一一个希腊人，是一位姓乔治奥波利斯的希腊绅士（Mr. Georgopolis），他人真的相当有钱，多萝西和我管他叫「购物郎」，因为不管什么钟点你都可以打电话叫他陪你去买东西，他还总是相当乐意，很少有绅士做得到这一点。而且他好像从不在乎东西多少钱。我是说，乔治奥波利斯先生还相当有文化，因为我认识不少能用法语跟侍者说话的绅士，可乔治奥波利斯先生还能用希腊语跟侍者说话，很少有绅士做得到这一点。

===Original===
April 1st:
I am taking special pains with my diary from now on as I am really writing it for Gerry. I mean he and I are going to read it together some evening in front of the fireplace. But Gerry leaves this evening for Boston as he has to lecture about all of his works at Boston, but he will rush right back as soon as possible. So I am going to spend all of my time improving myself while he is gone. And this afternoon we are both going to a museum on 5th Avenue, because Gerry wants to show me a very very beautiful cup made by an antique jeweler called Mr. Cellini and he wants me to read Mr. Cellini’s life which is a very very fine book and not dull while he is in Boston.

===Chinese===
4月1日：
从今往后我要格外用心写日记了，因为我真是为格里写的。我是说，将来哪个晚上，我们要一起坐在壁炉前读它。不过格里今晚要去波士顿，因为他得去波士顿就他全部的作品作讲演，但他会尽快赶回来。所以他不在的时候，我要把全部时间都用来提升自己。今天下午我们俩要一起去第五大道的一家博物馆，因为格里要给我看一只非常非常漂亮的杯子，出自一位叫切利尼先生（Mr. Cellini）的古董珠宝匠之手，他还让我趁他在波士顿期间读一读切利尼先生的生平，那是一本非常非常好的书，一点不乏味。

===Original===
So the famous playright friend of mine who is called Sam called up this morning and he wanted me to go to a literary party tonight that he and some other literary gentlemen are giving to Florence Mills in Harlem but Gerry does not want me to go with Sam as Sam always insists on telling riskay stories. But personally I am quite broad minded and I always say that I do not mind a riskay story as long as it is really funny. I mean I have a great sense of humor. But Gerry says Sam does not always select and choose his stories and he just as soon I did not go out with him. So I am going to stay home and read the book by Mr. Cellini instead, because, after all, the only thing I am really interested in, is improving my mind. So I am going to do nothing else but improve my mind while Gerry is in Boston. I mean I just received a cable from Willie Gwynn who arrives from Europe tomorrow, but I am not even going to bother to see him. He is a sweet boy but he never gets anywhere and I am not going to waste my time on such as him, after meeting a gentleman like Gerry.

===Chinese===
于是今天早上，我那位人称山姆的鼎鼎大名的剧作家朋友打来电话，想让我今晚去参加一个文学聚会，是他和另外几位文学绅士为哈莱姆的弗洛伦斯·米尔斯（Florence Mills）办的，可是格里不想让我跟山姆去，因为山姆老爱讲荤故事。不过我个人心胸相当开阔，我一向说，只要故事真的好笑，我不介意荤故事。我是说，我的幽默感可强了。可是格里说，山姆讲故事又不怎么挑挑拣拣，他还是宁可我不跟他出去。所以我打算待在家里，改读切利尼先生的那本书，因为说到底，我唯一真正感兴趣的，就是增进我的心智。所以格里在波士顿期间，我什么也不干，光增进心智。我是说，我刚收到威利·格温（Willie Gwynn）发来的电报，他明天从欧洲到，可我连见都懒得去见他。他是个讨人喜欢的男孩，可他永远混不出个名堂，结识了格里这样的绅士之后，我不想再在他这种人身上浪费时间。

===Original===
April 2nd:
I seem to be quite depressed this morning as I always am when there is nothing to put my mind to. Because I decided not to read the book by Mr. Cellini. I mean it was quite amuseing in spots because it was really quite riskay but the spots were not so close together and I never seem to like to always be hunting clear through a book for the spots I am looking for, especially when there are really not so many spots that seem to be so amuseing after all. So I did not waste my time on it but this morning I told Lulu to let all of the house work go and spend the day reading a book entitled *Lord Jim* and then tell me all about it, so that I would improve my mind while Gerry is away. But when I got her the book I nearly made a mistake and gave her a book by the title of *The Nigger of the Narcissus* which really would have hurt her feelings. I mean I do not know why authors cannot say “Negro” instead of “Nigger” as they have their feelings just the same as we have.

===Chinese===
4月2日：
今天早上我好像相当郁闷，一没事情让脑子转的时候我总是这样。因为我决定不读切利尼先生的那本书了。我是说，那书有些地方相当逗，因为它确实相当荤，可惜逗的地方隔得太远，我从来就不喜欢为找一处有意思的地方翻遍一整本书，尤其到最后其实并没有那么多地方真的那么逗。所以我没在它上面浪费时间，不过今天早上我叫露露把家务活全放下，花一整天读一本叫《吉姆爷》的书，然后讲给我听，这样格里不在的时候我也能增进心智。可是我把书拿给她的时候差点出了岔子，差一点递给她那本《水仙号上的黑鬼》，那可真会伤了她的感情。我是说，我不明白作家们为什么非说「黑鬼」而不说「黑人」，黑人的感情跟咱们的一模一样啊。

===Original===
Well I just got a telegram from Gerry that he will not be back until tomorrow and also some orchids from Willie Gwynn, so I may as well go to the theatre with Willie tonight to keep from getting depressed, as he really is a sweet boy after all. I mean he never really does anything obnoxious. And it is quite depressing to stay at home and do nothing but read, unless you really have a book that is worth bothering about.

===Chinese===
对了，我刚收到格里发来的电报，说他要到明天才回来，还有威利·格温送来的兰花，所以今晚我不妨和威利去看戏，免得闷闷不乐，毕竟他真是个讨人喜欢的男孩。我是说，他从来不干什么讨人嫌的事。一个人待在家里什么也不干光读书，实在太郁闷了，除非手里真有一本值得费神的书。

===Original===
April 3rd:
I was really so depressed this morning that I was even glad to get a letter from Mr. Eisman. Because last night Willie Gwynn came to take me to the Follies, but he was so intoxicated that I had to telephone his club to send around a taxi to take him home. So that left me alone with Lulu at nine o’clock with nothing to do, so I put in a telephone call for Boston to talk to Gerry but it never went through. So Lulu tried to teach me how to play mah-jongg, but I really could not keep my mind on it because I was so depressed. So today I think I had better go over to Madame Frances and order some new evening gowns to cheer me up.

===Chinese===
4月3日：
今天早上我实在郁闷得厉害，连收到艾斯曼先生的信都高兴。因为昨晚威利·格温来带我去看《福利斯》，可他醉得太厉害，我只好打电话到他的俱乐部，叫他们派辆出租车送他回家。于是九点钟就剩下我和露露没事可干，我往波士顿挂了个电话想跟格里说话，可一直没接通。于是露露试着教我打麻将，可我实在没法专心，因为我太郁闷了。所以今天我想，最好还是去弗朗西斯夫人那儿订几件新的晚礼服，让自己振作一下。

===Original===
Well Lulu just brought me a telegram from Gerry that he will be in this afternoon, but I must not meet him at the station on account of all of the reporters who always meet him at the station wherever he comes from. But he says he will come right up to see me as he has something to talk about.

===Chinese===
对了，露露刚拿来格里的一份电报，说他今天下午就到，可我千万别去车站接他，因为不管他从哪儿来，车站总有那么一群记者迎接他。不过他说他会直接上来看我，因为他有话要跟我谈。

===Original===
April 4th:
What an evening we had last evening. I mean it seems that Gerry is madly in love with me. Because all of the time he was in Boston lecturing to the womens clubs he said, as he looked over the faces of all those club women in Boston, he never realized I was so beautiful. And he said that there was only one in all the world and that was me. But it seems that Gerry thinks that Mr. Eisman is terrible and that no good can come of our friendship. I mean I was quite surprised, as they both seemed to get along quite well together, but it seems that Gerry never wants me to see Mr. Eisman again. And he wants me to give up everything and study French and he will get a divorce and we will be married. Because Gerry does not seem to like the kind of life all of us lead in New York and he wants me to go home to papa in Arkansas and he will send me books to read so that I will not get lonesome there. And he gave me his uncle’s Masonic ring, which came down from the time of Soloman and which he never even lets his wife wear, for our engagement ring, and this afternoon a lady friend of his is going to bring me a new system she thought up of how to learn French. But some way I still seem to be depressed. I mean I could not sleep all night thinking of the terrible things Gerry said about New York and about Mr. Eisman. Of course I can understand Gerry being jealous of any gentleman friend of mine and of course I never really thought that Mr. Eisman was Rudolph Valentino, but Gerry said it made him cringe to think of a sweet girl like I having a friendship with Mr. Eisman. So it really made me feel quite depressed. I mean Gerry likes to talk quite a lot and I always think a lot of talk is depressing and worries your brains with things you never even think of when you are busy. But so long as Gerry does not mind me going out with other gentlemen when they have something to give you mentally, I am going to luncheon with Eddie Goldmark of the Goldmark Films who is always wanting me to sign a contract to go into the cinema. Because Mr. Goldmark is madly in love with Dorothy and Dorothy is always wanting me to go back in the cinema because Dorothy says that she will go if I will go.

===Chinese===
4月4日：
昨晚我们过的可真是个不寻常的晚上。我是说，看来格里是疯狂地爱着我。因为他在波士顿给那些妇女俱乐部讲演的整个期间说，当他一张张扫过波士顿所有那些俱乐部妇女的脸，他才意识到我有多么美。他还说，全世界只有一个这样的人，那就是我。可是格里好像认为艾斯曼先生很可怕，认为我们这段友谊不会有好结果。我是说，我相当吃惊，因为他们俩以前看起来处得相当好，可格里好像再也不想让我见艾斯曼先生了。他要我放弃一切去学法语，他会去办离婚，然后我们结婚。因为格里好像不喜欢我们大家在纽约过的这种生活，他要我回阿肯色州回爸爸身边去，他会寄书给我读，这样我在那儿就不会寂寞。他把他叔叔的共济会戒指给了我做我们的订婚戒指，那戒指是所罗门时代传下来的，他连自己的妻子都从不让她戴。今天下午他的一位女朋友会给我送来一套她想出来的学法语的新方法。可是不知怎么，我好像还是相当郁闷。我是说，我一整夜都没睡着，想着格里说的那些关于纽约和艾斯曼先生的可怕的话。我当然能理解格里嫉妒我的任何一位绅士朋友，我也从来没真觉得艾斯曼先生是鲁道夫·瓦伦蒂诺，可格里说，一想到像我这样一个甜美的姑娘跟艾斯曼先生有交情，他就浑身发麻。这真让我觉得相当郁闷。我是说，格里就是爱长篇大论，我一向觉得话太多让人郁闷，让你为一些忙起来根本不会去想的事情劳神伤脑。不过只要格里不介意我出门见别的绅士——前提是他们能在思想上给我点东西——我就要和戈德马克电影公司的埃迪·戈德马克（Eddie Goldmark）吃午饭去，他一直想让我签合同重回电影界。因为戈德马克先生疯狂地爱着多萝西，而多萝西总想让我回电影界，因为多萝西说，只要我去，她就去。

===Original===
April 6th:
Well I finally wrote Mr. Eisman that I was going to get married and it seems that he is coming on at once as he would probably like to give me his advice. Getting married is really quite serious and Gerry talks to me for hours and hours about it. I mean he never seems to get tired of talking and he does not seem to even want to go to shows or dance or do anything else but talk, and if I don’t really have something definite to put my mind on soon I will scream.

===Chinese===
4月6日：
我终于写信告诉艾斯曼先生我要结婚了，看样子他马上就要赶来，大概是想给我点忠告。结婚实在是件大事，格里跟我谈了好多钟头又好多钟头。我是说，他谈起来好像永远不累，而且他好像连看演出、跳舞还是别的什么都不想干了，光想谈，要是我再不赶紧找点实实在在的事情让脑子忙起来，我真要尖叫了。

===Original===
April 7th:
Well Mr. Eisman arrived this morning and he and I had quite a long talk, and after all I think he is right. Because here is the first real opportunity I have ever really had. I mean to go to Paris and broaden out and improve my writing, and why should I give it up to marry an author, where he is the whole thing and all I would be would be the wife of Gerald Lamson? And on top of that I would have to be dragged into the scandal of a divorce court and get my name smirched. So Mr. Eisman said that opportunities come to seldom in a girls life for me to give up the first one I have really ever had. So I am sailing for France and London on Tuesday and taking Dorothy with me and Mr. Eisman says that he will see us there later. So Dorothy knows all of the ropes and she can get along in Paris just as though she knew French and besides she knows a French gentleman who was born and raised there, who speaks it like a native and knows Paris like a book. And Dorothy says that when we get to London nearly everybody speaks English anyway. So it is quite lucky that Mr. Lamson is out lecturing in Cincinnati and he will not be back until Wednesday and I can send him a letter and tell him that I have to go to Europe now but I will see him later perhaps. So anyway I will be spared listening to any more of his depressing conversation. So Mr. Eisman gave me quite a nice string of pearls and he gave Dorothy a diamond pin and we all went to the Colony for dinner and we all went to a show and supper at the Trocadero and we all spent quite a pleasant evening.

===Chinese===
4月7日：
艾斯曼先生今天早上到了，我和他谈了相当长的一次话，说到底，我觉得他是对的。因为这是我这辈子真正得到过的第一个机会。我是说，去巴黎开拓眼界、提高我的写作，我为什么要放弃它去嫁给一个作家，让他当全部，而我只落得个当杰拉尔德·拉姆森的太太？更何况我还会被拖进离婚法庭的丑闻，落个名声带污。所以艾斯曼先生说，一个姑娘一生中机会来得太少了，我不能放弃我真正得到过的第一个。于是我星期二就启航去法国和伦敦，带上多萝西，艾斯曼先生说他晚些时候会去那儿跟我们碰头。多萝西对所有的门道都门儿清，她在巴黎照样混得开，就仿佛她会法语似的，再说她还认识一位生在巴黎长在巴黎的法国绅士，法语说得跟本地人一样，对巴黎熟得像一本书。多萝西还说，我们到了伦敦，反正那儿几乎人人都会说英语。所以真是相当走运，拉姆森先生正外出到辛辛那提讲演，星期三才回来，我可以给他写封信，告诉他我现在必须去欧洲了，不过也许以后再见。这样无论如何，我总算可以不用再听他那些让人郁闷的长谈了。于是艾斯曼先生送了我一串相当不错的珍珠，还送了多萝西一枚钻石胸针，然后我们一起去科洛尼吃晚饭，一起看了场演出，一起去特罗卡德罗吃了夜宵，大家一起过了一个相当愉快的夜晚。

## II Fate Keeps on Happening / 第二章 命运总在接连发生

===Original===
April 11th:
Well Dorothy and I are really on the ship sailing to Europe as anyone could tell by looking at the ocean. I always love the ocean. I mean I always love a ship and I really love the *Majestic* because you would not know it was a ship because it is just like being at the Ritz, and the steward says the ocean is not so obnoxious this month as it generally is. So Mr. Eisman is going to meet us next month in Paris because he has to be there on business. I mean he always says that there is really no place to see the latest styles in buttons like Paris.

So Dorothy is out taking a walk up and down the deck with a gentleman she met on the steps, but I am not going to waste my time going around with gentlemen because if I did nothing but go around I would not finish my diary or read good books which I am always reading to improve my mind. But Dorothy really does not care about her mind and I always scold her because she does nothing but waste her time by going around with gentlemen who do not have anything, when Eddie Goldmark of the Goldmark Films is really quite wealthy and can make a girl delightful presents. But she does nothing but waste her time and yesterday, which was really the day before we sailed, she would not go to luncheon with Mr. Goldmark but she went to luncheon to meet a gentleman called Mr. Mencken from Baltimore who really only prints a green magazine which has not even got any pictures in it. But Mr. Eisman is always saying that every girl does not want to get ahead and get educated like me.

So Mr. Eisman and Lulu come down to the boat to see me off and Lulu cried quite a lot. I mean I really believe she could not care any more for me if she was light and not colored. Lulu has had a very sad life because when she was quite young a pullman porter fell madly in love with her. So she believed him and he lured her away from her home to Ashtabula and deceived her there. So she finally found out that she had been deceived and she really was broken hearted and when she tried to go back home she found out that it was to late because her best girl friend, who she had always trusted, had stolen her husband and he would not take Lulu back. So I have always said to her she could always work for me and she is going to take care of the apartment until I get back, because I would not sublet the apartment because Dorothy sublet her apartment when she went to Europe last year and the gentleman who sublet the apartment allowed girls to pay calls on him who were not nice.

Mr. Eisman has litereally filled our room with flowers and the steward has had quite a hard time to find enough vases to put them into. I mean the steward said he knew as soon as he saw Dorothy and I that he would have quite a heavy run on vases. And of course Mr. Eisman has sent me quite a lot of good books as he always does, because he always knows that good books are always welcome. So he has sent me quite a large book of Etiquette as he says there is quite a lot of Etiquette in England and London and it would be a good thing for a girl to learn. So I am going to take it on the deck after luncheon and read it, because I would often like to know what a girl ought to do when a gentleman she has just met, says something to her in a taxi. Of course I always become quite vexed but I always believe in giving a gentleman another chance.

So now the steward tells me it is luncheon time, so I will go upstairs as the gentleman Dorothy met on the steps has invited us to luncheon in the Ritz, which is a special dining room on the ship where you can spend quite a lot of money because they really give away the food in the other dining room.

===Chinese===
4月11日：
哎呀，多萝西和我这回可真的坐上开往欧洲的船了，随便谁看一眼这片大洋就知道。我一向喜欢大海。我是说我一向喜欢轮船，而这艘「庄严号」（*Majestic*）我更是真喜欢，因为你简直看不出它是条船，因为待在上面就跟待在丽兹酒店一模一样，服务员还说，这个月的大洋不像平常那么讨人厌。所以艾斯曼先生（Mr. Eisman）下个月会到巴黎来跟我们碰头，因为他得上那儿办事。我是说他老说，要看最新式样的纽扣，天下再没有比巴黎更好的地方了。

所以多萝西这会儿正在甲板上，跟一位她在舷梯上认识的绅士来来回回地散步，我可不想把时间浪费在陪绅士们逛来逛去上，因为要是整天光顾着逛，我就写不完日记，也没法读那些我一直在读来增进心智的好书了。可是多萝西真的不在乎她的心智，我老是骂她，因为她净把时间浪费在那些一无所有的绅士身上，而戈德马克电影公司的埃迪·戈德马克（Eddie Goldmark）其实相当有钱，送起姑娘礼物来可讨人喜欢了。可她偏偏净浪费时间，就在昨天——其实是我们开船的前一天——她不肯跟戈德马克先生去吃午饭，倒跑去跟一位从巴尔的摩来的、叫门肯先生（Mr. Mencken）的绅士吃午饭，那位先生其实不过印一份绿颜色的杂志，里面连一张图片都没有。不过艾斯曼先生总说，并不是每个姑娘都想像我这样上进、想受教育的。

于是艾斯曼先生和露露（Lulu）到码头来送我，露露哭了老半天。我是说我真的相信，就算她生来是白人而不是黑人，待我的心也不可能更亲了。露露这辈子命很苦，因为她还年纪轻轻的时候，一个普尔曼列车员疯狂地爱上了她。于是她信了他，他把她从家里骗到阿什塔比拉，在那儿欺骗了她。后来她终于发觉自己上了当，真是伤心欲碎，等她想回家的时候，才发现已经太迟了，因为她一直最信得过的闺蜜把她的丈夫抢走了，而他又不肯收回露露。所以我一直跟她说，她尽可以一直替我做事，我回来之前她替我照看公寓，因为我不愿把公寓转租出去——多萝西去年去欧洲时把她的公寓转租了，那位转租的绅士还让一些不正经的姑娘上门来拜访他。

艾斯曼先生简直把我们的房间塞满了鲜花，服务员费了好大的劲才找够插花的花瓶。我是说服务员说，他一看见我和多萝西，就知道花瓶准要供不应求。当然啦，艾斯曼先生照例送了我好些好书，因为他一向知道好书永远是受欢迎的。所以他送了我一本挺厚的《礼仪》大全，他说英国和伦敦讲究的礼仪特别多，姑娘家学一学是件好事。所以我打算午饭后把它带到甲板上去读，因为我常常很想知道，一位姑娘刚刚认识的绅士在出租汽车里对她说了些什么的时候，她该怎么办才得体。当然我总是相当恼火，不过我一向主张给绅士再来一次机会。

这不，服务员来告诉我午饭时间到了，我要上楼去了，因为多萝西在舷梯上认识的那位绅士请我们去丽兹餐厅吃午饭，那是船上特别的餐厅，在那儿你可以花掉好大一笔钱，因为另一间餐厅里的饭菜简直就是白送的。

===Original===
April 12th:
I am going to stay in bed this morning as I am quite upset as I saw a gentleman who quite upset me. I am not really sure it was the gentleman, as I saw him at quite a distants in the bar, but if it really is the gentleman it shows that when a girl has a lot of fate in her life it is sure to keep on happening. So when I thought I saw this gentleman I was with Dorothy and Major Falcon, who is the gentleman Dorothy met on the steps, and Major Falcon noticed that I became upset, so he wanted me to tell him what was the matter, but it is really so terrible that I would not want to tell anyone. So I said good night to Major Falcon and I left him with Dorothy and I went down to our room and did nothing but cry and send the steward for some champagne to cheer me up. I mean champagne always makes me feel philosophical because it makes me realize that when a girl’s life is as full of fate as mine seems to be, there is nothing else to do about it. So this morning the steward brought me my coffee and quite a large pitcher of ice water so I will stay in bed and not have any more champagne until luncheon time.

Dorothy never has any fate in her life and she does nothing but waste her time and I really wonder if I did right to bring her with me and not Lulu. I mean she really gives gentlemen a bad impression as she talks quite a lot of slang. Because when I went up yesterday to meet she and Major Falcon for luncheon, I overheard her say to Major Falcon that she really liked to become intoxicated once in a “dirty” while. Only she did not say intoxicated, but she really said a slang word that means intoxicated and I am always having to tell her that “dirty” is a slang word and she really should not say “dirty.”

Major Falcon is really quite a delightful gentleman for an Englishman. I mean he really spends quite a lot of money and we had quite a delightful luncheon and dinner in the Ritz until I thought I saw the gentleman who upset me and I am so upset I think I will get dressed and go up on the deck and see if it really is the one I think it is. I mean there is nothing else for me to do as I have finished writing in my diary for today and I have decided not to read the book of Ettiquette as I glanced through it and it does not seem to have anything in it that I would care to know because it wastes quite a lot of time telling you what to call a Lord and all the Lords I have met have told me what to call them and it is generally some quite cute name like Coocoo whose real name is really Lord Cooksleigh. So I will not waste my time on such a book. But I wish I did not feel so upset about the gentleman I think I saw.

===Chinese===
4月12日：
今天上午我要待在床上不起来，因为我心里相当不痛快，因为我看见了一位让我相当不痛快的绅士。其实我也说不准是不是那位绅士，因为我是隔着老远的距离在酒吧里看见他的，可要是真是那位绅士，那就说明一个姑娘的一生里要是命运太多，它就准会接连不断地发生。所以当我觉得看见了这位绅士的时候，我正跟多萝西和福尔肯少校（Major Falcon）在一起——福尔肯少校就是多萝西在舷梯上认识的那位绅士——福尔肯少校看出了我心神不宁，非要我告诉他出了什么事，可这件事实在可怕透了，我谁也不想告诉。于是我向福尔肯少校道了晚安，把他留给多萝西，自己回到下面的舱房里，什么也没干，光是哭，还差服务员去拿点香槟来给我解闷。我是说香槟总能让我变得很有哲理，因为它让我明白，一个姑娘的一生要是像我这样充满了命运，那就再没有别的办法可想了。于是今天早上服务员端来了我的咖啡和满满一大壶冰水，所以我要待在床上，午饭之前再也不碰香槟了。

多萝西的生活里从来没有什么命运，她净把时间白白浪费掉，我真怀疑自己带她来、而不是带露露来，是不是带对了。我是说她真的给绅士们留下很坏的印象，因为她满嘴俚语。因为昨天我上楼去跟她和福尔肯少校一起吃午饭的时候，我无意中听见她对福尔肯少校说，她其实喜欢隔三差五「痛快」地醉上一场。只不过她说的不是「醉」这个字，她说的是一个意思等于「醉」的俚语字眼，而且我还老得跟她说「痛快」这种词是俚语，她实在不应当说「痛快」。

福尔肯少校作为一个英国人来说，真是位相当讨人喜欢的绅士。我是说他花起钱来真是相当大方，我们在丽兹餐厅吃了一顿相当愉快的午饭和晚饭，直到我觉得看见了那位让我心烦意乱的绅士。我现在心乱得很，我想我还是穿上衣服到甲板上去，看看究竟是不是我心里想的那一位。我是说我也没别的事可干了，因为今天的日记我已经写完了，而且我决定不读那本《礼仪》了，因为我翻着看了一下，里面似乎没有什么我想知道的东西，因为它浪费好多篇幅教你怎么称呼一位勋爵，而我认识的那些勋爵全都自己告诉过我该怎么称呼他们，而且通常都是些相当俏皮的名字，比如「咕咕」（Coocoo），他的真名其实是库克斯利勋爵（Lord Cooksleigh）。所以我才不在这类书上浪费时间。可我真希望自己心里不要为那位我觉得看见了的绅士这么烦乱。

===Original===
April 13th:
It really is the gentleman I thought I saw. I mean when I found out it was the gentleman my heart really stopped. Because it all brought back things that anybody does not like to remember, no matter who they are. So yesterday when I went up on the deck to see if I could see the gentleman and see if it really was him, I met quite a delightful gentleman who I met once at a party called Mr. Ginzberg. Only his name is not Mr. Ginzberg any more because a gentleman in London called Mr. Battenburg, who is some relation to some king, changed his name to Mr. Mountbatten which Mr. Ginzberg says really means the same thing after all. So Mr. Ginsberg changed his name to Mr. Mountginz which he really thinks is more aristocratic. So we walked around the deck and we met the gentleman face to face and I really saw it was him and he really saw it was me. I mean his face became so red it was almost a picture. So I was so upset I said goodbye to Mr. Mountginz and I started to rush right down to my room and cry. But when I was going down the steps, I bumped right into Major Falcon who noticed that I was upset. So Major Falcon made me go to the Ritz and have some champagne and tell him all about it.

So then I told Major Falcon about the time in Arkansas when Papa sent me to Little Rock to study how to become a stenographer. I mean Papa and I had quite a little quarrel because Papa did not like a gentleman who used to pay calls on me in the park and Papa thought it would do me good to get away for awhile. So I was in the business colledge in Little Rock for about a week when a gentleman called Mr. Jennings paid a call on the business colledge because he wanted to have a new stenographer. So he looked over all we colledge girls and he picked me out. So he told our teacher that he would help me finish my course in his office because he was only a lawyer and I really did not have to know so much. So Mr. Jennings helped me quite a lot and I stayed in his office about a year when I found out that he was not the kind of a gentleman that a young girl is safe with. I mean one evening when I went to pay a call on him at his apartment, I found a girl there who really was famous all over Little Rock for not being nice. So when I found out that girls like that paid calls on Mr. Jennings I had quite a bad case of histerics and my mind was really a blank and when I came out of it, it seems that I had a revolver in my hand and it seems that the revolver had shot Mr. Jennings.

So this gentleman on the boat was really the District Attorney who was at the trial and he really was quite harsh at the trial and he called me names that I would not even put in my diary. Because everyone at the trial except the District Attorney was really lovely to me and all the gentlemen in the jury all cried when my lawyer pointed at me and told them that they practically all had had either a mother or a sister. So the jury was only out three minutes and then they came back and acquitted me and they were all so lovely that I really had to kiss all of them and when I kissed the judge he had tears in his eyes and he took me right home to his sister. I mean it was when Mr. Jennings became shot that I got the idea to go into the cinema, so Judge Hibbard got me a ticket to Hollywood. So it was Judge Hibbard who really gave me my name because he did not like the name I had because he said a girl ought to have a name that ought to express her personality. So he said my name ought to be Lorelei which is the name of a girl who became famous for sitting on a rock in Germany, So I was in Hollywood in the cinema when I met Mr. Eisman and he said that a girl with my brains ought not to be in the cinema but she ought to be educated, so he took me out of the cinema so he could educate me.

So Major Falcon was really quite interested in everything I talked about, because he said it was quite a co-instance because this District Attorney, who is called Mr. Bartlett, is now working for the government of America and he is on his way to a place called Vienna on some business for Uncle Sam that is quite a great secret and Mr. Falcon would like very much to know what the secret is, because the Government in London sent him to America especially to find out what it was. Only of course Mr. Bartlett does not know who Major Falcon is, because it is such a great secret, but Major Falcon can tell me, because he knows who he can trust. So Major Falcon says he thinks a girl like I ought to forgive and forget what Mr. Bartlett called me and he wants to bring us together and he says he thinks Mr. Bartlett would talk to me quite a lot when he really gets to know me and I forgive him for that time in Little Rock. Because it would be quite romantic for Mr. Bartlett and I to become friendly, and gentlemen who work for Uncle Sam generally like to become romantic with girls. So he is going to bring us together on the deck after dinner tonight and I am going to forgive him and talk with him quite a lot, because why should a girl hold a grudge against a gentleman who had to do it. So Major Falcon brought me quite a large bottle of perfume and a quite cute imitation of quite a large size dog in the little shop which is on board the boat. I mean Major Falcon really knows how to cheer a girl up quite a lot and so tonight I am going to make it all up with Mr. Bartlett.

===Chinese===
4月13日：
果然是我以为看见的那位绅士。我是说等我弄清那就是那位绅士的时候，我的心真的停止了跳动。因为这一切勾起了那些不管是谁都不愿意再回想的往事。于是昨天我到甲板上去，想看看能不能见着那位绅士、看看究竟是不是他的时候，我遇到了一位相当讨人喜欢的绅士，我曾在一次聚会上见过他，叫金兹伯格先生（Mr. Ginzberg）。只不过他现在不再叫金兹伯格先生了，因为伦敦有位叫巴滕伯格先生的绅士——跟哪位国王沾点亲——把自己的姓改成了蒙巴顿，而金兹伯格先生说，这两个姓说到底意思其实一模一样。于是金兹伯格先生把自己的姓改成芒特金兹（Mr. Mountginz），他真觉得这样更有贵族气派。于是我们在甲板上散步，迎面正好撞上那位绅士，我真的看清了是他，他也真的看清了是我。我是说他的脸涨得那么红，简直成了一幅画。于是我烦乱得不得了，跟芒特金兹先生道了别，就一头往我的舱房冲，想去哭一场。可我下扶梯的时候，一头撞在福尔肯少校身上，他看出了我心神不宁。于是福尔肯少校硬拉我到丽兹餐厅喝了点香槟，把事情原原本本讲给他听。

于是我把当年在阿肯色州（Arkansas）的事讲给福尔肯少校听，那时候爸爸送我去小石城（Little Rock）学速记。我是说爸爸和我小小吵了一场，因为爸爸不喜欢一位常到公园里来拜访我的绅士，爸爸觉得我出门躲一阵子对我有好处。于是我进了小石城的商业学院大约一个星期，有位叫詹宁斯先生（Mr. Jennings）的绅士到商业学院来拜访，因为他想物色一名新速记员。于是他把我们这些学院姑娘挨个打量了一遍，把我挑了出来。于是他告诉我们的老师，他会帮我在他的事务所里读完我的课程，因为他不过是个律师，我实在用不着懂得那么多。于是詹宁斯先生帮了我很多忙，我在他的事务所待了大约一年，直到我发觉他不是那种年轻姑娘跟他待在一起能保险的绅士。我是说有天傍晚我到他的公寓去拜访他，发现那儿有个姑娘，她正是因为不正经而全小石城出了名的。于是当我发现那种姑娘也会去拜访詹宁斯先生，我犯了挺厉害的一场歇斯底里，脑子里真是一片空白，等我醒过来的时候，我手里似乎就攥着一把左轮手枪，而这把左轮手枪似乎打中了詹宁斯先生。

所以船上这位绅士其实就是当年庭审上的那位地方检察官，他在法庭上真是相当刻毒，他骂了我一些连写进日记里我都不愿意的难听话。因为庭审上除了地方检察官，人人待我都真是好极了，陪审团的绅士们全都哭了，当我的律师指着我告诉他们，他们差不多人人都有过母亲或者姐妹。于是陪审团只退席了三分钟就回来了，宣布我无罪，他们个个都那么可爱，我实在不得不把他们挨个亲了一遍，我亲法官的时候，他眼里含着泪，当场就把我带回他妹妹家里去了。我是说正是在詹宁斯先生挨枪的时候，我萌生了进电影界的念头，于是希巴德法官（Judge Hibbard）给我弄了一张去好莱坞（Hollywood）的车票。而罗蕾莱这个名字其实就是希巴德法官给起的，因为他不喜欢我原来的名字，他说一个姑娘的名字应当能表达出她的个性。所以他说我该叫罗蕾莱（Lorelei），那是个姑娘的名字，她因为坐在德国的一块岩石上出了名。于是我到了好莱坞进了电影界，在那儿认识了艾斯曼先生，他说像我这样有头脑的姑娘不该待在电影界，而应该去受教育，于是他把我从电影界带出来，好教育我。

于是福尔肯少校对我讲的这一切真是相当感兴趣，因为他说这真是相当凑巧，因为这位地方检察官——叫巴特利特先生（Mr. Bartlett）——如今在为美国政府做事，他正要去一个叫维也纳（Vienna）的地方，替山姆大叔办一件十分重大的秘密差事，而福尔肯少校很想知道那秘密是什么，因为伦敦政府派他到美国来，就是专门为了打听这个。只不过巴特利特先生当然不知道福尔肯少校是谁，因为这是件天大的秘密，但福尔肯少校可以告诉我，因为他知道他信得过谁。于是福尔肯少校说，他觉得像我这样的姑娘，应该宽恕巴特利特先生当年骂我的那些话、把它忘掉，他想安排我们俩认识认识，他说他相信等巴特利特先生真正认识了我、我也原谅了他小石城那回的事以后，他会很乐意跟我多聊聊。因为巴特利特先生和我若能化敌为友是相当浪漫的，而替山姆大叔办事的绅士一般都喜欢跟姑娘们浪漫一番。所以他打算今晚晚饭后安排我们在甲板上见面，我要去原谅他，跟他好好聊聊，因为一个姑娘何必对一位当年不过奉命行事的绅士耿耿于怀呢。于是福尔肯少校送了我挺大的一瓶香水，还从船上小店买来一只挺俏皮的大号狗的仿制品。我是说福尔肯少校真懂得怎么让姑娘开心，所以今晚我要去跟巴特利特先生言归于好。

===Original===
April 14th:
Well Mr. Bartlett and I made it all up last night and we are going to be the best of friends and talk quite a lot. So when I went down to my room quite late Major Falcon came down to see if I and Mr. Bartlett were really going to be friends because he said a girl with brains like I ought to have lots to talk about with a gentleman with brains like Mr. Bartlett who knows all of Uncle Sam’s secrets.

So I told Major Falcon how Mr. Bartlett thinks that he and I seem to be like a play, because all the time he was calling me all those names in Little Rock he really thought I was. So when he found out that I turned out not to be, he said he always thought that I only used my brains against gentlemen and really had quite a cold heart. But now he thinks I ought to write a play about how he called me all those names in Little Rock and then, after seven years, we became friendly.

So I told Major Falcon that I told Mr. Bartlett I would like to write the play but I really did not have time as it takes quite a lot of time to write my diary and read good books. So Mr. Bartlett did not know that I read books which is quite a co-instance because he reads them to. So he is going to bring me a book of philosophy this afternoon called “Smile, Smile, Smile” which all the brainy senators in Washington are reading which cheers you up quite a lot.

So I told Major Falcon that having a friendship with Mr. Barlett was really quite enervating because Mr. Bartlett does not drink anything and the less anybody says about his dancing the better. But he did ask me to dine at his table, which is not in the Ritz and I told him I could not, but Major Falcon told me I ought to, but I told Major Falcon that there was a limit to almost everything. So I am going to stay in my room until luncheon and I am going to luncheon in the Ritz with Mr. Mountginz who really knows how to treat a girl.

Dorothy is up on the deck wasting quite a lot of time with a gentleman who is only a tennis champion. So I am going to ring for the steward and have some champagne which is quite good for a person on a boat. The steward is really quite a nice boy and he has had quite a sad life and he likes to tell me all about himself. I mean it seems that he was arrested in Flatbush because he promised a gentleman that he would bring him some very very good scotch and they mistook him for a bootlegger. So it seems they put him in a prison and they put him in a cell with two other gentlemen who were very, very famous burglars. I mean they really had their pictures in all the newspapers and everybody was talking about them. So my steward, whose real name is Fred, was very very proud to be in the same cell with such famous burglars. So when they asked him what he was in for, he did not like to tell them that he was only a bootlegger, so he told them that he set fire to a house and burned up quite a large family in Oklahoma. So everything would have gone alright except that the police had put a dictaphone in the cell and used it all against him and he could not get out until they had investigated all the fires in Oklahoma. So I always think that it is much more educational to talk to a boy like Fred who has been through a lot and really suffered than it is to talk to a gentleman like Mr. Bartlett. But I will have to talk to Mr. Bartlett all afternoon as Major Falcon has made an appointment for me to spend the whole afternoon with him.

===Chinese===
4月14日：
哎呀，巴特利特先生和我昨晚全都言归于好了，我们要做最好的朋友，好好聊个痛快。于是我挺晚才回舱房，福尔肯少校下来看看我和巴特利特先生是不是真要做成朋友，因为他说，像我这样有头脑的姑娘，跟巴特利特先生这样有头脑、又知道山姆大叔全部秘密的绅士，一定有许多话好谈。

于是我告诉福尔肯少校，巴特利特先生觉得他和我简直像一出戏，因为当年他在小石城骂我那些难听话的时候，他心里真当我是那样的人。后来等他发现我根本不是那样，他说他从前一直以为我只是拿头脑去对付绅士们，心肠其实相当冷。可是现在他觉得，我应当写一出戏，写他如何在小石城骂了我那些难听话，然后过了七年，我们又成了朋友。

于是我告诉福尔肯少校，我对巴特利特先生说了，我很愿意写这出戏，只是实在没有工夫，因为写日记、读好书就够占工夫的了。巴特利特先生原先不知道我还读书，这真是相当凑巧，因为他自己也读书。所以今天下午他要给我带来一本哲学书，叫《笑吧，笑吧，笑吧》，华盛顿那些有头脑的参议员们人人在读，读了让人精神相当振奋。

于是我告诉福尔肯少校，跟巴特利特先生交朋友实在相当累人，因为巴特利特先生滴酒不沾，至于他的舞跳得怎么样，那就更是不提为妙。不过他倒是请我到他的餐桌上吃饭，那可不在丽兹餐厅，我告诉他我去不了，可是福尔肯少校劝我应该去，我告诉福尔肯少校，天下几乎什么事都有个限度。所以我打算在舱房里一直待到午饭时候，然后跟芒特金兹先生去丽兹吃午饭，他才真懂得怎么款待一位姑娘。

多萝西这会儿正在甲板上，跟一位不过是个网球冠军的绅士大把大把地浪费时间。所以我要按铃叫服务员，来点香槟，坐船的人喝点香槟相当有好处。这位服务员真是个挺好的小伙子，他身世相当可怜，他喜欢把他的事一股脑儿讲给我听。我是说事情好像是这样的，他曾在弗拉特布什被捕，因为他答应过一位绅士要给他弄些非常非常好的威士忌，人家就把他错当成了私酒贩子。于是他们把他关进监狱，把他跟另外两位绅士关在同一间牢房，那两位是非常非常有名的窃贼。我是说他们的照片真的上了所有报纸，人人都在议论他们。于是我的服务员——他的真名叫弗雷德——能跟这样有名的窃贼关在同一间牢房，觉得非常非常得意。所以当他们问他犯了什么事进来的，他不好意思说自己不过是个私酒贩子，就告诉他们说他放火烧了一所房子，还在俄克拉荷马烧死了好大一家人。本来一切都会顺顺当当，只可惜警方在牢房里装了窃听器，把这话全拿来对付他，他们把俄克拉荷马的火灾统统查了个遍，他才放出来。所以我总认为，跟弗雷德这样阅历丰富、真正吃过苦头的小伙子说话，比跟巴特利特先生那样的绅士说话更有教育意义。可是今天整个下午我都得陪着巴特利特先生说话了，因为福尔肯少校替我约好了，要我把一整个下午都花在他身上。

===Original===
April 15th:
Last night there was quite a maskerade ball on the ship which was really all for the sake of charity because most of the sailors seem to have orphans which they get from going on the ocean when the sea is very rough. So they took up quite a collection and Mr. Bartlett made quite a long speech in favor of orphans especially when their parents are sailors. Mr. Bartlett really likes to make speeches quite a lot. I mean he even likes to make speeches when he is all alone with a girl when they are walking up and down a deck. But the maskerade ball was quite cute and one gentleman really looked almost like an imitation of Mr. Chaplin. So Dorothy and I really did not want to go to the ball but Mr. Bartlett bought us two scarfs at the little store which is on the ship so we tied them around our hips and everyone said we made quite a cute Carmen. So Mr. Bartlett and Major Falcon and the tennis champion were the Judges. So Dorothy and I won the prizes. I mean I really hope I do not get any more large size imitations of a dog as I have three now and I do not see why the Captain does not ask Mr. Cartier to have a jewelry store on the ship as it is really not much fun to go shopping on a ship with gentlemen, and buy nothing but imitations of dogs.

So after we won the prizes I had an engagement to go up on the top of the deck with Mr. Bartlett as it seems he likes to look at the moonlight quite a lot. So I told him to go up and wait for me and I would be up later as I promised a dance to Mr. Mountginz. So he asked me how long I would be dancing till, but I told him to wait up there and he would find out. So Mr. Mountginz and I had quite a delightful dance and champagne until Major Falcon found us. Because he was looking for me and he said I really should not keep Mr. Bartlett waiting. So I went up on the deck and Mr. Bartlett was up there waiting for me and it seems that he really is madly in love with me because he did not sleep a wink since we became friendly. Because he never thought that I really had brains but now that he knows it, it seems that he has been looking for a girl like me for years, and he said that really the place for me when he got back home was Washington d. c. where he lives. So I told him I thought a thing like that was nearly always the result of fate. So he wanted me to get off the ship tomorrow at France and take the same trip that he is taking to Vienna as it seems that Vienna is in France and if you go on to England you go to far. But I told him that I could not because I thought that if he was really madly in love with me he would take a trip to London instead. But he told me that he had serious business in Vienna that was a very, very great secret. But I told him I did not believe it was business but that it really was some girl, because what business could be so important? So he said it was business for the United States government at Washington and he could not tell anybody what it was. So then we looked at the moonlight quite a lot. So I told him I would go to Vienna if I really knew it was business and not some girl, because I could not see how business could be so important. So then he told me all about it. So it seems that Uncle Sam wants some new aeroplanes that everybody else seems to want, especially England, and Uncle Sam has quite a clever way to get them which is to long to put in my diary. So we sat up and saw the sun rise and I became quite stiff and told him I would have to go down to my room because, after all, the ship lands at France today and I said if I got off the boat at France to go to Vienna with him I would have to pack up.

So I went down to my room and went to bed. So then Dorothy came in and she was up on the deck with the tennis champion but she did not notice the sun rise as she really does not love nature but always wastes her time and ruins her clothes even though I always tell her not to drink champagne out of a bottle on the deck of the ship as it lurches quite a lot. So I am going to have luncheon in my room and I will send a note to Mr. Bartlett to tell him I will not be able to get off the boat at France to go to Vienna with him as I have quite a headache, but I will see him sometime somewhere else. So Major Falcon is going to come down at 12 and I have got to thinking over what Mr. Bartlett called me at Little Rock and I am quite upset. I mean a gentleman never pays for those things but a girl always pays. So I think I will tell Major Falcon all about the airoplane business as he really wants to know. And, after all I do not think Mr. Bartlett is a gentleman to call me all those names in Little Rock even if it was seven years ago. I mean Major Falcon is always a gentleman and he really wants to do quite a lot for us in London. Because he knows the Prince of Wales and he thinks that Dorothy and I would like the Prince of Wales once we had really got to meet him. So I am going to stay in my room until Mr. Bartlett gets off the ship at France, because I really do not seem to care if I never see Mr. Bartlett again.

So tomorrow we will be at England bright and early. And I really feel quite thrilled because Mr. Eisman sent me a cable this morning, as he does every morning, and he says to take advantage of everybody we meet as traveling is the highest form of education. I mean Mr. Eisman is always right and Major Falcon knows all the sights in London including the Prince of Wales so it really looks like Dorothy and I would have quite a delightful time in London.

===Chinese===
4月15日：
昨晚船上举行了一场相当热闹的化装舞会，其实全是为了慈善，因为大部分水手好像都有孤儿，都是海上风浪太大的时候出海得来的。于是大家募到了一大笔款子，巴特利特先生为孤儿们发表了一篇相当长的演说，尤其替父母是水手的孤儿说话。巴特利特先生真是相当喜欢发表演说。我是说哪怕单独跟一位姑娘在一起、在甲板上走来走去的时候，他都喜欢发表演说。不过化装舞会倒是相当俏皮，有位绅士打扮得简直就像卓别林先生（Mr. Chaplin）的仿制品。我和多萝西本来实在不想去参加舞会，可巴特利特先生在船上的小店里给我们俩买了两条披巾，我们把它们系在胯上，人人都说我们扮成了一对相当俏皮的卡门。于是巴特利特先生、福尔肯少校和网球冠军当了评委。于是我和多萝西得了奖。我是说我真希望自己别再收到大号的狗仿制品了，我已经有三只了，我真不明白船长为什么不请卡地亚先生到船上来开一家珠宝店，因为在船上跟绅士们逛商店、结果净买些狗的仿制品，实在没什么意思。

于是得了奖以后，我还有个约会，要跟巴特利特先生到甲板顶上去，因为他好像特别喜欢看月光。于是我叫他先上去等我，我晚一点再上去，因为我答应过芒特金兹先生一支舞。他问我跳舞要跳到什么时候，我叫他在上头等着，到时候自然就知道。于是芒特金兹先生和我跳了相当愉快的一支舞，还喝了香槟，直到福尔肯少校找到我们。因为他正在找我，他说我实在不该让巴特利特先生久等。于是我到甲板上去，巴特利特先生果然在上头等我，看来他真的是疯狂地爱着我，因为自从我们成了朋友，他连一眼都没合过。因为他从前从没想到我真的有头脑，如今既然知道了，看来他找一个像我这样的姑娘已经找了好多年了，还说等我回美国以后，我该待的地方是华盛顿特区，他住在那儿。于是我告诉他，我想这种事差不多永远是命运的结果。于是他要我明天在法国下船，跟他走同一条路线去维也纳，因为照他说维也纳就在法国，要是再往前去英国就走得过头了。可我告诉他我不行，我想，要是他真疯狂地爱着我，就该改去伦敦走一趟。可他告诉我，他在维也纳有正经公务，是件非常非常重大的秘密。可我告诉他我不信是公务，其实准是哪个姑娘，因为什么公务能重要到这个地步？于是他说那是替华盛顿的美国政府办的差事，他谁也不能告诉。于是我们看了好久好久的月光。于是我告诉他，只要让我确知那是公务、不是哪个姑娘，我就去维也纳，因为我实在不明白公务怎么可能重要到这个地步。于是他把事情原原本本全都告诉了我。原来山姆大叔想要一些新式飞机，别人好像也都想要，尤其是英国，而山姆大叔有个相当聪明的法子能弄到手，说来话长，写进我的日记里太长了。于是我们一直坐着看日出，我浑身都坐僵了，对他说我得回舱房去了，因为毕竟船今天就要到法国了，我说要是我要在法国下船跟他去维也纳，总得收拾行李呀。

于是我回舱房睡觉去了。后来多萝西进来了，她刚在甲板上跟网球冠军待着，可她压根儿没看到日出，因为她实在不爱大自然，老是把时间白白糟蹋，还糟蹋她的衣裳，尽管我总劝她别在晃得厉害的船甲板上对着瓶子喝香槟。所以我打算在房间里吃午饭，我要给巴特利特先生送张条子，告诉他我头疼得厉害，没法在法国下船跟他去维也纳了，不过日后在别的地方再见吧。福尔肯少校 12 点要来，我一直在琢磨巴特利特先生当年在小石城骂我的那些话，心里相当不痛快。我是说那种事绅士从来不用付出代价，付出代价的永远是姑娘。所以我想，我要把飞机的事一五一十告诉福尔肯少校，因为他真的很想知道。再说，我到底不认为巴特利特先生算个绅士，哪怕那是七年前的事，他也不该在小石城那样骂我。我是说福尔肯少校永远是个绅士，而且他真的想在伦敦为我们好好出力。因为他认识威尔士亲王，他觉得我和多萝西只要真见到了威尔士亲王，准会喜欢他的。所以我打算待在房间里，等巴特利特先生在法国下了船再说，因为我好像真的不在乎这辈子还见不见得到巴特利特先生了。

于是明天一早我们就到英国了。我真是觉得相当兴奋，因为艾斯曼先生今天早上又给我发来了电报——他每天早上都发——他说要好好利用我们遇到的每一个人，因为旅行是最高形式的教育。我是说艾斯曼先生永远是对的，而福尔肯少校熟悉伦敦的所有名胜，连威尔士亲王也算在内，所以看来我和多萝西在伦敦准会过得相当愉快。

## III London Is Really Nothing / 三 伦敦实在什么都不是

===Original===
April 17th:
Well, Dorothy and I are really at London. I mean we got to London on the train yesterday as the boat does not come clear up to London but it stops on the beach and you have to take a train. I mean everything is much better in New York, because the boat comes right up to New York and I am really beginning to think that London is not so educational after all. But I did not tell Mr. Eisman when I cabled him last night because Mr. Eisman really sent me to London to get educated and I would hate to tell him that London is a failure because we know more in New York.
So Dorothy and I came to the Ritz and it is delightfully full of Americans. I mean you would really think it was New York because I always think that the most delightful thing about traveling is to always be running into Americans and to always feel at home.
So yesterday Dorothy and I went down to luncheon at the Ritz and we saw a quite cute little blond girl at the next table and I nudged Dorothy under the table, because I do not think it is nice to nudge a person on top of the table as I am trying to teach good manners to Dorothy. So I said “That is quite a cute little girl so she must be an American girl.” And sure enough she called the headwaiter with quite an American accent and she was quite angry and she said to him, I have been coming to this hotel for 35 years and this is the first time I have been kept waiting. So I recognized her voice because it was really Fanny Ward. So we asked her to come over to our table and we were all three delighted to see each other. Because I and Fanny have known each other for about five years but I really feel as if I knew her better because mama knew her 45 years ago when she and mama used to go to school together and mama used to always follow all her weddings in all the newspapers. So now Fanny lives in London and is famous for being one of the cutest girls in London. I mean Fanny is almost historical, because when a girl is cute for 50 years it really begins to get historical.
So if mama did not die of hardening of the arterys she and Fanny and I could have quite a delightful time in London as Fanny loves to shop. So we went shopping for hats and instead of going to the regular shop we went to the childrens department and Fanny and I bought some quite cute hats as childrens hats only cost half as much and Fanny does it all the time. I mean Fanny really loves hats and she buys some in the children’s department every week, so she really saves quite a lot of money.
So we came back to the Ritz to meet Major Falcon because Major Falcon invited us to go to tea with him at a girls house called Lady Shelton. So Major Falcon invited Fanny to go with us to, but she was sorry because she had to go to her music lesson.

===Chinese===
4月17日：
哎，多萝西（Dorothy）和我真的到伦敦了。我是说，我们是昨天坐火车到的伦敦，因为轮船不能一直开到伦敦，它停在海滩边上，你还得再坐一趟火车。我是说，什么都还是纽约好得多，因为轮船是一直开到纽约门口的，所以我真的开始觉得，伦敦毕竟没那么长学问。不过我昨晚给艾斯曼先生拍电报的时候没这么说，因为艾斯曼先生（Mr. Eisman）送我来伦敦就是为了让我受教育的，我可不乐意告诉他伦敦是个失败，因为我们在纽约懂得更多。
于是多萝西和我住进了丽兹酒店，里面满满当当都是美国人，真叫人愉快。我是说，你真会以为这儿就是纽约，因为我总觉得旅行里最愉快的事，就是到处都能碰上美国人，到处都像在自己家一样。
于是昨天多萝西和我在丽兹酒店下楼吃午饭，看见邻桌坐着一位相当俏丽的小个子金发姑娘，我就在桌子底下用胳膊肘碰了碰多萝西，因为我觉得在桌面之上碰人不体面——我正教多萝西学礼貌呢。于是我说：“那位小姑娘真俏丽，那她准是个美国姑娘。”果不其然，她带着十足的美国口音把领班唤了过去，还挺生气的，她对他说：我来这家旅馆 35 年了，头一回让人等这么久。我一听声音就认出来了，那真是范妮·沃德（Fanny Ward）。于是我们请她到我们桌上来，我们三个见了彼此都高兴极了。因为我和范妮相识大概有五年了，可我真心觉得我跟她熟得很，因为妈妈 45 年前就认识她了，那时候她和妈妈一起上学，妈妈还总是在各家报纸上追着她一场一场的婚礼看。如今范妮住在伦敦，出名就出在她是伦敦最俏丽的姑娘之一。我是说，范妮都快算历史人物了，因为一个姑娘俏丽了 50 年，那可真就要俏成历史了。
唉，要是妈妈没有死于动脉硬化，她和范妮还有我准能在伦敦痛痛快快玩一场，因为范妮最爱买东西。于是我们去逛帽子，我们没去正经铺子，径直去了童装部，范妮和我买了好几顶怪俏皮的帽子，因为儿童帽只卖大人帽子一半的价钱，范妮一直这么干。我是说，范妮真心爱帽子，每礼拜都要去童装部买几顶，省下的钱可真不少。
于是我们回丽兹酒店去见福尔肯少校，因为福尔肯少校（Major Falcon）请我们去一位姑娘家喝茶，那位姑娘叫谢尔顿夫人（Lady Shelton）。福尔肯少校也请了范妮一同去，可惜她去不成，因为她要上音乐课。

===Original===
So at Lady Sheltons house we met quite a few people who seemed to be English. I mean some of the girls in London seem to be Ladies which seems to be the opposite of a Lord. And some who are not Ladies are honorable. But quite a few are not Ladies or honorable either, but are just like us, so all you have to call them is “Miss.” So Lady Shelton was really delighted to have we Americans come to her house. I mean she took Dorothy and I into the back parlor and tried to sell us some shell flowers she seems to make out of sea shells for 25 pounds. So we asked her how much it was in money and it seems it is 125 dollars. I mean I am really going to have a quite hard time in London with Dorothy because she really should not say to an English lady what she said. I mean she should not say to an English lady that in America we use shells the same way only we put a dry pea under one of them and we call it a game. But I told Lady Shelton we really did not need any shell flowers. So Lady Shelton said she knew we Americans loved dogs so she would love us to meet her mother.
So then she took Dorothy and Major Falcon and I to her mother’s house which was just around the corner from her house. Because her mother seems to be called a Countess and raise dogs. So her mother was having a party too, and she seemed to have quite red hair and quite a lot of paint for such an elderly lady. So the first thing she asked us was she asked us if we bought some shell flowers from her daughter. So we told her no. But she did not seem to act like a Countess of her elderly age should act. Because she said, “You were right my dears﻿—don’t let my daughter stick you﻿—they fall apart in less than a week.” So then she asked us if we would like to buy a dog. I mean I could not stop Dorothy but she said “How long before the dogs fall apart?” But I do not think the Countess acted like a Countess ought to act because she laughed very, very loud and she said that Dorothy was really priceless and she grabbed Dorothy and kissed her and held her arm around her all the time. I mean I really think that a Countess should not encouradge Dorothy or else she is just as unrefined as Dorothy seems to be. But I told the Countess that we did not need any dog.

===Chinese===
于是，在谢尔顿夫人家里，我们见到了好些看上去是英国人的人。我是说，伦敦有些姑娘似乎是「Lady」（夫人），看样子正好是 Lord（勋爵）的反义词。有些当不上 Lady 的，则算「Honourable」（尊贵）。可也有好些既不是 Lady 也不是 Honourable 的，那就跟我们一样，只消称一声「小姐」就行。谢尔顿夫人见我们美国人到她家里来，真是高兴得很。我是说，她把多萝西和我领进后客厅，想卖给我们一些她大概是拿海贝壳做的贝壳花，要价 25 英镑。我们问她合多少钱，一算竟是 125 美元。我是说，我带着多萝西在伦敦怕是要大吃苦头，因为她方才对一位英国夫人说的那些话实在不该说。我是说，她不该对一位英国夫人说，在美国我们也这么使贝壳，只不过在其中一只底下藏颗干豌豆，管它叫游戏。可我告诉谢尔顿夫人，我们真的不需要什么贝壳花。谢尔顿夫人便说，她知道我们美国人爱狗，所以很想让我们见见她的母亲。
于是她带着多萝西、福尔肯少校和我去了她母亲家，就在她家拐角过去一点。因为她母亲似乎被人称作伯爵夫人，养狗。她母亲那天也在开茶会，就那么大年纪的太太而言，她的头发红得可以，脂粉也搽得可以。她开口第一件事，就是问我们有没有买她女儿的贝壳花。我们说没有。可她的做派实在不像她那把年纪的伯爵夫人该有的做派。因为她说：“你们做得对，亲爱的们——别让我女儿宰你们——那玩意儿不出一个礼拜就散架了。”接着她问我们想不想买条狗。我是说，我拦都拦不住多萝西，她脱口就问：“这狗要多久散架？”可我觉得伯爵夫人实在不像伯爵夫人该有的样子，因为她放声大笑，笑得非常非常响，还说多萝西真是妙极了，一把搂过多萝西亲她，胳膊还一直搂着她不放。我是说，我真觉得一个伯爵夫人不该这么纵着多萝西，要不然她就跟多萝西一样没教养。可我告诉伯爵夫人，我们不需要狗。

===Original===
So then I met quite a delightful English lady who had a very, very beautiful diamond tiara in her hand bag because she said that she thought some Americans would be at the party and it was really a very, very great bargain. I mean I think a diamond tiara is delightful because it is a place where I really never thought of wearing diamonds before, and I thought I had almost one of everything until I saw a diamond tiara. The English lady who is called Mrs. Weeks said it was in her family for years but the good thing about diamonds is they always look new. So I was really very intreeged and I asked her how much it cost in money and it seems it was $7,500.
So then I looked around the room and I noticed a gentleman who seemed to be quite well groomed. So I asked Major Falcon who he was and he said he was called Sir Francis Beekman and it seems he is very, very wealthy. So then I asked Major Falcon to give us an introduction to one another and we met one another and I asked Sir Francis Beekman if he would hold my hat while I could try on the diamond tiara because I could wear it backwards with a ribbon, on account of my hair being hobbed, and I told Sir Francis Beekman that I really thought it looked quite cute. So he thought it did to, but he seemed to have another engagement. So the Countess came up to me and she is really very unrefined because she said to me “Do not waste your time on him” because she said that whenever Sir Francis Beekman spent a haypenny the statue of a gentleman called Mr. Nelson took off his hat and bowed. I mean some people are so unrefined they seem to have unrefined thoughts about everything.
So I really have my heart set on the diamond tiara and I became quite worried because Mrs. Weeks said she was going to a delightful party last night that would be full of delightful Americans and it would be snaped up. So I was so worried that I gave her 100 dollars and she is going to hold the diamond tiara for me. Because what is the use of traveling if you do not take advantadge of oportunities and it really is quite unusual to get a bargain from an English lady. So last night I cabled Mr. Eisman and I told Mr. Eisman that he does not seem to how know much it costs to get educated by traveling and I said I really would have to have $10,000 and I said I hoped I would not have to borrow the money from some strange English gentleman, even if he might be very very good looking. So I really could not sleep all night because of all of my worrying because if I do not get the money to buy the diamond tiara it may be a quite hard thing to get back $100 from an English lady.
So now I must really get dressed as Major Falcon is going to take Dorothy and I to look at all the sights in London. But I really think if I do not get the diamond tiara my whole trip to London will be quite a failure.

===Chinese===
于是接着我遇见了一位非常讨人喜欢的英国太太，她手提包里藏着一顶非常非常漂亮的钻石头冠，因为她说她料到茶会上会有些美国人，这实在是桩非常非常划算的买卖。我是说，我觉得钻石头冠妙极了，因为戴钻石的这个部位，我以前真从没想到过；我还以为天下的钻石首饰我差不多每样都有一件了，直到我见了钻石头冠。这位太太叫威克斯夫人（Mrs. Weeks），说这头冠在她家传了好些年，不过钻石的好处就是永远看着像新的。于是我真是非常非常心动，问她合多少钱，一算竟是 7,500 美元。
于是我在屋里四下一看，注意到一位先生，瞧着打扮得相当讲究。我问福尔肯少校他是谁，他说他叫弗朗西斯·比克曼爵士（Sir Francis Beekman），而且据说非常非常有钱。于是我便请福尔肯少校给我们互相引见，我们就算认识了，我请弗朗西斯·比克曼爵士替我拿着帽子，好让我试试那顶钻石头冠，因为我可以系条缎带把它倒着戴——我的头发剪成了波波头——我还对弗朗西斯·比克曼爵士说，我觉得这样戴真俏皮。他也觉得俏皮，只是他好像另外还有约。这时伯爵夫人朝我走过来，她真是非常没教养，因为她对我说：“别在他身上浪费时间”，因为她说，弗朗西斯·比克曼爵士但凡花掉半个便士，一位叫纳尔逊先生的绅士的雕像就会脱帽鞠躬。我是说，有些人就是没教养到家了，看什么都往没教养处想。
于是我真是铁了心要那顶钻石头冠，心里着急得不行，因为威克斯夫人说她昨晚要去一个茶会，满场都是讨人喜欢的美国人，头冠一准儿被人抢走。我急得没法，先给了她 100 美元，她答应替我把钻石头冠留着。因为出门旅行要是抓不住机会，那还旅行个什么劲儿，何况能从一位英国太太手里捡着便宜，实在难得。于是昨晚我给艾斯曼先生拍了电报，我对艾斯曼先生说，他似乎根本「不道知」旅行受教育要花多少钱，我实在得有 10,000 美元才够，我还说，我希望不至于向哪位素不相识的英国绅士去借这笔钱，哪怕他长得非常非常俊也不成。这一夜我担心得简直没合眼，因为万一我凑不够买钻石头冠的钱，想从一位英国太太手里讨回那 100 美元，恐怕是件相当难的事。
好了，这会儿我真得去换衣服了，因为福尔肯少校要带多萝西和我去逛遍伦敦的名胜。可我真心觉得，要是弄不到那顶钻石头冠，我这趟伦敦之行就全砸了。

===Original===
April 18th:
Yesterday was quite a day and night. I mean Major Falcon came to take Dorothy and I to see all the sights in London. So I thought it would be delightful if we had another gentleman and I made Major Falcon call up Sir Francis Beekman. I mean I had a cable from Mr. Eisman which told me he could not send me 10,000 dollars but he would send me 1,000 dollars which really would not be a drop in the bucket for the diamond tiara. So Sir Francis Beekman said that he could not come but I teased him and teased him over the telephone so he finally said he would come.
So Major Falcon drives his own car so Dorothy sat with him and I sat with Sir Francis Beekman but I told him that I was not going to call him Sir Francis Beekman but I was really going to call him Piggie.
In London they make a very, very great fuss over nothing at all. I mean London is really nothing at all. For instants, they make a great fuss over a tower that really is not even as tall as the Hickox building in Little Rock Arkansas and it would only make a chimney on one of our towers in New York. So Sir Francis Beekman wanted us to get out and look at the tower because he said that quite a famous Queen had her head cut off there one morning and Dorothy said “What a fool she was to get up that morning” and that is really the only sensible thing that Dorothy has said in London. So we did not bother to get out.
So we did not go to any more sights because they really have delicious champagne cocktails at a very very smart new restaurant called the Café de Paris that you could not get in New York for neither love or money and I told Piggie that when you are travelling you really ought to take advantadges of what you can not do at home.
So while Dorothy and I were in the Café de Paris powdering our nose in the lady’s dressing room we met an American girl who Dorothy knew in the Follies, but now she is living in London. So she told us all about London. So it seems the gentlemen in London have quite a quaint custom of not giving a girl many presents. I mean the English girls really seem to be satisfied with a gold cigaret holder or else what they call a “bangle” which means a bracelet in English which is only gold and does not have any stones in it which American girls would really give to their maid. So she said you could tell what English gentlemen were like when you realize that not even English ladys could get anything out of them. So she said Sir Francis Beekman was really famous all over London for not spending so much money as most English gentlemen. So then Dorothy and I said goodbye to Dorothy’s girl friend and Dorothy said, “Lets tell our two boy friends that we have a headache and go back to the Ritz, where men are Americans.” Because Dorothy said that the society of a gentleman like Sir Francis Beekman was to great a price to pay for a couple of rounds of champagne cocktails. But I told Dorothy that I always believe that there is nothing like trying and I think it would be nice for an American girl like I to educate an English gentleman like Piggie, as I call Sir Francis Beekman.
So then we went back to the table and I almost have to admit that Dorothy is in the right about Piggie because he really likes to talk quite a lot and he is always talking about a friend of his who was quite a famous King in London called King Edward. So Piggie said he would never never forget the jokes King Edward was always saying and he would never forget one time they were all on a yacht and they were all sitting at a table and King Edward got up and said “I don’t care what you gentlemen do﻿—I’m going to smoke a cigar.” So then Piggie laughed very, very loud. So of course I laughed very, very loud and I told Piggie he was wonderful the way he could tell jokes. I mean you can always tell when to laugh because Piggie always laughs first.

===Chinese===
4月18日：
昨天可真是热闹的一天一夜。我是说，福尔肯少校来带多萝西和我去看伦敦的名胜。我想，要是再添一位绅士就更有意思了，便让福尔肯少校打电话给弗朗西斯·比克曼爵士。我是说，艾斯曼先生来了电报，说他没法给我寄 10,000 美元，只能寄 1,000 美元，这点钱对钻石头冠来说实在连杯水车薪都算不上。弗朗西斯·比克曼爵士先是说来不了，可我在电话里磨了又磨、磨了又磨，他终于说来。
福尔肯少校自己开车，于是多萝西跟他坐，我跟弗朗西斯·比克曼爵士坐。不过我告诉他，我不打算叫他弗朗西斯·比克曼爵士，我真心打算叫他「小猪猪」（Piggie）。
伦敦人对什么都不存在的玩意儿大惊小怪个没完。我是说，伦敦实在什么都不是。比如说，他们对一座塔大惊小怪，那塔还真不如阿肯色州小石城的希科克斯大楼高，搁在纽约我们的大楼顶上，也就配当个烟囱。弗朗西斯·比克曼爵士要我们下车看看那座塔，因为他说有位相当有名的女王某天早晨就在那儿被砍了头，多萝西说：“她可真傻，那天早晨干嘛还要起床。”这实在是多萝西在伦敦说的唯一一句明白话。所以我们也就懒得下车了。
后来我们一处名胜也没再去看，因为一家叫巴黎咖啡馆（Café de Paris）的时髦新餐厅里，香槟鸡尾酒实在好喝得很，在纽约你爱也爱不来、钱也买不着。我对小猪猪说，出门在外，真该好好享受一番在家享受不到的东西。
于是，趁多萝西和我在巴黎咖啡馆的女士化妆间里补粉，我们碰上了一位美国姑娘，是多萝西在《福利斯》歌舞团（the Follies）时的旧识，如今住在伦敦。于是她把伦敦的事给我们讲了个遍。原来伦敦的绅士们有个相当古怪的风俗：不大送姑娘礼物。我是说，英国姑娘们好像真的满足于一支金烟嘴，要不然就是他们所谓的「bangle」（手镯）——照英国话讲就是镯子，光是金的，一颗宝石都不镶，美国姑娘顶多把它赏给自家的女仆。她说，你只要想想连英国太太们都从他们手里掏不出什么东西来，就知道英国绅士是什么样子了。她还说，弗朗西斯·比克曼爵士在全伦敦出名，就出在花钱没有大多数英国绅士那么多。于是多萝西和我同那位姑娘道了别，多萝西说：“咱们跟这两位男朋友说咱们头疼，回丽兹去吧，那儿的男人才是美国人。”因为多萝西说，陪弗朗西斯·比克曼爵士这种绅士社交，几轮香槟鸡尾酒的代价未免太大了。可我对多萝西说，我一向信奉凡事都要试试，而且我想，像我这样的美国姑娘，去教育教育小猪猪这样的英国绅士——我就是这么叫弗朗西斯·比克曼爵士的——倒是件美事。
于是我们回到桌边，我几乎不得不承认，多萝西说小猪猪说得没错，因为他实在爱讲话，张口闭口都是他的一位朋友，说是伦敦相当有名的一位国王，叫爱德华国王。小猪猪说他永远永远忘不了爱德华国王常讲的那些笑话，还说他永远忘不了有一回他们都在一条游艇上，大家围桌坐着，爱德华国王站起来说：“我才不管你们诸位绅士干什么——我要抽支雪茄。”说完小猪猪放声大笑，笑得非常非常响。我当然也放声大笑，笑得非常非常响，还夸小猪猪讲笑话的本事真了不起。我是说，你什么时候该笑总归有数，因为小猪猪总是先笑。

===Original===
So in the afternoon a lot of lady friends of Mrs. Weeks heard about me buying the diamond tiara and called us up and asked us to their house to tea so Dorothy and I went and we took a gentleman Dorothy met in the lobby who is very, very good looking but he is only an English ballroom dancer in a café when he has a job.
So we went to tea to a lady’s house called Lady Elmsworth and what she has to sell we Americans seems to be a picture of her father painted in oil paint who she said was a whistler. But I told her my own father was a whistler and used to whistle all of the time and I did not even have a picture of him but every time he used to go to Little Rock I asked him to go to the photographers but he did not go.
So then we met a lady called Lady Chizzleby that wanted us to go to her house to tea but we told her that we really did not want to buy anything. But she said that she did not have anything to sell but she wanted to borrow five pounds. So we did not go and I am really glad that Mr. Eisman did not come to London as all the English ladys would ask him to tea and he would have a whole ship load of shell flowers and dogs and anteek pictures that do nobody any good.
So last night Piggie and I and Dorothy and the dancer who is called Gerald went to the Kit Kat Club as Gerald had nothing better to do because he is out of a job. So Dorothy and I had quite a little quarrel because I told Dorothy that she was wasting quite a lot of time going with any gentleman who is out of a job but Dorothy is always getting to really like somebody and she will never learn how to act. I mean I always seem to think that when a girl really enjoys being with a gentleman, it puts her to quite a disadvantage and no real good can come of it.
Well tonight is going to be quite a night because Major Falcon is going to take Dorothy and I to a dance at a lady’s house tonight to meet the Prince of Wales. And now I must get ready to see Piggie because he and I seem to be getting to be quite good friends even if he has not sent me any flowers yet.

===Chinese===
下午，威克斯夫人的一大批太太朋友听说我买了钻石头冠，纷纷打电话请我们去她们家喝茶，多萝西和我便去了，还带上了一位多萝西在大堂里结识的先生，他长得非常非常俊，可惜只是个英国人，有活儿干的时候，在咖啡馆里当交谊舞师。
我们去了一位叫埃尔斯沃思夫人（Lady Elmsworth）的太太家喝茶，她要卖给我们美国人的，似乎是她父亲一幅油画像，她说她的父亲是位「惠斯勒」（whistler）。可我告诉她，我自己的父亲就是位吹口哨的（whistler），从前一天到晚吹个不停，我连他一张画像都没有；他每回上小石城去，我都求他去照相馆照一张相，他偏不去。
后来我们又遇见一位叫奇泽尔比夫人（Lady Chizzleby）的太太，想请我们去她家喝茶，可我们对她说，我们真的什么都不想买。但她说她没有东西要卖，只想借五英镑。于是我们没去。我真庆幸艾斯曼先生没来伦敦，不然全英国的太太们都要请他喝茶，他准会满载一船贝壳花、狗和古董画回去，那些玩意儿对谁都没好处。
昨晚，小猪猪和我、多萝西，还有那位叫杰拉尔德（Gerald）的舞师一起去了基特卡特俱乐部（Kit Kat Club），反正杰拉尔德闲着没事，他正失业。多萝西和我小小拌了几句嘴，因为我对多萝西说，跟一个失业的绅士来往，纯粹是浪费时间，可多萝西总是动不动就真喜欢上什么人，她永远学不会怎么做事。我是说，我总觉得，一个姑娘要是真心喜欢跟一位绅士待在一起，那她就相当吃亏了，不会有好结果。
嗯，今晚可要有好戏了，因为福尔肯少校今晚要带多萝西和我去一位太太家的舞会，见威尔士亲王。这会儿我得去打扮打扮，好见小猪猪，因为我和他似乎快成相当要好的朋友了，虽说他连一朵花都还没送过我。

===Original===
April 19th:
Last night we really met the Prince of Wales. I mean Major Falcon called for Dorothy and I at eleven and took us to a ladys house where the lady was having a party. The Prince of Wales is really wonderful. I mean even if he was not a prince he would be wonderful, because even if he was not a prince, he would be able to make his living playing the ukelele, if he had a little more practice. So the lady came up to me and told me that the Prince of Wales would like to meet me, so she gave us an introduction to one another and I was very very thrilled when he asked me for a dance. So I decided I would write down every word he said to me in my diary so I could always go back and read it over and over when I am really old. So then we started to dance and I asked him if he was still able to be fond of horses, and he said he was. So after our dance was all over he asked Dorothy for a dance but Dorothy will never learn how to act in front of a prince.
