Because she handed me her fan and she said “Hold this while I slip a new page into English histry,” right in front of the Prince of Wales. So I was very very worried while Dorothy was dancing with the Prince of Wales because she talked to the Prince of Wales all the time and when she got through the Prince of Wales wrote some of the slang words she is always saying on his cuff, so if he tells the Queen some day to be “a good Elk” or some other slang word Dorothy is always saying, the Queen will really blame me for bringing such a girl into English society. So when Dorothy came back we had quite a little quarrel because Dorothy said that since I met the Prince of Wales I was becoming too English. But really, I mean to say, I often remember papa back in Arkansas and he often used to say that his grandpa came from a place in England called Australia, so really, I mean to say, it is no wonder that the English seems to come out of me sometimes. Because if a girl seems to have an English accent I really think it is quite jolly.

===Chinese===
4月19日：
昨晚我们真见到威尔士亲王了。我是说，福尔肯少校十一点来接多萝西和我，带我们去一位太太家，那位太太正开着舞会。威尔士亲王真是了不起。我是说，就算他不是亲王，他也了不起，因为就算他不是亲王，他也能靠弹尤克里里谋生——只要再多练一练。后来那位太太走过来对我说，威尔士亲王想认识我，于是她给我们互相引见，他请我跳舞的时候，我真是激动得不得了。于是我决定，把他对我说的一字一句都记进日记里，等我真老了，还可以翻出来一遍一遍地读。接着我们跳起舞来，我问他是不是依旧喜欢马，他说喜欢。我们这支舞跳完，他又请多萝西跳，可多萝西在亲王面前永远学不会规矩。因为她把手里的扇子往我手里一塞，说：“替我拿着，我去给英国历史添新的一页。”当着威尔士亲王的面就这么说。多萝西跟威尔士亲王跳舞的时候，我一直提心吊胆，因为她跟威尔士亲王从头讲到尾，跳完了，威尔士亲王竟把她满嘴的那些俚语词儿记了几个在袖口上，哪天他要是叫王后「当个好麋鹿」或者别的什么多萝西常挂嘴边的俚语，王后准会怪我，怪我把这么个姑娘带进了英国社交界。多萝西跳完回来，我们俩就小小拌了几句嘴，因为多萝西说，我自从见了威尔士亲王，就变得太英国腔了。可说真的，我是说，我常常想起在阿肯色州的爸爸，他常说他的爷爷是从英国一个叫澳大利亚的地方来的，所以，我是说，难怪我身上有时会冒出英国味儿来。因为一个姑娘要是说话带英国口音，我觉得实在挺妙的。

===Original===
April 20th:
Yesterday afternoon I really thought I ought to begin to educate Piggie how to act with a girl like American gentlemen act with a girl. So I asked him to come up to have tea in our sitting room in the hotel because I had quite a headache. I mean I really look quite cute in my pink negligay. So I sent out a bell hop friend of Dorothy and I who is quite a nice boy who is called Harry and who we talk to quite a lot. So I gave Harry ten pounds of English money and I told him to go to the most expensive florist and to buy some very very expensive orchids for 10 pounds and to bring them to our sitting room at fifteen minutes past five and not to say a word but to say they were for me. So Piggie came to tea and we were having tea when Harry came in and he did not say a word but he gave me a quite large box and he said it was for me. So I opened the box and sure enough they were a dozen very very beautiful orchids. So I looked for a card, but of course there was no card so I grabbed Piggie and I said I would have to give him quite a large hug because it must have been him. But he said it was not him. But I said it must be him because I said that there was only one gentleman in London who was so sweet and generous and had such a large heart to send a girl one dozen orchids like him. So he still said it was not him. But I said I knew it was him, because there was not a gentleman in London so really marvelous and so wonderful and such a marvelous gentleman to send a girl one dozen orchids every day as him. So I really had to apologize for giving him such a large hug but I told him I was so full of impulses that when I knew he was going to send me one dozen orchids every day I became so impulsive I could not help it!
So then Dorothy and Gerald came in and I told them all about what a wonderful gentleman Piggie turned out to be and I told them when a gentleman sent a girl one dozen orchids every day he really reminded me of a prince. So Piggie blushed quite a lot and he was really very very pleased and he did not say any more that it was not him. So then I started to make a fuss over him and I told him he would have to look out because he was really so good looking and I was so full of impulses that I might even lose my mind some time and give him a kiss. So Piggie really felt very very good to be such a good looking gentleman. So he could not help blushing all the time and he could not help grinning all the time from one ear to another. So he asked us all to dinner and then he and Gerald went to change their clothes for dinner. So Dorothy and I had quite a little quarrel after they went because Dorothy asked me which one of the Jesse James brothers was my father. But I told her I was not so unrefined that I would waste my time with any gentleman who was only a ballroom dancer when he had a job. So Dorothy said Gerald was a gentleman because he wrote her a note and it had a crest. So I told her to try and eat it. So then we had to get dressed.
So this morning Harry, the boy friend of ours who is the bell hop, waked me up at ten o’clock because he had a box of one dozen orchids from Piggie. So by the time Piggie pays for a few dozen orchids, the diamond tiara will really seem like quite a bargain. Because I always think that spending money is only just a habit and if you get a gentleman started on buying one dozen orchids at a time he really gets very good habits.

===Chinese===
4月20日：
昨天下午，我真觉得该开始教育教育小猪猪了，教他学学美国绅士对待姑娘的那套做派。于是我请他到我们旅馆的起居室来喝茶，因为我头疼得厉害。我是说，我穿着粉色晨衣，样子实在俏皮。于是我派出去多萝西和我的一个朋友——旅馆的行李员，是个挺不错的小伙子，叫哈里（Harry），我们常跟他聊天。我给了哈里十英镑英国钱，叫他去最贵的花店，花 10 英镑买些非常非常贵的兰花，五点一刻送到我们起居室来，一个字也别多说，只说是送给我的。小猪猪来喝茶了，我们正喝着，哈里进来了，一声不吭，捧来好大一个盒子，说是送给我的。我打开盒子，果不其然，是一打非常非常漂亮的兰花。我找卡片，当然没有卡片，于是我一把搂住小猪猪，说我非好好抱他一大下不可，因为准是他送的。可他说不是他。可我说准是他，因为伦敦只有一位绅士，像他这样体贴、这样大方、这样心胸宽广，会送姑娘一打兰花。他还是说不是他。可我就说我知道是他，因为伦敦再没有哪位绅士，像他这样真是了不起、真是了不得，这样了不起的绅士，会天天送姑娘一打兰花。于是我只好为方才抱他那一大下道歉，可我对他说，我这个人满腔冲动，一想到他要天天送我一打兰花，我就冲动得管不住自己！
接着多萝西和杰拉尔德进来了，我把小猪猪原是这么了不得的一位绅士的事讲给他们听，还说一位绅士要是天天送姑娘一打兰花，真让我想起一位王子。小猪猪的脸涨得通红，心里实在非常非常受用，也就不再说不是他送的了。于是我接着大捧特捧他，对他说他可得当心点儿，因为他长得实在太俊，我又满腔冲动，说不定哪天我就昏了头，赏他一个吻。小猪猪为自己是这么俊的一位绅士，感觉实在非常非常良好。他于是红着脸红个不停，咧着嘴从一只耳朵咧到另一只耳朵咧个不停。他请我们全体去吃晚饭，然后他和杰拉尔德去换晚装。他们走后，多萝西和我小小拌了几句嘴，因为多萝西问我，杰西·詹姆斯兄弟里头，哪一个是我父亲。可我对她说，我可没没教养到那种地步，去在一个有活儿干时也不过当个交谊舞师的绅士身上浪费时间。多萝西说杰拉尔德是绅士，因为他给她写过一张字条，上头还有纹章呢。我叫她把字条吃了试试。然后我们就得去换衣服了。
今天早上，我们的男朋友哈里——就是那个行李员——十点钟把我叫醒，因为小猪猪又送来一盒一打兰花。这么一来，等小猪猪付过几打兰花的钱，那顶钻石头冠就显得真算得上便宜了。因为我总觉得，花钱不过是一种习惯，你要是让一位绅士开了头，一打一打地买起兰花来，他可真就养成了非常好的习惯。

===Original===
April 21st:
Well, yesterday afternoon I took Piggie shopping on a street called Bond Street. So I took him to a jewelery store because I told him I had to have a silver picture frame because I had to have a picture of him to go in it. Because I told Piggie that when a girl gets to know such a good looking gentleman as him she really wants to have a picture of him on her dressing table where she can look at it a lot. So Piggie became quite intreeged. So we looked at all the silver picture frames. But then I told him that I really did not think a silver picture frame was good enough for a picture of him because I forgot that they had gold picture frames until I saw them. So then we started to look at the gold picture frames. So then it came out that his picture was taken in his unaform. So I said he must be so good looking in his unaform that I really did not think even the gold picture frames were good enough but they did not have any platinum picture frames so we had to buy the best one we could.
So then I asked him if he could put on his unaform tomorrow because I would love to see him in his unaform and we could go to tea at Mrs. Weeks. So he really became very pleased because he grinned quite a lot and he said that he would. So then I said that poor little I would really look like nothing at all to be going out with him in his georgous unaform. So then we started to look at some bracelets but a lady friend of his who is quite friendly with his wife, who is in their country house in the country, came in to the store, so Piggie became quite nervous to be caught in a jewelery store where he has not been for years and years, so we had to go out.
This morning Gerald called up Dorothy and he said that day after tomorrow they are having a theatrical garden party to sell things to people for charity so he asked if Dorothy and I would be one of the ones who sells things to people for charity. So we said we would.
So now I must telephone Mrs. Weeks and say I will bring Sir Francis Beekman to tea tomorrow and I hope it all comes out all right. But I really wish Piggie would not tell so many storys. I mean I do not mind a gentleman when he tells a great many storys if they are new, but a gentleman who tells a great many storys and they are all the same storys is quite enervating. I mean London is really so uneducational that all I seem to be learning is some of Piggies storys and I even want to forget them. So I am really becoming jolly well fed up with London.

===Chinese===
4月21日：
嗯，昨天下午我带小猪猪去一条叫邦德街（Bond Street）的街上买东西。我带他进了一家珠宝店，因为我告诉他，我得买个银相框，因为我得放一张他的照片进去。因为我对小猪猪说，一个姑娘结识了他这么俊的一位绅士，就真想把他的照片摆在梳妆台上，好时时刻刻看个够。小猪猪于是相当来劲。我们把银相框看了个遍。可后来我对他说，我实在觉得银相框配不上他的照片——我一看见金相框才想起来，他们家还有金相框呢。于是我们又开始看金相框。看着看着，说定他的照片要穿制服照。我说他穿上制服准俊得不得了，恐怕连金相框都配不上，可惜他们没有白金相框，我们只好买了最好的一只。
于是我问他明天能不能穿上制服，因为我真爱看他穿制服的样子，我们还可以去威克斯夫人家喝茶。他一听真是高兴坏了，咧着嘴直笑，说一定穿。于是我叹道，可怜小小的我陪着他穿一身华丽制服出门，可真要黯淡得看不见影儿了。接着我们开始看手镯，可他的一位太太朋友进了店——这位太太跟他妻子交情颇深，眼下正住在他们乡下的乡间宅邸里——小猪猪想到自己多年多年没进珠宝店、偏偏在这儿被撞见，顿时慌了神，我们只好出去。
今天早上杰拉尔德打电话给多萝西，说后天他们要办一场戏剧游园会，向人们义卖东西做慈善，他问多萝西和我愿不愿意当义卖的人。我们说愿意。
这会儿我得给威克斯夫人打电话，说我明天带弗朗西斯·比克曼爵士去喝茶，但愿一切顺顺当当。可我真心希望小猪猪别讲那么多故事。我是说，一位绅士讲很多故事我倒不介意，只要故事新鲜，可一位绅士讲很多故事、翻来覆去又全是同一批故事，那可真让人泄气。我是说，伦敦实在不长学问，我学到的好像净是小猪猪的几个故事，连这几个我都想忘掉。所以我真是受够伦敦了，真的。

===Original===
April 22nd:
Yesterday Piggie came in his unaform but he was really quite upset because he had a letter. I mean his wife is coming to London because she always comes to London every year to get her old clothes made over as she has a girl who does it very very cheap. So she is going to stay with the lady who saw us in the jewelery store, because it always saves money to stay with a friend. So I wanted to cheer Piggie up so I told him that I did not think the lady saw us and if she did see us, she really could not believe her eyes to see him in a jewelery store. But I did not tell him that I think that Dorothy and I had better go to Paris soon. Because, after all, Piggie’s society is beginning to tell on a girls nerves. But I really made Piggie feel quite good about his unaform because I told him I only felt fit to be with him in a diamond tiara. So then I told him that, even if his wife was in London, we could still be friends, because I could not help but admire him even if his wife was in London and I told him I really thought a thing like that was nearly always the result of fate. So then we went to tea at Mrs. Weeks. So Piggie arranged with Mrs. Weeks to pay her for the diamond tiara and she nearly fell dead but she will keep it a secret because no one would believe it anyway. So now I have the diamond tiara and I have to admit that everything always turns out for the best. But I promised Piggie that I would always stay in London and we would always be friendly. Because Piggie always says that I am the only one who admires him for what he really is.

===Chinese===
4月22日：
昨天小猪猪穿着制服来了，可他实在心烦得很，因为他收到一封信。我是说，他妻子要来伦敦了，因为她每年总要来伦敦一趟，把旧衣裳拿去翻改，她有个姑娘改得非常非常便宜。她要住到那位在珠宝店见过我们的太太家去，因为住朋友家总归省钱。我想让小猪猪宽宽心，就对他说，我想那位太太没看见我们，就算看见了，看见他居然出现在珠宝店里，她也真不敢相信自己的眼睛。可我没告诉他，我觉得多萝西和我还是趁早去巴黎的好。因为说到底，小猪猪的陪伴已经渐渐磨人的神经了。不过我倒真把小猪猪哄得对制服美滋滋的，因为我对他说，我非得戴上钻石头冠，才配跟他走在一起。接着我对他说，就算他妻子来了伦敦，我们照样可以做朋友，因为就算他妻子在伦敦，我也忍不住仰慕他，我还说，这种事十有八九是命运的安排。然后我们去威克斯夫人家喝茶。小猪猪跟威克斯夫人谈妥，由他来付那顶钻石头冠的钱，她差点当场昏死过去，不过这事她会保密的，反正说了也没人信。如今钻石头冠到手了，我不得不承认，事情到头来总是往最好的方向走。可我也答应了小猪猪，说我会一直留在伦敦，我们永远是好朋友。因为小猪猪总说，只有我一人仰慕的是他本来的样子。

===Original===
April 25th:
Well, we were so busy the last days I did not have time to write in my diary because now we are on a ship that seems to be quite a small ship to be sailing to Paris and we will be at Paris this afternoon. Because it does not take nearly so long to come to Paris as it does to come to London. I mean it seems quite unusual to think that it takes 6 days to come to London and only one day to come to Paris.
So Dorothy is quite upset because she did not want to come as she is madly in love with Gerald and Gerald said that we really ought not to leave London without going to see England while we happened to be here. But I told him that if England was the same kind of a place that London seems to be, I really know to much to bother with such a place. I mean we had quite a little quarrel because Gerald showed up at the station with a bangle for Dorothy so I told Dorothy she was well rid of such a person. So Dorothy had to come with me because Mr. Eisman is paying her expenses because he wants Dorothy to be my chaperone.
So the last thing in London was the garden party. I sold quite a lot of red baloons and I sold a red baloon to Harry Lauder the famous Scotch gentleman who is the famous Scotch tenor for 20 pounds. So Dorothy said I did not need to buy any ticket to Paris on the boat because if I could do that, I could walk across the channel.
So Piggy does not know that we have gone but I sent him a letter and told him I would see him some time again some time. And I was really glad to get out of our rooms at the Ritz﻿—I mean 50 or 60 orchids really make a girl think of a funeral. So I cabled Mr. Eisman and I told him we could not learn anything in London because we knew to much, so if we went to Paris at least we could learn French, if we made up our mind to it.
So I am really very very intreeged as I have heard so much about Paris and I feel that it must be much more educational than London and I can hardly wait to see the Ritz hotel in Paris.

===Chinese===
4月25日：
嗯，这几天忙得我连写日记的工夫都没有，因为这会儿我们已经在船上了，开往巴黎的船似乎小了点，今天下午就到巴黎。因为来巴黎远不如去伦敦那么费工夫。我是说，来伦敦要花 6 天，来巴黎只要一天，想想真是稀奇。
多萝西一路闷闷不乐，因为她本不想来，她正疯狂地爱着杰拉尔德，杰拉尔德还说，我们既然碰巧到了这里，实在不该连英格兰都没看一眼就离开伦敦。可我对他说，要是英格兰跟伦敦看来是同一种货色，那我懂得实在太多，犯不着为这种地方费神。我是说，我们还小小拌了几句嘴，因为杰拉尔德赶到车站，送给多萝西一只「bangle」（镯子），我对多萝西说，甩掉这么个人真是甩得好。多萝西只好跟我走，因为艾斯曼先生替她出路费，他要多萝西给我当陪护。
在伦敦的最后一桩事就是那场游园会。我卖掉了好多好多红气球，还卖了一个红气球给哈里·劳德（Harry Lauder），那位有名的苏格兰绅士、有名的苏格兰男高音，卖了 20 英镑。多萝西说，我用不着买船票去巴黎了，因为我既然有这本事，索性从海峡上走过去好了。
小猪猪并不知道我们走了，不过我给他留了封信，说哪天有空我还会再见他的。能搬出丽兹酒店我们的房间，我真是松了口气——我是说，50 支、60 支兰花堆在屋里，真让姑娘想起葬礼。于是我给艾斯曼先生拍了电报，说我们在伦敦什么也学不到，因为我们懂得太多，要是去巴黎，至少还能学学法文——只要我们下得了决心。
于是我真是非常非常心痒难耐，巴黎的大名我听了那么多，我觉得它一定比伦敦长学问得多，我简直等不及要看看巴黎的丽兹酒店了。

## IV Paris Is Devine / 四 巴黎真是妙极了

===Original===
April 27th:
Paris is devine. I mean Dorothy and I got to Paris yesterday, and it really is devine. Because the French are devine. Because when we were coming off the boat, and we were coming through the customs, it was quite hot and it seemed to smell quite a lot and all the French gentlemen in the customs, were squealing quite a lot. So I looked around and I picked out a French gentleman who was really in a very gorgeous uniform and he seemed to be a very, very important gentleman and I gave him twenty francs worth of French money and he was very very gallant and he knocked everybody else down and took our bags right through the custom. Because I really think that twenty Francs is quite cheap for a gentleman that has got on at least $100 worth of gold braid on his coat alone, to speak nothing of his trousers.

I mean the French gentlemen always seem to be squealing quite a lot, especially taxi drivers when they only get a small size yellow dime called a “fifty santeems” for a tip. But the good thing about French gentlemen is that every time a French gentleman starts in to squeal, you can always stop him with five francs, no matter who he is. I mean it is so refreshing to listen to a French gentleman stop squeaking, that it would really be quite a bargain even for ten francs.

===Chinese===
4月27日：
巴黎真是妙极了。我是说，多萝西（Dorothy）和我昨天到了巴黎，它真是妙极了。因为法国人妙极了。因为下船的时候，我们正过海关，天气挺热，气味好像也挺冲，海关里的法国绅士们个个尖叫个不停。于是我四下一望，挑中了一位制服实在华丽非凡的法国绅士，他瞧着是位非常非常重要的绅士，我便给了他相当于 20 法郎的法国钱，他顿时非常非常有骑士风度，把别人统统撞开，提着我们的行李一路直通过海关。因为我真心觉得，光外套上就至少缝着价值 100 美元金绦带的一位绅士，20 法郎就能打发，实在是便宜——这还没算他的裤子呢。

我是说，法国绅士们好像总在尖声叫嚷，尤其是出租车司机，要是小费只拿到一枚小个儿的黄色硬币——那玩意儿叫「五十生丁」（fifty santeems）——更是嚷得凶。不过法国绅士的好处是：不管他是谁，每逢一位法国绅士开始叫嚷，你总可以用 5 法郎叫他住口。我是说，听一位法国绅士戛然住口，实在叫人神清气爽，就算花上 10 法郎也真算得上划算。

===Original===
So we came to the Ritz Hotel and the Ritz Hotel is devine. Because when a girl can sit in a delightful bar and have delicious champagne cocktails and look at all the important French people in Paris, I think it is devine. I mean when a girl can sit there and look at the Dolly sisters and Pearl White and Maybelle Gilman Corey, and Mrs. Nash, it is beyond worlds. Because when a girl looks at Mrs. Nash and realizes what Mrs. Nash has got out of gentlemen, it really makes a girl hold her breath.

And when a girl walks around and reads all of the signs with all of the famous historical names it really makes you hold your breath. Because when Dorothy and I went on a walk, we only walked a few blocks but in only a few blocks we read all of the famous historical names, like Coty and Cartier and I knew we were seeing something educational at last and our whole trip was not a failure. I mean I really try to make Dorothy get educated and have reverance. So when we stood at the corner of a place called the Place Vandome, if you turn your back on a monument they have in the middle and look up, you can see none other than Coty’s sign. So I said to Dorothy, does it not really give you a thrill to realize that that is the historical spot where Mr. Coty makes all the perfume? So then Dorothy said that she supposed Mr. Coty came to Paris and he smelled Paris and he realized that something had to be done. So Dorothy will really never have any reverance.

===Chinese===
于是我们住进了丽兹酒店，丽兹酒店真是妙极了。因为一个姑娘能坐在怡人的酒吧里，喝着美味的香槟鸡尾酒，把巴黎所有的重要人物看个遍，我觉得这就叫妙极了。我是说，一个姑娘能坐在那里，看多莉姐妹（the Dolly sisters）、珀尔·怀特（Pearl White）、梅贝尔·吉尔曼·科里夫人（Maybelle Gilman Corey），还有纳什夫人（Mrs. Nash），那简直超出人间之外了。因为一个姑娘望着纳什夫人，一想到纳什夫人从绅士们身上弄到手的一切，真会紧张得屏住呼吸。

还有，一个姑娘在街上走走，读读那些满载著名历史名号的招牌，也真叫人屏住呼吸。因为多萝西和我出去散步，只走了几个街区，可就在这几个街区里，我们把所有著名的历史名号读了个遍，比如科蒂（Coty）和卡地亚（Cartier），我就知道我们总算看到长学问的东西了，我们这一路总算没有白来。我是说，我一向努力让多萝西受受教育、长长敬畏之心。于是我们站在一个叫旺多姆广场（Place Vendôme）的地方的拐角上，你只要背对广场中央那座纪念碑，一抬头，看见的不是别个，正是科蒂的招牌。于是我对多萝西说，一想到这就是科蒂先生酿造所有香水的圣地，你难道不激动吗？多萝西却说，她猜科蒂先生当年来到巴黎，闻了闻巴黎的味儿，就明白总得有人做点什么。所以多萝西真是永远也不会有什么敬畏之心。

===Original===
So then we saw a jewelry store and we saw some jewelry in the window and it really seemed to be a very very great bargain but the price marks all had francs on them and Dorothy and I do not seem to be mathematical enough to tell how much francs is in money. So we went in and asked and it seems it was only 20 dollars and it seems it is not diamonds but it is a thing called “paste” which is the name of a word which means imitations. So Dorothy said “paste” is the name of the word a girl ought to do to a gentleman that handed her one. I mean I would really be embarrassed, but the gentleman did not seem to understand Dorothy’s english.

So it really makes a girl feel depressed to think a girl could not tell that it was nothing but an imitation. I mean a gentleman could deceeve a girl because he could give her a present and it would only be worth 20 dollars. So when Mr. Eisman comes to Paris next week, if he wants to make me a present I will make him take me along with him because he is really quite an inveteran bargain hunter at heart. So the gentleman at the jewelry store said that quite a lot of famous girls in Paris had imitations of all their jewelry and they put the jewelry in the safe and they really wore the imitations, so they could wear it and have a good time. But I told him I thought that any girl who was a lady would not even think of having such a good time that she did not remember to hang on to her jewelry.

===Chinese===
后来我们又看见一家珠宝店，橱窗里摆着些首饰，瞧着真像是一桩非常非常划算的买卖，可标价签上写的全是法郎，多萝西和我好像数学不够好，算不出法郎合多少钱。于是我们进去一问，原来才合 20 美元，原来那不是钻石，是一种叫「paste」（假钻）的东西，这个词的意思就是仿造品。于是多萝西说，「paste」这个词，正该让姑娘拿来对付那位递给她一粒假钻的绅士——照这个词的另一个讲法，狠狠糊他一记。我是说，我真是难为情死了，好在那位先生好像听不懂多萝西的英文。

想想一个姑娘居然看不出那不过是件仿造品，真让人丧气。我是说，一位绅士是能够欺骗姑娘的，因为他可以送她一件礼物，而它只值 20 美元。所以等艾斯曼先生下礼拜到巴黎来，要是他想送我件礼物，我就要让他带着我一起去挑，因为他骨子里实在是个积习难改的专捡便宜货的老手。珠宝店那位先生说，巴黎好多有名的姑娘都给全部首饰配了仿造品，把真首饰锁进保险柜，戴的其实是仿造品，这样既戴得上首饰，又能高高兴兴。可我对他说，我想凡是位淑女的姑娘，绝不会玩得高兴到连看好自己的首饰都忘了的地步。

===Original===
So then we went back to the Ritz and unpacked our trunks with the aid of really a delightful waiter who brought us up some delicious luncheon and who is called Leon and who speaks english almost like an American and who Dorothy and I talk to quite a lot. So Leon said that we ought not to stay around the Ritz all of the time, but we really ought to see Paris. So Dorothy said she would go down in the lobby and meet some gentleman to show us Paris. So in a couple of minutes she called up on the telephone from the lobby and she said “I have got a French bird down here who is a French title nobleman, who is called a veecount so come on down.” So I said “How did a Frenchman get into the Ritz.” So Dorothy said “He came in to get out of the rain and he has not noticed that it is stopped.” So I said “I suppose you have picked up something without taxi fare as usual. Why did you not get an American gentleman who always have money?” So Dorothy said she thought a French gentleman had ought to know Paris better. So I said “He does not even know it is not raining.” But I went down.

===Chinese===
于是我们回到丽兹酒店，在一位实在讨人喜欢的侍者帮助下打开行李箱，他还给我们端上了美味的午餐，他叫莱昂（Leon），英文说得几乎跟美国人一样，多萝西和我常跟他聊天。莱昂说，我们不该一天到晚守着丽兹酒店转，实在应该去看看巴黎。于是多萝西说她要下楼到大堂里去结识个把绅士，带我们逛巴黎。没过两分钟，她就从楼下大堂打来电话说：「我这儿逮着一只法国鸟儿，是个有法国头衔的贵族，叫什么『子爵』（veecount），快下来吧。」我说：「一个法国人是怎么混进丽兹来的？」多萝西说：「他是进来躲雨的，还没发现雨早停了。」我说：「我猜你又是照老样子，捡了个连车钱都付不起的。你怎么不找个美国绅士，美国人总是有钱的？」多萝西说，她觉得法国绅士总该更熟巴黎。我说：「他连天不下雨都不知道。」可我还是下去了。

===Original===
So the veecount was really delightful after all. So then we rode around and we saw Paris and we saw how devine it really is. I mean the Eyefull Tower is devine and it is much more educational than the London Tower, because you can not even see the London Tower if you happen to be two blocks away. But when a girl looks at the Eyefull Tower she really knows she is looking at something. And it would even be very difficult not to notice the Eyefull Tower.

So then we went to a place called the Madrid to tea and it really was devine. I mean we saw the Dolley Sisters and Pearl White and Mrs. Corey and Mrs. Nash all over again.

So then we went to dinner and then we went to Momart and it really was devine because we saw them all over again. I mean in Momart they have genuine American jazz bands and quite a lot of New York people which we knew and you really would think you were in New York and it was devine. So we came back to the Ritz quite late. So Dorothy and I had quite a little quarrel because Dorothy said that when we were looking at Paris I asked the French veecount what was the name of the unknown soldier who is buried under quite a large monument. So I said I really did not mean to ask him, if I did, because what I did mean to ask him was, what was the name of his mother because it is always the mother of a dead soldier that I always seem to think about more than the dead soldier that has died.

So the French veecount is going to call up in the morning but I am not going to see him again. Because French gentlemen are really quite deceeving. I mean they take you to quite cute places and they make you feel quite good about yourself and you really seem to have a delightful time but when you get home and come to think it all over, all you have got is a fan that only cost 20 francs and a doll that they gave you away for nothing in a restaurant. I mean a girl has to look out in Paris, or she would have such a good time in Paris that she would not get anywheres. So I really think that American gentlemen are the best after all, because kissing your hand may make you feel very very good but a diamond and safire bracelet lasts forever. Besides, I do not think that I ought to go out with any gentlemen in Paris because Mr. Eisman will be here next week and he told me that the only kind of gentlemen he wants me to go out with are intelectual gentlemen who are good for a girls brains. So I really do not seem to see many gentlemen around the Ritz who seem to look like they would be good for a girl’s brains. So tomorrow we are going to go shopping and I suppose it would really be to much to expect to find a gentleman who would look to Mr. Eisman like he was good for a girls brains and at the same time he would like to take us shopping.

===Chinese===
话说回来，那位子爵还真是讨人喜欢。于是我们坐着车四处转了转，看了巴黎，也见识了它究竟有多妙。我是说，埃菲尔铁塔（Eyefull Tower）妙极了，而且比伦敦塔长学问得多，因为你哪怕碰巧只隔着两个街区，连伦敦塔都望不见。可一个姑娘望着埃菲尔铁塔，她心里就真知道自己在看一样了不起的东西。要想不去注意埃菲尔铁塔，那倒是件很难的事呢。

后来我们去一家叫「马德里」（the Madrid）的地方喝茶，那儿真是妙极了。我是说，我们又把多莉姐妹、珀尔·怀特、科里夫人和纳什夫人看了个遍。

然后我们去吃晚饭，吃完去了蒙马特（Momart），那儿也真是妙极了，因为我们把他们又看了个遍。我是说，蒙马特有正宗的美国爵士乐队，还有好些我们认识的纽约人，你真会以为自己在纽约，真是妙极了。我们回到丽兹已经挺晚了。多萝西和我小小拌了几句嘴，因为多萝西说，我们看巴黎的时候，我问那位法国子爵，埋在那座挺大的纪念碑底下的无名战士叫什么名字。我说我真不是故意要问的——就算问了，我原本想问的也是，他母亲叫什么名字，因为一位阵亡士兵的母亲，总好像比那位阵亡了的士兵更让我惦记。

那位法国子爵明早还要来电话，可我不想再见他了。因为法国绅士实在很会骗人。我是说，他们带你到些怪可爱的地方去，让你觉得自己怪不错的，你好像也真玩得挺开心，可等回了家，把事情从头到尾一想，你到手的只有一把只值 20 法郎的扇子，还有一个餐馆白送的玩偶娃娃。我是说，一个姑娘在巴黎可得当心，不然她会在巴黎玩得太开心，开心得哪儿也到不了。所以我真心觉得，到底还是美国绅士最好，因为吻你的手固然让你觉得非常非常受用，可钻石蓝宝石手镯才是永远的。再说，我觉得我在巴黎也不该跟随便哪位绅士出门，因为艾斯曼先生（Mr. Eisman）下礼拜就到，他嘱咐过我，他唯一愿意让我与之出门的，是对姑娘的头脑有好处的有智力的绅士。可我在丽兹酒店四周，实在看不出哪位绅士长得像是对姑娘的头脑有好处的样子。所以明天我们要出门买东西，我想，既要找一位在艾斯曼先生眼里像是对姑娘头脑有好处的绅士，又要他乐意带我们去买东西，这恐怕实在有点过分指望了。

===Original===
April 29th:
Yesterday was quite a day. I mean Dorothy and I were getting ready to go shopping and the telephone rang and they said that Lady Francis Beekman was downstairs and she wanted to come upstairs. So I really was quite surprised. I mean I did not know what to say, so I said all right. So then I told Dorothy and then we put our brains together. Because it seems that Lady Francis Beekman is the wife of the gentleman called Sir Francis Beekman who was the admirer of mine in London who seemed to admire me so much that he asked me if he could make me a present of a diamond tiara. So it seemed as if his wife must have heard about it, and it really seemed as if she must have come clear over from London about it. So there was a very very loud knock at the door so we asked her to come in. So Lady Francis Beekman came in and she is a quite large size lady who seems to resemble Bill Hart quite a lot. I mean Dorothy thinks that Lady Francis Beeckman resembles Bill Hart quite a lot, only she really thinks she looks more like Bill Hart’s horse. So it seems that she said that if I did not give her back the diamond tiara right away, she would make quite a fuss and she would ruin my reputation. Because she said that something really must be wrong about the whole thing. Because it seems that Sir Francis Beekman and she have been married for 35 years and the last present he gave to her was a wedding ring. So Dorothy spoke up and she said “Lady you could no more ruin my girl friends reputation than you could sink the Jewish fleet.” I mean I was quite proud of Dorothy the way she stood up for my reputation. Because I really think that there is nothing so wonderful as two girls when they stand up for each other and help each other lot. Because no matter how vigarous Lady Francis Beekman seems to be, she had to realize that she could not sink a whole fleet full of ships. So she had to stop talking against my reputation.

===Chinese===
4月29日：
昨天可真是热闹的一天。我是说，多萝西和我正收拾停当要出门买东西，电话响了，对方说弗朗西斯·比克曼夫人（Lady Francis Beekman）在楼下，想要上来。我真是相当吃惊。我是说，我不知道该说什么好，就说好吧。于是我告诉了多萝西，然后我们俩把头脑凑到了一起。因为这位弗朗西斯·比克曼夫人，就是那位叫弗朗西斯·比克曼爵士的绅士的妻子——爵士在伦敦倾慕过我，倾慕到问我能不能送我一顶钻石头冠当礼物的地步。这么看来，他妻子准是听到了风声，而且准是为这事专门从伦敦一路赶了过来。接着传来非常非常响的敲门声，我们便请她进来。弗朗西斯·比克曼夫人走了进来，她是位块头相当大的夫人，长得好像非常像比尔·哈特（Bill Hart）。我是说，多萝西觉得弗朗西斯·比克曼夫人非常像比尔·哈特，只觉得她其实更像比尔·哈特那匹马。看样子她是说了，要是我不立刻把钻石头冠还给她，她就要大闹一场，还要毁掉我的名声。因为她说，这整件事一定出了什么岔子。因为据说弗朗西斯·比克曼爵士和她结婚都 35 年了，他送她的最后一件礼物是一枚结婚戒指。于是多萝西开了腔：「夫人，你想毁掉我闺蜜的名声，就像你想击沉犹太人的舰队一样，没门儿。」我是说，多萝西这样维护我的名声，我真为她骄傲。因为我真心觉得，两个姑娘彼此维护、互相帮衬，世上再没有比这更了不起的事了。因为不管弗朗西斯·比克曼夫人多么有劲，她也不得不明白，一整支船队她是沉不了的。于是她只好不再说毁我名声的话了。

===Original===
So then she said she would drag it into the court and she would say that it was undue influence. So I said to her, “If you wear that hat into a court, we will see if the judge thinks it took an undue influence to make Sir Francis Beekman look at a girl.” So then Dorothy spoke up and Dorothy said “My girl friend is right, Lady. You have got to be the Queen of England to get away with a hat like that.” So Lady Francis Beekman seemed to get quite angry. So then she said she would send for Sir Francis Beekman where he suddenly went to Scotland, to go hunting when he found out that Lady Francis Beekman had found out. So Dorothy said “Do you mean that you have left Sir Francis Beekman loose with all those spendthrifts down in Scotland?” So Dorothy said she would better look out or he would get together with the boys some night and simply massacre a haypenny. I mean I always encouradge Dorothy to talk quite a lot when we are talking to unrefined people like Lady Francis Beekman, because Dorothy speaks their own languadge to unrefined people better than a refined girl like I. So Dorothy said, “You had better not send for Sir Francis Beekman because if my girl friend really wanted to turn loose on Sir Francis Beekman, all he would have left would be his title.” So then I spoke right up and said Yes that I was an American girl and we American girls do not care about a title because we American girls always say that what is good enough for Washington is good enough for us. So Lady Francis Beekman really seemed to get more angry and more angry all of the time.

===Chinese===
于是她又说要把这事拖上法庭，说这叫「不正当影响」（undue influence）。于是我对她说：「你要是戴着你那顶帽子上法庭，咱们倒要看看，法官会不会觉得，非得使出天大的不正当影响，才能让弗朗西斯·比克曼爵士看上一个姑娘。」于是多萝西开了腔：「我闺蜜说得对，夫人。要想戴着那么一顶帽子还能全身而退，你就得当上英国王后才行。」弗朗西斯·比克曼夫人好像气得可以。于是她又说，她要派人去找弗朗西斯·比克曼爵士——他一发觉弗朗西斯·比克曼夫人发觉了，就突然跑到苏格兰打猎去了。多萝西说：「你是说，你就把弗朗西斯·比克曼爵士撒在苏格兰，跟那帮挥金如土的家伙混在一起？」多萝西说，她最好当心点，不然哪天夜里他跟那帮小子凑到一起，没准儿会对半个便士来一场大屠杀。我是说，跟弗朗西斯·比克曼夫人这种没教养的人讲话时，我总是鼓励多萝西多讲讲，因为对付没教养的人，多萝西讲他们自己的语言，比我这样有教养的姑娘讲得好。于是多萝西说：「你还是别派人去找弗朗西斯·比克曼爵士了，因为我闺蜜要是真想对弗朗西斯·比克曼爵士撒开手来，到头来他剩下的就只有一个爵士头衔了。」于是我紧接着开了腔，说不错，我是个美国姑娘，我们美国姑娘才不在乎什么头衔，因为我们美国姑娘总说，华盛顿够用的，我们也就够用了。这么一来，弗朗西斯·比克曼夫人好像真是越气越厉害、越气越厉害了。

===Original===
So then she said that if it was necessary, she would tell the judge that Sir Francis Beekman went out of his mind when he gave it to me. So Dorothy said “Lady, if you go into a court and if the judge gets a good look at you, he will think that Sir Francis Beekman was out of his mind 35 years ago.” So then Lady Francis Beekman said she knew what kind of a person she had to deal with and she would not deal with any such a person because she said it hurt her dignity. So Dorothy said “Lady, if we hurt your dignity like you hurt our eyesight I hope for your sake, you are a Christian science.” So that seemed to make Lady Francis Beekman angry. So she said she would turn it all over to her soliciter. So when she went out she tripped over quite a long train which she had on her skirt and she nearly fell down. So Dorothy leaned out of the door and Dorothy called down the hall and said, “Take a tuck in that skirt Isabel, its 1925.” So I really felt quite depressed because I felt as if our whole morning was really very unrefined just because we had to mix with such an unrefined lady as Lady Francis Beekman.

===Chinese===
于是她又说，万一有必要，她要告诉法官，弗朗西斯·比克曼爵士把它送给我的时候是精神失常。于是多萝西说：「夫人，你要是走上法庭，法官只要好好看你一眼，就会认为弗朗西斯·比克曼爵士 35 年前就精神失常了。」于是弗朗西斯·比克曼夫人说，她算知道自己面对的是什么样的人了，这种人不值得她打交道，因为她说这有损她的尊严。于是多萝西说：「夫人，我们要是伤了你的尊严，像你伤了我们的视力一样，那我看在你的份上，但愿你是信基督教科学派的。」这话好像真把弗朗西斯·比克曼夫人惹火了。于是她说要把这事统统交给她的律师。她出去的时候，被自己裙子上那条老长的拖裙绊了一下，差点摔倒。多萝西从门里探出身去，朝着走廊那头喊：「把那条裙子往里缝一缝吧，伊莎贝尔，都 1925 年了。」我真是觉得相当丧气，因为只因为不得不跟弗朗西斯·比克曼夫人这样没教养的夫人搅在一起，我们一整个上午都显得非常没教养。

===Original===
April 30th:
So sure enough yesterday morning Lady Francis Beekman’s solicitor came. Only he really was not a solicitor, but his name was on a card and it seems his name is Mons. Broussard and it seems that he is an advocat because an advocat is a lawyer in the French landguage. So Dorothy and I were getting dressed and we were in our negligay as usual when there was quite a loud knock on the door and before we could even say come in he jumped right into the room. So it seems that he is of French extraction. I mean Lady Francis Beekman’s solicitor can really squeal just like a taxi driver. I mean he was squealing quite loud when he jumped into the room and he kept right on squealing. So Dorothy and I rushed into the parlor and Dorothy looked at him and Dorothy said, “This town has got to stop playing jokes on us every morning” because our nerves could not stand it. So Mons. Broussard handed us his card and he squealed and squealed and he really waved his arms in the air quite a lot. So Dorothy said He gives quite a good imitation of the Moulan Rouge, which is really a red wind mill, only Dorothy said he makes more noise and he runs on his own wind. So we stood and watched him for quite a long while, but he seemed to get quite monotonous after quite a long while because he was always talking in French, which really means nothing to us. So Dorothy said “Lets see if 25 francs will stop him, because if 5 francs will stop a taxi driver, 25 francs ought to stop an advocat.” Because he was making about 5 times as much noise as a taxi driver and 5 times 5 is 25. So as soon as he heard us start in to talk about francs he seemed to calm down quite a little. So Dorothy got her pocket book and she gave him 25 francs. So then he stopped squealing and he put it in his pocket, but then he got out quite a large size handkerchief with purple elefants on it and he started in to cry. So Dorothy really got discouraged and she said, “Look here, you have given us a quite an amusing morning but if you keep that up much longer, wet or dry, out you go.”

===Chinese===
4月30日：
果不其然，昨天上午弗朗西斯·比克曼夫人的律师登门了。只是他实在算不上「solicitor」（事务律师），不过名片上印着名字，原来他叫布鲁萨尔先生（Mons. Broussard），而且他是位「advocat」，因为 advocat 在法国话里就是律师的意思。多萝西和我正在换衣裳，照例穿着晨衣，忽然响起相当响的敲门声，我们连「请进」都没来得及说，他就一头冲进了房间。看样子他是有法国血统的。我是说，弗朗西斯·比克曼夫人的律师尖叫起来，真能跟出租车司机一个样。我是说，他冲进房间的时候尖叫得相当响，还一直叫个不停。多萝西和我冲进起居室，多萝西看了看他，说：「这座城市必须停止每天早上跟我们开玩笑了。」因为我们的神经受不了。布鲁萨尔先生把名片递给我们，叫了又叫，两只胳膊还着实朝天挥了好一阵。多萝西说，他学红磨坊（Moulin Rouge）学得相当像——红磨坊就是一座红色的风车——只是多萝西说，他动静更大，而且是靠自己的风力转的。我们站着看了他好半天，可看了好半天之后，他就显得相当单调了，因为他讲的全是法国话，对我们实在等于零。于是多萝西说：「咱们试试 25 法郎能不能叫他停下，既然 5 法郎能叫停一个出租车司机，25 法郎总该叫停一位 advocat。」因为他闹的动静差不多是出租车司机的 5 倍，5 乘 5 等于 25。可他一听见我们说起法郎，好像马上就安静了不少。于是多萝西拿出钱包，给了他 25 法郎。他果然不叫了，把钱装进口袋，可接着又掏出一块好大的手帕，上头印着紫色的象，竟自顾自哭了起来。多萝西真是没了脾气，她说：「我说，你今儿早上让我们乐是乐了一把，可你要再这么哭下去，管你湿的干的，一律请出去。」

===Original===
So then he started in to pointing at the telephone and he seemed to want to use the telephone and Dorothy said, “If you think you can get a number over that thing, go to it, but as far as we have found out, it is a wall bracket.” So then he started in to telephone so Dorothy and I went about our business to get dressed. So when he finished telephoning he kept running to my door and then he kept running to Dorothy’s door, and he kept on crying and talking a lot, but he seemed to have lost all of his novelty to us so we paid no more attention to him.

===Chinese===
于是他开始指着电话，好像是想用电话，多萝西说：「你要是觉得能从那玩意儿上叫通一个号码，你就去试，不过据我们摸清的情况，那玩意儿是个墙上的架子。」于是他打起电话来，多萝西和我就各干各的，接着换衣裳。他打完电话，就一个劲往我门口跑，又一个劲往多萝西门口跑，哭个不停，讲个不停，可他对我们来说已经完全没了新鲜感，我们就不再搭理他了。

===Original===
So finally there was another loud knock on the door so we heard him rush to the door so we both went in to the parlor to see what it was and it really was a sight. Because it was another Frenchman. So the new Frenchman rushed in and he yelled Papa and he kissed him. So it seems that it was his son because his son is really his papa’s partner in the advocat business. So then his papa talked quite a lot and then he pointed at I and Dorothy. So then his son looked at us and then his son let out quite a large size squeal, and he said in French “May papa, elles sont sharmant.” So it seems he was telling his papa in French that we were really charming. So then Mons. Broussard stopped crying and put on his glasses and took a good look at us. So then his son put up the window shade, so his papa could get a better look at us. So when his papa had finished looking at us he really became delighted. So he became all smiles and he pinched our cheeks and he kept on saying Sharmant all of the time because Sharmant means charming in the French languadge. So then his son broke right out into english and he really speaks english as good as an American. So then he told us his papa telephoned for him to come over because we did not seem to understand what his papa was saying to us. So it seems that Mons. Broussard had been talking to us in english all of the time but we did not seem to understand his kind of english. So Dorothy said, “If what your papa was talking in was english, I could get a gold medal for my greek.” So then his son told his papa and his papa laughed very very loud and he pinched Dorothys cheek and he was very delighted even if the joke was on him. So then Dorothy and I asked his son what he was saying, when he was talking to us in english and his son said he was telling us all about his client, Lady Francis Beekman. So then we asked his son why his papa kept crying. So then his son said his papa kept crying because he was thinking about Lady Francis Beekman. So Dorothy said, “If he cries when he thinks about her, what does he do when he looks at her?” So then his son explained to his papa what Dorothy said. So then Mons. Broussard laughed very very loud, so then he kissed Dorothy’s hand, so he said, after that, we would all really have to have a bottle of champagne. So he went to the telephone and ordered a bottle of champagne.

===Chinese===
后来门上又响起很响的敲门声，我们听见他冲过去开门，我们俩便走进起居室去看是怎么回事，那真是一景。因为来的又是一个法国人。新来的法国人冲进来，喊了一声「爸爸」，就吻起他来。原来那是他的儿子，因为儿子正是爸爸律师行里的合伙人。接着爸爸讲了好一大通，又指了指我和多萝西。儿子看了看我们，随即放出好大一声尖叫，用法语说：「哎呀爸爸，elles sont sharmant（她们真迷人）。」原来他是用法语告诉他爸爸，我们实在迷人。于是布鲁萨尔先生不哭了，戴上眼镜，把我们好好打量了一番。儿子又把窗帘卷上去，好让爸爸把我们看得更清楚些。爸爸看完我们，真是高兴坏了。他满脸堆笑，捏我们的脸蛋，嘴里一直不停地说「Sharmant」（迷人），因为 Sharmant 在法国话里就是迷人的意思。接着他儿子干脆说起英文来，他的英文说得跟美国人一样好。于是他告诉我们，他爸爸打电话叫他过来，因为我们好像听不懂他爸爸对我们说的话。原来布鲁萨尔先生从头到尾都是在跟我们讲英文，只是我们好像听不懂他那一一路的英文。于是多萝西说：「要是你爸爸方才讲的是英文，那我讲的希腊文都能拿金牌了。」于是儿子把这话告诉他爸爸，他爸爸放声大笑，笑得非常非常响，捏了捏多萝西的脸蛋，哪怕笑话落在自己头上也高兴得很。接着多萝西和我问他儿子，他方才跟我们讲话时讲的都是什么，他儿子说，他讲的全是关于他的当事人弗朗西斯·比克曼夫人的事。于是我们问他儿子，他爸爸干嘛一直哭。他儿子说，他爸爸一直哭，是因为他一想起弗朗西斯·比克曼夫人就忍不住。多萝西说：「他想起她就要哭，那他见到她本人还不得怎样？」于是儿子把多萝西的话解释给他爸爸听。布鲁萨尔先生放声大笑，笑得非常非常响，接着吻了多萝西的手，还说打这以后，我们大家真该来上一瓶香槟。于是他走到电话跟前，叫了一瓶香槟。

===Original===
So then his son said to his papa, “Why do we not ask the charming ladies to go out to Fountainblo today.” So his papa said it would be charming. So then I said, “How are we going to tell you gentlemen apart, because if it is the same in Paris as it is in America, you would both seem to be Monshure Broussard.” So then we got the idea to call them by their first name. So it seems that his son’s name is Louie so Dorothy spoke up and said, “I hear that they number all of you Louies over here in Paris.” Because a girl is always hearing someone talk about Louie the sixteenth who seemed to be in the anteek furniture business. I mean I was surprised to hear Dorothy get so historical so she may really be getting educated in spite of everything. But Dorothy told Louie he need not try to figure out his number because she got it the minute she looked at him. So it seems his papa’s name is Robber, which means Robert in French. So Dorothy started in to think about her 25 francs and she said to Robber, “Your mother certainly knew her grammer when she called you that.”

===Chinese===
于是他儿子对他爸爸说：「咱们何不请这两位迷人的女士今天去枫丹白露（Fountainblo）呢？」他爸爸说那太迷人了。于是我说：「我们可怎么把你们两位先生分清楚呢，因为要是巴黎跟美国一个规矩，你们两位岂不都成了布鲁萨尔先生。」于是我们想出了个主意，直接叫他们的名字。原来他儿子叫路易（Louie），多萝西便开腔道：「我听说你们巴黎这边的路易个个都编着号呢。」因为一个姑娘总能听见人说起路易十六，他好像是做古董家具生意的。我是说，多萝西居然这么有历史头脑，真叫我吃惊，看来她不管怎么着，可能真在长学问。不过多萝西告诉路易，他用不着费心算自己是几号，因为她一看见他，号码就有了。原来他爸爸的名字叫「罗贝尔」（Robber），照法国话讲，就是罗伯特。于是多萝西想起了她那 25 法郎，对罗贝尔说：「你妈妈给你起这个名字的时候，肯定把文法琢磨到家了。」

===Original===
So Dorothy said we might as well go out to Fountainblo with Louie and Robber if Louie would take off his yellow spats that were made out of yellow shammy skin with pink pearl buttons. Because Dorothy said, “Fun is fun but no girl wants to laugh all of the time.” So Louie is really always anxious to please, so he took off his spats but when he took off his spats, we saw his socks and when we saw his socks we saw that they were Scotch plaid with small size rainbows running through them. So Dorothy looked at them a little while and she really became quite discouraged and she said, “Well Louie, I think you had better put your spats back on.”

So then Leon, our friend who is the waiter, came in with the bottle of champagne. So while he was opening the bottle of champagne Louie and Robber talked together in French quite a lot and I really think I had ought to find out what they said in French because it might be about the diamond tiara. Because French gentlemen are very very gallant, but I really do not think a girl can trust one of them around a corner. So, when I get a chance, I am going to ask Leon what they said.

So then we went to Fountainblo and then we went to Momart and we got home very late, and we really had quite a delightful day and night, even if we did not go out shopping and buy anything. But I really think we ought to do more shopping because shopping really seems to be what Paris is principaly for.

===Chinese===
于是多萝西说，只要路易肯脱掉他那双黄色鞋罩——黄麂皮做的，缀着粉色珍珠纽扣——我们倒不妨跟路易和罗贝尔去一趟枫丹白露。因为多萝西说：「玩乐归玩乐，可没有哪个姑娘想笑个没完。」路易这人真是处处巴结人，当下就脱了鞋罩，可他一脱鞋罩，我们就看见了他的袜子，一看袜子，才发现原来是苏格兰格子呢的，里头还穿行着一道道小彩虹。多萝西瞧了一会儿，真是没了指望，说：「我说路易，我看你还是把鞋罩穿回去吧。」

于是我们的朋友、那位当侍者的莱昂，端着一瓶香槟进来了。他开香槟的当儿，路易和罗贝尔用法语聊了好一大通，我真心觉得我该弄清楚他们说了什么法语，因为说不定说的就是钻石头冠。因为法国绅士非常有骑士风度，可我实在觉得，姑娘没法信得过他们，一转过街角就不行。所以一有机会，我就要去问问莱昂他们说了什么。

于是我们去了枫丹白露，后来又去了蒙马特，回到家已经很晚，这一天一夜我们实在过得非常愉快，尽管没出门买东西、什么也没买。可我真心觉得我们应该多买点东西，因为买东西好像正是巴黎顶要紧的用途。

===Original===
May 1st:
Well this morning I sent for Leon, who is Dorothy and my waiter friend, and I asked him what Louie and Robber said in French. So it seems that they said in French that we seemed to attract them very very much because they really thought that we were very very charming, and they had not met girls that were so charming in quite a long time. So it seems that they said that they would ask us out a lot and that they would charge up all the bills to Lady Francis Beekman because they would watch for their chance and they would steal the diamond tiara. So then they said that even if they could not steal it from us, we were really so charming that it would be delightful to go around with us, even if they could not steal from us. So no matter what happens they really could not lose. Because it seems that Lady Francis Beekman would be glad to pay all the bills when they told her they had to take us out a lot so they could watch for their chance and steal it. Because Lady Francis Beekman is the kind of a wealthy lady that does not spend money on anything else but she will always spend money on a lawsuit. And she really would not mind spending the money because it seems that something either I or Dorothy said to Lady Francis Beekman seemed to make her angry.

===Chinese===
5月1日：
嗯，今天早上我派人把莱昂——多萝西和我的那位侍者朋友——请了来，我问他路易和罗贝尔用法语说了些什么。原来他们法语里说的是，我们好像把他们迷得不轻，因为他们真心觉得我们非常非常迷人，好久好久没遇上这么迷人的姑娘了。据说他们还说，他们要常常请我们出门，把账单统统记到弗朗西斯·比克曼夫人头上，因为他们要伺机下手，偷走那顶钻石头冠。他们还说，就算从我们手里偷不成，我们实在迷人得很，陪着我们四处走走也是桩乐事，就算偷不着也不吃亏。所以不管怎样，他们横竖不吃亏。因为据说弗朗西斯·比克曼夫人一听他们得多多带我们出门、好伺机下手偷头冠，会很乐意替他们付所有的账单。因为弗朗西斯·比克曼夫人就是那种有钱夫人，别的钱一概不花，就是肯为官司花钱。而且她真不会心疼这笔钱，因为据说我或多萝西当初对弗朗西斯·比克曼夫人说的哪句话，把她气得不轻。

===Original===
So then I decided it was time to do some thinking and I really thought quite a lot. So I told Dorothy I thought I would put the real diamond tiara in the safe at the Ritz and then I would buy an imitation of a diamond tiara at the jewelry store that has the imitations that are called paste. So then I would leave the imitation of the diamond tiara lying around, so Louie and Robber could see how careless I seem to be with it so then they would get full of encouradgement. So when we go out with Louie and Robber I could put it in my hand bag and I could take it with me so Louie and Robber could always feel that the diamond tiara was within reach. So then Dorothy and I could get them to go shopping and we could get them to spend quite a lot and every time they seemed to get discouradged, I could open my hand bag, and let them get a glimpse of the imitation of a diamond tiara and they would become more encouradged and then they would spend some more money. Because I even might let them steal it at the last, because they were really charming gentlemen after all and I really would like to help Louie and Robber. I mean it would be quite amusing for them to steal it for Lady Francis Beekman and she would have to pay them quite a lot and then she would find out it was only made out of paste after all. Because Lady Francis Beekman has never seen the real diamond tiara and the imitation of a diamond tiara would really deceive her, at least until Louie and Robber got all of their money for all of the hard work they did. I mean the imitation of a diamond tiara would only cost about 65 dollars and what is 65 dollars if Dorothy and I could do some delightful shopping and get some delightful presents that would even seem more delightful when we stopped to realize that Lady Francis Beekman paid for them. And it would teach Lady Francis Beekman a lesson not to say what she said to two American girls like I and Dorothy, who were all alone in Paris and had no gentleman to protect them.

===Chinese===
于是我断定该动动脑子了，我也着实想了好多。我对多萝西说，我想把真正的钻石头冠存进丽兹的保险柜，再去那家卖「paste」假钻仿造品的珠宝店，买一顶假钻头冠。然后我就把那顶假钻头冠随处乱放，好让路易和罗贝尔瞧见我拿它多么不当心，他们便会勇气百倍。等我们跟路易和罗贝尔出门时，我可以把它装进手提包，随身带着，好让路易和罗贝尔时刻觉得钻石头冠近在咫尺。然后多萝西和我就能哄他们去买东西，让他们花上一大笔，每逢他们露出泄气的样子，我就打开手提包，让他们瞟一眼那顶假钻头冠，他们便会重新鼓起勇气，接着再花上一笔。到末了我甚至可以让他们把它偷走，因为他们到底是两位迷人的绅士，我真心想帮路易和罗贝尔一把。我是说，让他们把它偷去交给弗朗西斯·比克曼夫人，倒也挺好玩，她得付他们好大一笔钱，然后才发现它终究不过是假钻做的。因为弗朗西斯·比克曼夫人从来没见过真正的钻石头冠，假钻头冠准能骗过她，至少能撑到路易和罗贝尔把他们辛苦钱统统挣到手。我是说，假钻头冠不过值 65 美元上下，只要多萝西和我能痛痛快快买东西、收些痛痛快快的礼物——而且一想到这些都是弗朗西斯·比克曼夫人掏的钱，礼物显得更痛快了——65 美元又算得了什么。再说这也能教训教训弗朗西斯·比克曼夫人，谁叫她对我跟多萝西这样两个孤身在巴黎、连个保护的绅士都没有的美国姑娘说那种话。

===Original===
So when I got through telling Dorothy what I thought up, Dorothy looked at me and looked at me and she really said she thought my brains were a miracle. I mean she said my brains reminded her of a radio because you listen to it for days and days and you get discouradged and just when you are getting ready to smash it, something comes out that is a masterpiece.

So then Louie called us up so Dorothy told him that we thought it would be delightful if he and Robber would take us out shopping tomorrow morning. So then Louie asked his papa and his papa said they would. So then they asked us if we would like to go to see a play called The Foley Bergere tonight. So he said that all of the French people who live in Paris are always delighted to have some Americans, so it will give them an excuse to go to the Foley Bergere. So we said we would go. So now Dorothy and I are going out shopping to buy the imitation of a diamond tiara and we are going out window shopping to pick out where we would like Louie and Robber to take us shopping tomorrow.

So I really think that everything always works out for the best. Because after all, we really need some gentlemen to take us around until Mr. Eisman gets to Paris and we could not go around with any really attractive gentlemen because Mr. Eisman only wants me to go out with gentlemen that have brains. So I said to Dorothy that, even if Louie and Robber do not look so full of brains, we could tell Mr. Eisman that all we were learning from them was French. So even if I have not seemed to learn French yet, I have really almost learned to understand Robbers english so when Robber talks in front of Mr. Eisman and I seem to understand what he is saying, Mr. Eisman will probably think I know French.

===Chinese===
等我把想好的这番话对多萝西讲完，多萝西看了我又看我，当真说了一句，她觉得我的头脑是个奇迹。我是说，她说我的头脑让她想起收音机，你一天又一天听它，越听越泄气，正要把砸了，它忽然蹦出个绝世杰作来。

后来路易来电话，多萝西便对他说，要是他跟罗贝尔明天上午带我们出门买东西，那真是再愉快不过了。于是路易问了他爸爸，他爸爸说好。于是他们问我们今晚愿不愿意去看一出叫《福利贝热尔》（Foley Bergere）的戏。他说，凡是住在巴黎的法国人，一有美国人来就高兴得很，因为正好有借口去福利贝热尔。于是我们说去。这会儿多萝西和我正要出门，去买那顶假钻头冠，还要沿街逛逛橱窗，挑好明天想让路易和罗贝尔带我们去买东西的地方。

所以我真心觉得，事情到头来总是往最好的方向走。因为说到底，在艾斯曼先生到巴黎之前，我们实在需要几位绅士带着到处走走，可我们又不能跟真正有魅力的绅士出门，因为艾斯曼先生只许我跟有头脑的绅士来往。于是我对多萝西说，就算路易和罗贝尔瞧着头脑不太灵光，我们可以对艾斯曼先生说，我们从他们那儿学的净是法语。这么着，就算我好像还没学会法语，我其实已经差不多能听懂罗贝尔的英文了，等罗贝尔当着艾斯曼先生讲话、我又好像听懂了他在说什么，艾斯曼先生八成会当我懂法语了。

===Original===
May 2nd:
So last night we went to the Foley Bergere and it really was devine. I mean it was very very artistic because it had girls in it that were in the nude. So one of the girls was a friend of Louie and he said that she was a very very nice girl, and that she was only 18 years of age. So Dorothy said, “She is slipping it over on you Louie, because how could a girl get such dirty knees in only 18 years?” So Louie and Robber really laughed very very loud. I mean Dorothy was very unrefined at the Foley Bergere. But I always think that when girls are in the nude it is very artistic and if you have artistic thoughts you think it is beautiful and I really would not laugh in an artistic place like the Foley Bergere.

So I wore the imitation of a diamond tiara to the Foley Bergere. I mean it really would deceeve an expert and Louie and Robber could hardly take their eyes off of it. But they did not really annoy me because I had it tied on very very tight. I mean it would be fatal if they got the diamond tiara before Dorothy and I took them shopping a lot.

So we are all ready to go shopping this morning and Robber was here bright and early and he is in the parlor with Dorothy and we are waiting for Louie. So I left the daimond tiara on the table in the parlor so Robber could see how careless I really am with everything but Dorothy is keeping her eye on Robber. So I just heard Louie come in because I heard him kissing Robber. I mean Louie is always kissing Robber and Dorothy told Louie that if he did not stop kissing Robber, people would think that he painted batiks.

So now I must join the others and I will put the diamond tiara in my hand bag so that Louie and Robber will feel that it is always around and we will all go shopping. And I almost have to smile when I think of Lady Francis Beekman.

===Chinese===
5月2日：
于是昨晚我们去了福利贝热尔，那儿真是妙极了。我是说，它非常非常有艺术性，因为里头有姑娘们光着身子。其中有一位姑娘是路易的朋友，他说她是个非常非常好的姑娘，今年才 18 岁。于是多萝西说：「她在蒙你呢路易，一个姑娘 18 年里怎么肯把膝盖弄这么脏？」路易和罗贝尔真是放声大笑，笑得非常非常响。我是说，多萝西在福利贝热尔真是没教养透了。不过我一向觉得，姑娘们光着身子是很有艺术性的，你抱着艺术的想法去看，就觉得它美，在福利贝热尔这样有艺术的地方，我可绝不会笑。

我戴着那顶假钻头冠去了福利贝热尔。我是说，它真能骗过行家，路易和罗贝尔的眼睛几乎没法从它上头挪开。不过他们倒真没让我烦心，因为我把它扎得非常非常紧。我是说，要是在多萝西和我带他们大大地买一轮东西之前，他们就把钻石头冠弄到了手，那可就致命了。

今天早上我们整装待发要去买东西，罗贝尔一大早就到了，正在起居室陪多萝西，我们等路易。我把那顶钻石头冠留在了起居室的桌上，好让罗贝尔瞧瞧我对什么都不当心，不过多萝西一直盯着罗贝尔。刚才我听见路易进来了，因为我听见他在吻罗贝尔。我是说，路易老是吻罗贝尔，多萝西告诉过路易，他要是再这样吻个不停地吻罗贝尔，人家会以为他是画蜡染的。

这会儿我该去跟大伙儿会合了，我要把钻石头冠装进手提包，好让路易和罗贝尔觉得它始终在跟前，然后我们就全员出动买东西。一想起弗朗西斯·比克曼夫人，我几乎要笑出声来。

===Original===
May 3rd:
Yesterday was really delightful. I mean Louie and Robber bought Dorothy and I some delightful presents. But then they began to run out of all the franks they had with them, so they began to get discouradged but just as soon as they began to get discouradged, I gave Robber my hand bag to hold while I went to the fitting room to try on a blouse. So he was cheered up quite a lot, but of course Dorothy stayed with them and kept her eye on Robber so he did not get a chance. But it really cheered him up quite a lot to even hold it.

So after all their franks were gone, Robber said he would have to telephone to someone, so I suppose he telephoned to Lady Francis Beekman and she must have said All right because Robber left us at a place called the Café de la Paix because he had to go on an errand and when he came back from his errand he seemed to have quite a lot more franks. So then they took us to luncheon so that after luncheon we could go out shopping some more.

But I am really learning quite a lot of French in spite of everything. I mean if you want delicious chicken and peas for luncheon all you have to say is “pettypas” and “pulle.” I mean French is really very easy, for instance the French use the word “sheik” for everything, while we only seem to use it for gentlemen when they seem to resemble Rudolf Valentino.

===Chinese===
5月3日：
昨天真是愉快极了。我是说，路易和罗贝尔给多萝西和我买了些可爱的礼物。可后来他们随身带的法郎渐渐花光了，开始泄气，可他们刚一露泄气的样子，我就把手提包交给罗贝尔拿着，借口去试衣间试一件衬衫。他顿时又来了精神，当然啦，多萝西一直守在他们身边，眼睛盯着罗贝尔，没让他得手。不过光是拿着它，也真让他精神了不少。

等他们的法郎全花光了，罗贝尔说必须给什么人打个电话，我猜他是打给了弗朗西斯·比克曼夫人，她准是说好吧，因为罗贝尔在一家叫和平咖啡馆（Café de la Paix）的地方撇下了我们，说是要办点事，等他办完事回来，身上的法郎好像又多了不少。于是他们带我们去吃午饭，好吃过午饭接着再出门买东西。

不过不管怎么说，我还真学了不少法语。我是说，午饭想吃美味的鸡和豌豆，只消说「pettypas」（小豌豆）和「pulle」（鸡）就行了。我是说，法语实在容易得很，比方说，法国人管什么都叫「sheik」（帅哥），我们好像只有在哪位绅士长得像鲁道夫·瓦伦蒂诺的时候，才这么叫。

===Original===
So while we were shopping in the afternoon I saw Louie get Dorothy off in a corner and whisper to her quite a lot. So then I saw Robber get her off in a corner and whisper to her quite a lot. So when we got back to the Ritz, Dorothy told me why they whispered to her. So it seems when Louie whispered to Dorothy, Louie told Dorothy that if she would steal the diamond tiara from me and give it to him and not let his papa know, he would give her 1,000 franks. Because it seems that Lady Francis Beekman has got her heart set on it and she will pay quite a lot for it because she is quite angry and when she really gets as angry as she is, she is only a woman with one idea. So if Louie could get it and his papa would not find it out, he could keep all the money for himself. So it seems that later on, when Robber was whispering to Dorothy, he was making her the same proposition for 2,000 franks so that Louie would not find out and Robber could keep all the money for himself. So I really think it would be delightful if Dorothy could make some money for herself because it might make Dorothy get some ambishions. So tomorrow morning Dorothy is going to take the diamond tiara and she is going to tell Louie that she stole it and she is going to sell it to Louie. But she will make him hand over the money first and then, just as she is going to hand over the diamond tiara, I am going to walk in on them and say, “Oh there is my diamond tiara. I have been looking for it everywhere.” So then I will get it back. So then she will tell him that she might just as well keep the 1,000 franks because she will steal it for him again in the afternoon. So in the afternoon she is going to sell it to Robber and I really think we will let Robber keep it. Because I am quite fond of Robber. I mean he is quite a sweet old gentleman and it is really refreshing the way he and his son love one another. Because even if it is unusual for an American to see a French gentleman always kissing his father, I really think it is refreshing and I think that we Americans would be better off if we American fathers and sons would love one another more like Louie and Robber.

So Dorothy and I have quite a lot of delightful hand bags and stockings and handkerchiefs and scarfs and things and some quite cute models of evening gowns that are all covered with imitations of diamonds, only they do not call them “paste” when they are on a dress but they call them “diamonteys” and I really think a girl looks quite cute when she is covered all over with “diamonteys.”

===Chinese===
下午买东西的时候，我看见路易把多萝西拉到角落里，跟她嘀咕了好半天。接着我又看见罗贝尔把她拉到角落里，也嘀咕了好半天。等回到丽兹，多萝西把原因告诉了我。原来路易跟多萝西嘀咕时说的是，要是她肯把钻石头冠从我那儿偷来给他，又不让他爸爸知道，他就给她 1,000 法郎。因为据说弗朗西斯·比克曼夫人铁了心要它，肯为它出大价钱，因为她气得不轻，而她一气到这个份上，就是个一根筋的女人了。这么着，要是路易能弄到手又不让他爸爸发现，钱就全归他自己了。据说后来罗贝尔跟多萝西嘀咕时，出 2,000 法郎跟她做了同样的交易，好不让路易发现，钱全归罗贝尔自己。所以我真心觉得，多萝西要是能自己挣点钱，倒也挺好，说不定能让她长点志气。于是明天上午，多萝西就去拿那顶钻石头冠，告诉路易说是她偷的，把它卖给路易。不过她会让他先把钱交出来，然后，正当她要把钻石头冠递过去的时候，我就踱进去，说：「哟，我的钻石头冠在这儿呢。我到处找它。」这样我就能把它收回来。然后她再告诉他，那 1,000 法郎他不妨照留着，因为下午她还会再替他偷一回。到了下午，她就把它卖给罗贝尔，而且我真心觉得，这一回我们不妨让罗贝尔留下它。因为我挺喜欢罗贝尔的。我是说，他是位相当可爱的老绅士，他跟他儿子相亲相爱的样子，实在叫人心里舒坦。因为尽管美国人看到一位法国绅士老是在吻自己的父亲会觉得稀奇，我真心觉得这很清新，而且我想，要是我们美国的父子能像路易和罗贝尔这样彼此相爱，我们美国人会过得更好。

于是多萝西和我有了好些可爱的手提包、长筒袜、手帕、围巾什么的，还有几件怪俏皮的晚礼服新款，上面缀满了假钻石，只不过钻石缀在裙子上的时候，他们不叫「paste」，叫「diamonteys」（水钻），我真心觉得，一个姑娘浑身缀满「水钻」的时候，样子怪俏皮的。

===Original===
May 5th:
So yesterday morning Dorothy sold the imitation of a diamond tiara to Louie. So then we got it back. So in the afternoon we all went out to Versigh. I mean Louie and Robber were quite delighted not to go shopping any more so I suppose that Lady Francis Beekman really thinks that there is a limit to almost everything. So I took Louie for a walk at Versigh so that Dorothy would have a chance to sell it to Robber. So then she sold it to Robber. So then he put it in his pocket. But when we were coming home I got to thinking things over and I really got to thinking that an imitation of a diamond tiara was quite a good thing to have after all. I mean especially if a girl goes around a lot in Paris, with admirers who are of the French extraction. And after all, I really do not think a girl ought to encouradge Robber to steal something from two American girls who are all alone in Paris and have no gentleman to protect them. So I asked Dorothy which pocket Robber put it in, so I sat next to him in the automobile coming home and I took it out.

===Chinese===
5月5日：
于是昨天上午，多萝西把那顶假钻头冠卖给了路易。然后我们又把它弄了回来。下午我们全体去了凡尔赛（Versigh）。我是说，路易和罗贝尔不必再带我们买东西，高兴得很，所以我估计弗朗西斯·比克曼夫人真该明白，差不多什么事都有个限度。在凡尔赛，我拉着路易去散步，好让多萝西有机会把它卖给罗贝尔。后来她果然卖给了罗贝尔。罗贝尔把它装进了口袋。可回家的路上，我把事情前后一想，越想越觉得，一顶假钻头冠到底是个好东西，值得留在身边。我是说，一个姑娘要是在巴黎到处跑，身边又尽是些法国血统的仰慕者，尤其如此。再说，我实在觉得，姑娘不该纵容罗贝尔去偷两个孤身在巴黎、连个保护的绅士都没有的美国姑娘的东西。于是我问多萝西罗贝尔把它放进了哪只口袋，回程的汽车里我就挨着他坐，把它掏了出来。

===Original===
So we were in quite a quaint restaurant for dinner when Robber put his hand in his pocket and then he started in to squeal once more. So it seems he had lost something, so he and Louie had one of their regular squealing and shoulder shrugging matches. But Louie told his papa that he did not steal it out of his papa’s pocket. But then Robber started in to cry to think that his son would steal something out of his own papa’s pocket. So after Dorothy and I had had about all we could stand, I told them all about it. I mean I really felt sorry for Robber so I told him not to cry any more because it was nothing but paste after all. So then I showed it to them. So then Louie and Robber looked at Dorothy and I and they really held their breath. So I suppose that most of the girls in Paris do not have such brains as we American girls.

===Chinese===
我们正在一家相当古雅的餐馆吃晚饭，罗贝尔把手伸进口袋，随即又尖叫了起来。原来他丢了东西，于是他跟路易照例来了一场尖叫耸肩大赛。路易对他爸爸说，可不是他从他爸爸口袋里偷的。可罗贝尔又哭了起来，一想到亲生儿子竟会从亲爸爸口袋里偷东西。等多萝西和我实在看够了，我把事情原原本本告诉了他们。我是说，我真替罗贝尔难过，便叫他别再哭了，因为它不过是假钻做的罢了。然后我把它拿出来给他们看。路易和罗贝尔看看多萝西又看看我，当真屏住了呼吸。我估计，巴黎的大多数姑娘，都不像我们美国姑娘这样有头脑。

===Original===
So after it was all over, Louie and Robber seemed to be so depressed that I really felt sorry for them. So I got an idea. So I told them that we would all go out tomorrow to the imitation of a jewelry store and they could buy another imitation of a diamond tiara to give to Lady Francis Beekman and they could get the man in the jewelry store to put on the bill that it was a hand bag and they could charge the bill to Lady Francis Beekman along with the other expenses. Because Lady Francis Beekman had never seen the real diamond tiara anyway. So Dorothy spoke up and Dorothy said that as far as Lady Francis Beekman would know about diamonds, you could nick off a piece of ice and give it to her, only it would melt. So then Robber looked at me and looked at me, and he reached over and kissed me on the forehead in a way that was really full of reverance.

===Chinese===
等这一切都过去，路易和罗贝尔垂头丧气的，我真心可怜他们。于是我想出了个主意。我对他们说，明天我们大家一起上那家卖仿造品的珠宝店去，他们可以再买一顶假钻头冠送给弗朗西斯·比克曼夫人，还可以让珠宝店的人在账单上写成一只手提包，把账单跟其余花销一道记到弗朗西斯·比克曼夫人头上。反正弗朗西斯·比克曼夫人从来没见过真正的钻石头冠。于是多萝西开了腔，说就凭弗朗西斯·比克曼夫人认钻石的本事，你就是从冰上敲下一块来给她都行，只可惜冰会化。于是罗贝尔看了我又看我，探过身来，在我的额头上吻了一下，那一吻真是充满了敬意。

===Original===
So then we had quite a delightful evening. I mean because we all seem to understand one another because, after all, Dorothy and I could really have a platonick friendship with gentlemen like Louie and Robber. I mean there seems to be something common between us, especially when we all get to thinking about Lady Francis Beekman.

So they are going to charge Lady Francis Beekman quite a lot of money when they give her the imitation of a diamond tiara and I told Robber if she seems to complane, to ask her, if she knew that Sir Francis Beekman sent me 10 pounds worth of orchids every day while we were in London. So that would make her so angry that she would be glad to pay almost anything to get the diamond tiara.

So when Lady Francis Beekman pays them all the money, Louie and Robber are going to give us a dinner in our honor at Ciros. So when Mr. Eisman gets here on Saturday, Dorothy and I are going to make Mr. Eisman give Louie and Robber a dinner in their honor at Ciros because of the way they helped us when we were two American girls all alone in Paris and could not even speak the French landguage.

So Louie and Robber asked us to come to a party at their sister’s house today but Dorothy says we had better not go because it is raining and we both have brand new umbrellas that are quite cute and Dorothy says she would not think of leaving a brand new umbrella in a French lady’s hall and it is no fun to hang on to an umbrella all the time you are at a party. So we had better be on the safe side and stay away. So we called up Louie and told him we had a headache but we thanked him for all of his hospitality. Because it is the way all the French people like Louie and Robber are so hospitable to we Americans that really makes Paris so devine.

===Chinese===
于是我们度过了一个非常愉快的夜晚。我是说，因为我们大家好像彼此都心知肚明，因为说到底，多萝西和我是真能跟路易和罗贝尔这样的绅士结成柏拉图式的情谊的。我是说，我们之间好像有着某种共通的东西，尤其是在大家一起想起弗朗西斯·比克曼夫人的时候。

他们把假钻头冠交给弗朗西斯·比克曼夫人时，要狠狠敲她一大笔钱，我还嘱咐罗贝尔，她要是抱怨，就问她知不知道，我们在伦敦那阵子，弗朗西斯·比克曼爵士天天送我价值 10 英镑的兰花。保管气得她宁可出几乎随便什么价钱，也要把钻石头冠弄到手。

等弗朗西斯·比克曼夫人把钱统统付清，路易和罗贝尔要在西罗餐厅（Ciro's）为我们设一场庆功宴。等艾斯曼先生星期六到了，多萝西和我还要让艾斯曼先生为路易和罗贝尔在西罗餐厅设一场庆功宴，谢谢他们在我们两个美国姑娘孤身巴黎、连法国话都不会讲的时候帮的忙。

路易和罗贝尔请我们今天去他们姐妹家参加聚会，可多萝西说我们最好别去，因为正下着雨，而我们俩都有一把怪俏皮的崭新的伞，多萝西说她才不想把一把崭新的伞留在法国太太家的门厅里，而且聚会从头到尾攥着把伞也没意思。所以我们还是稳妥为上，别去了。于是我们打电话给路易，说我们头疼，不过谢谢他的一番好客。因为正是路易和罗贝尔这样的法国人对我们美国人的这份好客，才真正让巴黎变得这么妙极了。

## V The Central of Europe / 五 中欧

===Original===
May 16th:
I really have not written in my diary for quite a long time, because Mr. Eisman arrived in Paris and when Mr. Eisman is in Paris we really do not seem to do practically anything else but the same thing.
I mean we go shopping and we go to a show and we go to Momart and when a girl is always going with Mr. Eisman nothing practically happens. And I did not even bother to learn any more French because I always seem to think it is better to leave French to those that can not do anything else but talk French. So finally Mr. Eisman seemed to lose quite a lot of interest in all of my shopping. So he heard about a button factory that was for sale quite cheaply in Vienna and as Mr. Eisman is in the button profession, he thought it would be a quite good thing to have a button factory in Vienna so he went to Vienna and he said he did not care if he did not ever see the rue de la Paix again. So he said if he thought Vienna would be good for a girl’s brains, he would send for Dorothy and I and we could meet him at Vienna and learn something. Because Mr. Eisman really wants me to get educated more than anything else, especially shopping.
So now we have a telegram, and Mr. Eisman says in the telegram for Dorothy and I to take an oriental express because we really ought to see the central of Europe because we American girls have quite a lot to learn in the central of Europe. So Dorothy says if Mr. Eisman wants us to see the central of Europe she bets there is not a rue de la Paix in the whole central of Europe.
So Dorothy and I are going to take an oriental express tomorrow and I really think it is quite unusual for two American girls like I and Dorothy to take an oriental express all alone, because it seems that in the Central of Europe they talk some other kinds of landguages which we do not understand besides French. But I always think that there is nearly always some gentleman who will protect two American girls like I and Dorothy who are all alone and who are traveling in the Central of Europe to get educated.

===Chinese===
5月16日：
我真是好长时间没写日记了，因为艾斯曼先生到巴黎来了，而艾斯曼先生在巴黎的时候，我们实在简直除了老一套，别的什么都不干。
我是说，我们去买东西，我们去看戏，我们去蒙马特（Momart），一个姑娘要是老跟着艾斯曼先生跑，简直什么事也不会发生。我连法文也懒得再多学了，因为我总好像觉得，法文还是留给那些除了讲法文什么都不会干的人去讲比较好。于是到后来，艾斯曼先生对我买东西这件事好像失去了不少兴致。他打听到维也纳有一家纽扣工厂贱价出售，艾斯曼先生既然是干纽扣这一行的，就想到在维也纳有一家纽扣工厂倒也是桩相当不错的事，于是他去了维也纳，还说他才不在乎这辈子再看不见和平大街（rue de la Paix）呢。他说，要是他觉得维也纳对一个姑娘的头脑有好处，他就把多萝西和我接过去，我们在维也纳跟他碰头，也长长学问。因为艾斯曼先生一心想要的，就是我受教育，这比什么都要紧，比买东西尤其要紧。
于是现在我们收到了电报，艾斯曼先生在电报里叫多萝西和我去坐东方快车，因为我们实在该看一看中欧，因为美国姑娘在中欧实在有好多东西要学。多萝西说，要是艾斯曼先生想让我们看中欧，她敢打赌整个中欧连一条和平大街都没有。
于是多萝西和我明天就要去坐东方快车了，我实在觉得，像我和多萝西这样的两个美国姑娘，单独去坐东方快车，相当稀奇，因为据说在中欧，他们除了法文，还讲好些别的我们听不懂的语言。不过我一向觉得，几乎总会有哪位绅士，来保护像我和多萝西这样孤身在中欧旅行、为受教育而来的美国姑娘。

===Original===
May 17th:
So now we are on an oriental express and everything seems to be quite unusual. I mean Dorothy and I got up this morning and we looked out of the window of our compartment and it was really quite unusual. Because it was farms, and we saw quite a lot of girls who seemed to be putting small size hay stacks onto large size hay stacks while their husbands seemed to sit at a table under quite a shady tree and drink beer. Or else their husbands seemed to sit on a fence and smoke their pipe and watch them. So Dorothy and I looked at two girls who seemed to be ploughing up all of the ground with only the aid of a cow and Dorothy said, “I think we girls have gone one step to far away from New York, because it begins to look to me as if the Central of Europe is no country for we girls.” So we both became quite worried. I mean I became quite depressed because if this is what Mr. Eisman thinks we American girls ought to learn I really think it is quite depressing. So I do not think we care to meet any gentlemen who have been born and raised in the Central of Europe. I mean the more I travel and the more I seem to see other gentlemen the more I seem to think of American gentlemen.
So now I am going to get dressed and go to the dining car and look for some American gentleman and hold a conversation, because I really feel so depressed. I mean Dorothy keeps trying to depress me because she keeps saying that I will probably end up in a farm in the Central of Europe doing a sister act with a plough. Because Dorothy’s jokes are really very unrefined and I think that I will feel much better if I go to the dining car and have some luncheon.

===Chinese===
5月17日：
这会儿我们坐上东方快车了，样样事情看来都相当稀奇。我是说，多萝西和我今天早上起了床，从我们包厢的窗户往外看，那光景真是相当稀奇。因为到处是农田，我们看见好些姑娘，好像在把小号的干草垛往大号的干草垛上堆，她们的丈夫呢，好像坐在一棵相当阴凉的树底下的桌子边喝啤酒。要不然就是她们的丈夫好像骑坐在栅栏上，抽着烟斗看她们干活。于是多萝西和我望着两个姑娘，她们好像只凭一头牛帮忙，就把整块地犁了，多萝西说："我看咱们姑娘们离开纽约走得实在太远一步了，因为照我看，中欧这地方实在不是咱们姑娘待的国度。"于是我们俩都担心起来。我是说，我变得相当丧气，因为要是这就是艾斯曼先生认为我们美国姑娘该学的东西，我实在觉得这事相当让人丧气。所以我们不想结识任何生在中欧、长在中欧的绅士了。我是说，我旅行得越多、见到的别国绅士越多，我就越是想念美国绅士。
这会儿我要去换衣服，到餐车去，找一位美国绅士聊聊天，因为我实在觉得太丧气了。我是说，多萝西一个劲儿地想叫我更丧气，因为她不停地说，我多半到头来要落到中欧的一座农场上，扶着犁跟人搭伙演「姐妹档」。因为多萝西的笑话实在非常不文雅，我想我要是去餐车吃顿午饭，心里会好受得多。

===Original===
Well I went to the dining car and I met a gentleman who was quite a delightful American gentleman, I mean it was quite a co-instance, because we girls have always heard about Henry Spoffard and it was really nobody else but the famous Henry Spoffard, who is the famous Spoffard family, who is a very very fine old family who is very very wealthy. I mean Mr. Spoffard is one of the most famous familys in New York and he is not like most gentlemen who are wealthy, but he works all of the time for the good of the others. I mean he is the gentleman who always gets his picture in all of the newspapers because he is always senshuring all of the plays that are not good for peoples morals. And all of we girls remember the time when he was in the Ritz for luncheon and he met a gentleman friend of his and the gentleman friend had Peggy Hopkins Joyce to luncheon and he introduced Peggy Hopkins Joyce to Mr. Spoffard and Mr. Spoffard turned on his heels and walked away. Because Mr. Spoffard is a very very famous Prespyterian and he is really much to Prespyterian to meet Peggy Hopkins Joyce. I mean it is unusual to see a gentleman who is such a young gentleman as Mr. Spoffard be so Prespyterian, because when most gentlemen are 35 years of age their minds nearly always seem to be on something else.
So when I saw no one else but the famous Mr. Spoffard I really became quite thrilled. Because all of we girls have tried very hard to have an introduction to Henry Spoffard and it was quite unusual to be shut up on a train in the Central of Europe with him. So I thought it would be quite unusual for a girl like I to have a friendship with a gentleman like Mr. Spoffard, who really does not even look at a girl unless she at least looks like a Prespyterian. And I mean our family in Little Rock were really not so Prespyterians.

===Chinese===
哎，我到餐车去了，遇见一位先生，是位非常讨人喜欢的美国绅士，我是说，真是好巧不巧，因为我们姑娘们早就听说过亨利·斯波福德（Henry Spoffard）这个人，而这一位不是别人，正是大名鼎鼎的亨利·斯波福德，出身鼎鼎大名的斯波福德家族，那是非常非常古老又非常非常有钱的名门。我是说，斯波福德先生一家是纽约最有名望的人家之一，他跟大多数有钱的绅士不一样，他一天到晚都在为别人的好处操劳。我是说，他这位绅士的照片老是登在所有的报纸上，因为他老是审查那些对人们的道德没好处的戏。我们姑娘们全都还记得那一回，他在丽兹酒店吃午饭，碰上他的一位绅士朋友，那位绅士朋友正请佩吉·霍普金斯·乔伊斯（Peggy Hopkins Joyce）吃午饭，把她引见给斯波福德先生，斯波福德先生当场转过脚跟就走。因为斯波福德先生是一位非常非常有名的长老会教徒，他实在长老会得过了头，不肯见佩吉·霍普金斯·乔伊斯。我是说，像斯波福德先生这样年轻的绅士居然这么长老会，实在少见，因为大多数绅士到了 35 岁，脑子十有八九都放在别的什么上头。
所以当我看见不是别人、正是大名鼎鼎的斯波福德先生时，我真是激动得很。因为我们姑娘们全都千方百计想让人引见亨利·斯波福德，如今竟和他一同关在中欧的一列火车上，实在稀奇。我想，像我这样的姑娘，能跟斯波福德先生这样的绅士交上朋友，实在相当稀奇，因为他这样的绅士，连看都不看姑娘一眼，除非那姑娘至少长得像个长老会教徒。我是说，我们家在小石城，实在算不上那么长老会。

===Original===
So I thought I would sit at his table. So then I had to ask him about all of the money because all of the money they use in the Central of Europe has not even got so much sense to it as the kind of franks they use in Paris. Because it seems to be called kronens and it seems to take quite a lot of them because it takes 50,000 of them to even buy a small size package of cigarettes and Dorothy says if the cigarettes had tobacco in them, we couldn’t lift enough kronens over a counter to pay for a package. So this morning Dorothy and I asked the porter to bring us a bottle of champagne and we really did not know what to give him for a tip. So Dorothy said for me to take one of the things called a one million kronens and she would take one of them called a one million kronens and I would give him mine first and if he gave me quite a dirty look, she would give him hers. So after we paid for the bottle of champagne I gave him my one million kronens and before we could do anything else he started in to grabbing my hand and kissing my hand and getting down on his knees. So we finally had to push him right out of the compartment. So one million kronens seemed to be enough. So I told Mr. Spoffard how we did not know what to give the porter when he brought us our bottle of minral water. So then I asked him to tell me all about all of the money because I told him I always seem to think that a penny earned was a penny saved. So it really was quite unusual because Mr. Spoffard said that that was his favorite motto.

===Chinese===
于是我想，我就坐到他的桌边去吧。接着我不得不向他请教钱的事，因为中欧用的那些钱，实在连巴黎人用的那种法郎都不如，一点道理都讲不通。因为那钱好像叫「克朗」（kronens），好像得用上好大一把，因为买一小包香烟也得用上 50,000 克朗，多萝西说，要是那香烟里真有烟丝，咱们就是把克朗搬过柜台去，也搬不动买一包的数。于是今天早上，多萝西和我叫列车员给我们送一瓶香槟来，我们实在不知道该给他多少小费。多萝西说，让我拿一张那种叫一百万克朗的票子，她也拿一张叫一百万克朗的，我先把我的给他，他要是朝我一脸嫌弃，她再把她的给他。于是付过那瓶香槟的钱之后，我把一百万克朗给了他，还没等我们再干什么，他就动手来抓我的手、亲我的手，还扑通跪了下去。我们最后只好把他推出包厢去。这么看，一百万克朗似乎是够了。我告诉斯波福德先生，列车员给我们送一瓶矿泉水来的时候，我们实在不知道该给他多少小费。接着我请他把这些钱的事统统讲给我听，因为我对他说，我总好像觉得，挣来的一分钱就是省下的一分钱。这真是相当稀奇，因为斯波福德先生说，那正是他最喜欢的座右铭。

===Original===
So then we got to talking quite a lot and I told him that I was traveling to get educated and I told him I had a girl with me who I was trying to reform because I thought if she would put her mind more on getting educated, she would get more reformed. Because after all Mr. Spoffard will have to meet Dorothy sooner or later and he might wonder what a refined girl like I was doing with a girl like Dorothy. So Mr. Spoffard really became quite intreeged. Because Mr. Spoffard loves to reform people and he loves to senshure everything and he really came over to Europe to look at all the things that Americans come over to Europe to look at, when they really should not look at them but they should look at all of the museums instead. Because if that is all we Americans come to Europe to look at, we should stay home and look at America first. So Mr. Spoffard spends all of his time looking at things that spoil peoples morals. So Mr. Spoffard really must have very very strong morals or else all the things that spoil other peoples morals would spoil his morals. But they do not seem to spoil Mr. Spoffards morals and I really think it is wonderful to have such strong morals. So I told Mr. Spoffard that I thought that civilization is not what it ought to be and we really ought to have something else to take its place.
So Mr. Spoffard said that he would come to call on Dorothy and I in our compartment this afternoon and we would talk it all over, if his mother does not seem to need him in her compartment. Because Mr. Spoffards mother always travels with Mr. Spoffard and he never does anything unless he tells his mother all about it, and asks his mother if he ought to. So he told me that that is the reason he has never got married, because his mother does not think that all of the flappers we seem to have nowadays are what a young man ought to marry when a young man is full of so many morals as Mr. Spoffard seems to be full of. So I told Mr. Spoffard that I really felt just like his mother feels about all of the flappers because I am an old fashioned girl.
So then I got to worrying about Dorothy quite a lot because Dorothy is really not so old-fashioned and she might say something in front of Mr. Spoffard that might make Mr. Spoffard wonder what such an old-fashioned girl as I was doing with such a girl as Dorothy. So I told him how I was having quite a hard time reforming Dorothy and I would like to have him meet Dorothy so he could tell me if he really thinks I am wasting quite a lot of time trying to reform a girl like Dorothy. So then he had to go to his mother. So I really hope that Dorothy will act more reformed than she usually acts in front of Mr. Spoffard.

===Chinese===
于是我们聊了好多好多，我告诉他，我是出来旅行受教育的，还告诉他我随身带着一个姑娘，我正想法子改造她，因为我想，她要是把心思多放在受教育上，就能改造得更好。因为说到底，斯波福德先生迟早要见多萝西的，他也许会纳闷，像我这样文雅的姑娘，怎么跟多萝西这样的姑娘混在一起。于是斯波福德先生真是相当来劲。因为斯波福德先生最爱改造人，最爱审查一切，他到欧洲来，正是为了看一看美国人到欧洲来看的那些东西——其实他们实在不该看那些，该看的是各家博物馆。因为要是我们美国人到欧洲来就为了看那些，那还不如待在家里先看美国。于是斯波福德先生把全部时间都用来视察那些败坏人们道德的东西。这么看，斯波福德先生的道德一定非常非常坚强，要不然，那些败坏别人道德的东西，早把他的道德也败坏了。可它们好像并没有败坏斯波福德先生的道德，我觉得有这么坚强的道德，实在了不起。于是我对斯波福德先生说，我觉得文明这东西实在不像它该有的样子，我们实在该拿点别的什么来取而代之。
于是斯波福德先生说，今天下午他要到我们包厢来拜访多萝西和我，咱们把这一切好好谈一谈——只要他那会儿好像用不着去他母亲的包厢。因为斯波福德先生的母亲总是跟着斯波福德先生一起旅行，他做什么事都得先一五一十告诉他母亲，问他母亲该不该做。于是他告诉我，这就是他一直没结婚的原因，因为他母亲认为，如今我们到处见的这些轻佻姑娘，不是一个满肚子道德的年轻人该娶的——斯波福德先生满肚子装的就是道德。于是我对斯波福德先生说，他母亲对这些轻佻姑娘的看法，我真是再有同感不过了，因为我是个老派姑娘。
接着我又为多萝西担心得不行，因为多萝西实在算不上老派，她当着斯波福德先生的面也许会说些什么，让他纳闷，像我这么老派的姑娘，怎么跟多萝西这样的姑娘混在一起。于是我告诉他，我改造多萝西改造得好辛苦，我想让他见见多萝西，好让他告诉我，他是不是真觉得我在多萝西这样的姑娘身上浪费了大把时间。然后他就得去他母亲那儿了。我真心希望多萝西在斯波福德先生面前，能表现得比平常更有改造过的样子。

===Original===
Well Mr. Spoffard just left our compartment so he really came to pay a call on us after all. So Mr. Spoffard told us all about his mother and I was really very very intreeged because if Mr. Spoffard and I become friendly he is the kind of a gentleman that always wants a girl to meet his mother. I mean if a girl gets to know what kind of a mother a gentlemans mother is like, she really knows more what kind of a conversation to use on a gentleman’s mother when she meets her. Because a girl like I is really always on the verge of meeting gentlemen’s mothers. But such an unrefined girl as Dorothy is really not the kind of a girl that ever meets gentlemens mothers.

===Chinese===
哎，斯波福德先生刚刚离开我们的包厢，可见他到底还是来拜访我们了。斯波福德先生把他母亲的事给我们讲了讲，我真是非常非常来劲，因为要是斯波福德先生和我要好起来，他是那种总要让姑娘见母亲的绅士。我是说，一个姑娘要是先弄明白了一位绅士的母亲是哪一种母亲，等真见面的时候，就更有数该跟一位绅士的母亲用什么样的谈话了。因为像我这样的姑娘，实在总是处在快要见到绅士们母亲的关口上。可是多萝西这样不文雅的姑娘，实在不是那种能见到绅士们母亲的姑娘。

===Original===
So Mr. Spoffard says his mother has to have him take care of her quite a lot. Because Mr. Spoffards mothers brains have never really been so strong. Because it seems his mother came from such a very fine old family that even when she was quite a small size child she had to be sent to a school that was a special school for people of very fine old familys who had to have things very easy on their brain. So she still has to have things very easy on her brain, so she has a girl who is called her companion who goes with her everywhere who is called Miss Chapman. Because Mr. Spoffard says that there is always something new going on in the world which they did not get a chance to tell her about at the school. So now Miss Chapman keeps telling her instead. Because how would she know what to think about such a new thing as a radio, for instance, if she did not have Miss Chapman to tell her what it was, for instance. So Dorothy spoke up and Dorothy said, “What a responsibility that girl has got on her shoulders. For instance, what if Miss Chapman told her a radio was something to build a fire in, and she would get cold some day and stuff it full of papers and light it.” But Mr. Spoffard told Dorothy that Miss Chapman would never make such a mistake. Because he said that Miss Chapman came from a very very fine old family herself and she really had a fine brain. So Dorothy said, “If she really has got such a fine brain I bet her fine old family once had an ice man who could not be trusted.” So Mr. Spoffard and I did not pay any more attention to Dorothy because Dorothy really does not know how to hold a conversation.

===Chinese===
于是斯波福德先生说，他母亲得他照应的时候很多。因为斯波福德先生母亲的头脑实在一直不那么坚强。因为据说他母亲出身非常非常古老的名门，所以她还是个小不点儿的年纪，就得上一所特别的学校，专门收名门世家、头脑得省着用的子弟。所以她到如今干什么都得省着头脑用，于是她有个姑娘，人称她的「女伴」，走到哪儿跟到哪儿，叫查普曼小姐（Miss Chapman）。因为斯波福德先生说，世界上总有新鲜事发生，都是当年学校里没来得及讲给她听的。如今就由查普曼小姐讲给她听。因为比如说，收音机这么个新鲜玩意儿，要是没有查普曼小姐讲给她听那是什么，她怎么会知道该拿它怎么办呢，比如说。于是多萝西插嘴了，多萝西说："这姑娘肩上的担子可真不轻。比如说，查普曼小姐要是告诉她收音机是用来生火的，哪天她冷了，把它塞满报纸，一点火，那可怎么好。"可斯波福德先生对多萝西说，查普曼小姐绝不会犯这种错。因为他说查普曼小姐自己也出身非常非常古老的名门，头脑实在好得很。于是多萝西说："她头脑要是真那么好，我敢打赌她那古老的名门，早先一定收留过一位靠不住的送冰人。"于是斯波福德先生和我不再理会多萝西，因为多萝西实在不懂得怎么谈话。

===Original===
So then I and Mr. Spoffard held a conversation all about morals and Mr. Spoffard says he really thinks the future of everything is between the hands of Mr. Blank the district attorney who is the famous district attorney who is closing up all of the places in New York where they sell all of the liquor. So Mr. Spoffard said that a few months ago, when Mr. Blank decided he would try to get the job to be the district attorney, he put 1,000 dollars worth of liquor down his sink. So now Mr. Blank says that everybody else has got to put it down their sink. So Dorothy spoke up, and Dorothy said, “If he poured 1,000 dollars worth down his sink to get himself one million dollars worth of publicity and a good job—when we pour it down our sink, what do we get?” But Mr. Spoffard is to brainy a gentleman to answer any such a foolish question. So he gave Dorothy a look that was full of dignity and he said he would have to go back to his Mother. So I was really quite angry at Dorothy. So I followed Mr. Spoffard down the hall of the railway train and I asked Mr. Spoffard if he thought I was wasting quite a lot of time reforming a girl like Dorothy. So Mr. Spoffard thinks I am, because he really thinks a girl like Dorothy will never have any reverance. So I told Mr. Spoffard I had wasted so much time on Dorothy it would really break my heart to be a failure. So then I had tears in my eyes. So Mr. Spoffard is really very very sympathetic because when he saw that I did not have any handkerchief, he took his own handkerchief and he dried up all of my tears. So then he said he would help me with Dorothy quite a lot and get her mind to running on things that are more educational.

===Chinese===
