于是我和斯波福德先生大谈其道德，斯波福德先生说，他实在觉得万事的前途都攥在布兰克先生（Mr. Blank）手里，那位大名鼎鼎的地方检察官，正在查封纽约所有卖酒的地方。斯波福德先生说，几个月以前，布兰克先生决定争取地方检察官这个位子的时候，把值 1,000 美元的酒倒进了自家水池。如今布兰克先生说，别人也都得把酒倒进自家水池。于是多萝西又插嘴了，多萝西说："他往水池里倒 1,000 美元的酒，给自己换来 100 万美元的名声外加一个好差事——那我们往水池里倒的时候，换着什么？"可斯波福德先生是位头脑太好使的绅士，不屑回答这种傻问题。他满是尊严地瞥了多萝西一眼，说他得回他母亲那儿去了。于是我真是对多萝西相当生气。我跟着斯波福德先生走到火车的过道里，问斯波福德先生，他是不是觉得我在改造多萝西这样的姑娘上浪费了大把时间。斯波福德先生觉得是，因为他实在觉得多萝西这样的姑娘永远学不会敬畏。于是我对斯波福德先生说，我在多萝西身上花了那么多工夫，要是落个失败，我的心真要碎了。说着我的眼睛里就有了眼泪。斯波福德先生实在非常非常富于同情心，因为他看见我一块手绢也没有，就掏出他自己的手绢，把我的眼泪统统揩干了。接着他说，他要帮我多多管教多萝西，让她的心思跑到更有教育意义的事情上去。

===Original===
So then he said he thought that we ought to get off the train at a place called Munich because it was very full of art, which they call *kunst* in Munich, which is very, very educational. So he said he and Dorothy and I would get off of the train in Munich because he could send his mother right on to Vienna with Miss Chapman, because every place always seems to look alike to his mother anyway. So we are all going to get off the train at Munich and I can send Mr. Eisman a telegram when nobody is looking. Because I really do not think I will tell Mr. Spoffard about Mr. Eisman, because, after all, their religions are different and when two gentlemen have such different religions they do not seem to have so much to get congeneal about. So I can telegraph Mr. Eisman that Dorothy and I thought we would get off the train at Munich to look at all of the art.
So then I went back to Dorothy and I told Dorothy if she did not have anything to say in the future to not say it. Because even if Mr. Spoffard is a fine old family and even if he is very Prespyterian, I and he could really be friendly after all and talk together quite a lot. I mean Mr. Spoffard likes to talk about himself quite a lot, so I said to Dorothy it really shows that, after all, he is just like any other gentleman. But Dorothy said she would demand more proof than that. So Dorothy says she thinks that maybe I might become quite friendly with Mr. Spoffard and especially with his mother because she thinks his mother and I have quite a lot that is common, but she says, if I ever bump into Miss Chapman, she thinks I will come to a kropper because Dorothy saw Miss Chapman when she was at luncheon and Dorothy says Miss Chapman is the kind of a girl that wears a collar and a tie even when she is not on horseback. And Dorothy said it was the look that Miss Chapman gave her at luncheon that really gave her the idea about the ice man. So Dorothy says she thinks Miss Chapman has got 3 thirds of the brains of that trio of Geegans, because Geegans is the slang word that Dorothy has thought up to use on people who are society people. Because Dorothy says she thinks any gentleman with Mr. Spoffards brains had ought to spend his time putting nickels into an electric piano, but I did not even bother to talk back at such a girl as Dorothy. So now we must get ready to get off the train when the train gets to Munich so that we can look at all of the kunst in Munich.

===Chinese===
接着他说，他觉得我们该在一个叫慕尼黑的地方下火车，因为那里满城都是艺术，慕尼黑人管艺术叫 kunst，非常非常有教育意义。于是他说，他和多萝西和我要在慕尼黑下车，因为他母亲可以由查普曼小姐陪着直接送去维也纳，反正各地在他母亲眼里长得总是一个样。于是我们都要在慕尼黑下车了，我还能趁人不注意的时候给艾斯曼先生拍电报。因为我实在不打算把艾斯曼先生的事告诉斯波福德先生，因为说到底，他们俩的宗教不同，两位绅士的宗教要是这么不同，彼此实在合不大来。于是我可以打电报告诉艾斯曼先生，多萝西和我打算在慕尼黑下车，看看所有的艺术。
接着我回到多萝西那儿，我对多萝西说，她往后要是没什么可说的，就别说。因为斯波福德先生就算出身古老名门，就算他非常长老会，我和他到底还是能要好起来，还能在一起聊好多好多。我是说，斯波福德先生挺爱谈论他自己，于是我对多萝西说，这实在说明，他到底跟别的绅士一个样。可多萝西说，她还得要更多的证据。于是多萝西说，她觉得我也许真能跟斯波福德先生要好起来，尤其能跟他母亲要好，因为他母亲和我有相当多共同之处；不过她说，我要是撞上查普曼小姐，准要摔个大跟头，因为多萝西吃午饭的时候见过查普曼小姐，多萝西说查普曼小姐就是那种姑娘，人不在马背上也要戴硬领打领带。多萝西还说，就是查普曼小姐午饭时看她的那一眼，让她想起了送冰人那档子事。于是多萝西说，她觉得查普曼小姐占着那一窝「吉根」（Geegans）三分之三的头脑——Geegans 是多萝西自己发明的俚语，专用来叫那些上流社会的人。因为多萝西还说，她觉得任何一位有斯波福德先生这样头脑的绅士，就该把时间花在往电风琴里塞五分镍币上，不过对多萝西这样的姑娘，我连回嘴都懒得回。这会儿我们得收拾好，等火车到慕尼黑就下车，好把慕尼黑所有的 kunst 都看个遍。

===Original===
May 19th:
Well yesterday Mr. Spoffard and I and Dorothy got off the train at Munich to see all of the kunst in Munich, but you only call it Munich when you are on the train because as soon as you get off of the train they seem to call it Munchen. So you really would know that Munchen was full of kunst because in case you would not know it, they have painted the word “kunst” in large size black letters on everything in Munchen, and you can not even see a boot black’s stand in Munchen that is not full of kunst.
So Mr. Spoffard said that we really ought to go to the theater in Munchen because even the theater in Munchen was full of kunst. So we looked at all of the bills of all of the theaters, with the aid of quite an intelectual hotel clerk who seemed to be able to read it and tell us what it said, because it really meant nothing to us. So it seems they were playing Kiki in Munchen, so I said, let us go and see Kiki because we have seen Lenore Ulric in New York and we would really know what it is all about even if they do not seem to talk the English landguage. So then we went to the Kunst theater. So it seems that Munchen is practically full of Germans and the lobby of the Kunst theater was really full of Germans who stand in the lobby and drink beer and eat quite a lot of Bermudian onions and garlick sausage and hard boiled eggs and beer before all of the acts. So I really had to ask Mr. Spoffard if he thought we had come to the right theatre because the lobby seemed to smell such a lot. I mean when the smell of beer gets to be anteek it gets to smell quite a lot. But Mr. Spoffard seemed to think that the lobby of the Kunst theatre did not smell any worse than all of the other places in Munich. So then Dorothy spoke up and Dorothy said “You can say what you want about the Germans being full of ‘kunst,’ but what they are really full of is delicatessen.”
So then we went into the Kunst theater. But the Kunst theater does not seem to smell so good as the lobby of the Kunst theater. And the Kunst theater seems to be decorated with quite a lot of what tripe would look like if it was pasted on the wall and gilded. Only you could not really see the gilding because it was covered with quite a lot of dust. So Dorothy looked around and Dorothy said, if this is “kunst,” the art center of the world is Union Hill New Jersey.
So then they started in to playing Kiki but it seems that it was not the same kind of a Kiki that we have in America, because it seemed to be all about a family of large size German people who seemed to keep getting in each others ways. I mean when a stage is completely full of 2 or 3 German people who are quite large size, they really cannot help it if they seem to get in each others ways. So then Dorothy got to talking with a young gentleman who seemed to be a German gentleman who sat back of her, who she thought was applauding. But what he was really doing was he was cracking a hard boiled egg on the back of her chair. So he talked English with quite an accent that seemed to be quite a German accent. So Dorothy asked him if Kiki had come out on the stage yet. So he said no, but she was really a beautiful german actress who came clear from Berlin and he said we should really wait until she came out, even if we did not seem to understand it. So finally she came out. I mean we knew it was her because Dorothy’s German gentleman friend nudged Dorothy with a sausage. So we looked at her, and we looked at her and Dorothy said, “If Schuman Heinke still has a grandmother, we have dug her up in Munchen.” So we did not bother to see any more of Kiki because Dorothy said she would really have to know more about the foundations of that building before she would risk our lives to see Kiki do that famous scene where she faints in the last act. Because Dorothy said, if the foundations of that building were as anteek as the smell, there was going to be a catasterophy when Kiki hit the floor. So even Mr. Spoffard was quite discouradged, but he was really glad because he said he was 100 percent of an American and it served the Germans right for starting such a war against all we Americans.

===Chinese===
5月19日：
哎，昨天斯波福德先生和我、多萝西在慕尼黑下了车，去看慕尼黑所有的 kunst，不过你只有在火车上才管它叫慕尼黑（Munich），因为一下火车，他们好像就管它叫明兴（Munchen）了。所以你真会知道明兴满城都是 kunst，因为怕你不知道，他们把 kunst 这个词用大号黑漆刷在了明兴的每一样东西上，明兴城里连一个擦鞋摊，你都不可能看见不是满摊 kunst 的。
于是斯波福德先生说，我们实在该去明兴的剧院看戏，因为连明兴的剧院都满是 kunst。于是我们把各家剧院的戏报看了个遍——多亏一位相当有学问的旅馆职员，他好像认得那些字，念给我们听那上头说的是什么，因为那对我们实在等于天书。原来他们明兴正演着《琪琪》（Kiki），于是我说，咱们去看《琪琪》吧，因为我们在纽约看过莉诺·乌尔里克（Lenore Ulric）演的，就算他们好像不讲英语，咱们也能真看明白是怎么回事。于是我们去了艺术剧院。原来明兴简直满城都是德国人，艺术剧院的前厅实在挤满了德国人，他们在前厅里站着喝啤酒，每一幕开演之前，还吃好多好多百慕大洋葱、蒜肠、煮鸡蛋，灌啤酒。我实在只好问斯波福德先生，咱们是不是走错了剧院，因为那前厅的味儿实在冲得很。我是说，啤酒的气味一变得古董起来，那味儿可就相当冲了。不过斯波福德先生好像觉得，艺术剧院的前厅并不比慕尼黑其他任何地方更难闻。于是多萝西又开口了，多萝西说："德国人满肚子『kunst』，你爱怎么说随你，可他们真正满肚子的是熟食。"
于是我们走进艺术剧院。可艺术剧院里面，好像还不如它的前厅好闻。艺术剧院的墙上装饰着好多东西，活像把牛肚贴在墙上又镀了金。只是那镀金你实在看不大出来，因为上头落了厚厚一层灰。于是多萝西四下看了看，说：要是这就叫「kunst」，那世界艺术中心就是新泽西州的联合山（Union Hill）。
于是他们开演《琪琪》了，可那好像跟我们美国的《琪琪》不是一路货，因为演的似乎全是一大家子大块头的德国人，好像老互相碍手碍脚。我是说，台上挤满两三个大块头的德国人，他们要是互相碍起手碍起脚来，实在怪不得他们。接着多萝西跟坐在她后排的一位年轻先生聊了起来，那好像是个德国绅士，她起先还当人家在鼓掌。可人家真正干的，是在她椅背上磕煮鸡蛋。那人讲英语带着相当重的口音，好像是相当德国的口音。多萝西问他琪琪出台没有。他说还没有，又说她实在是一位漂亮的德国女演员，大老远从柏林赶来的，还说我们实在该等她出台，哪怕我们好像听不懂。她总算出台了。我是说，我们知道是她，因为多萝西的德国绅士朋友拿一根香肠捅了捅多萝西。于是我们瞧着她，瞧了又瞧，多萝西说："舒曼-海因克（Schuman Heinke）要是还有祖母在世，咱们算是在明兴把她老人家挖出来了。"于是我们懒得再看《琪琪》了，因为多萝西说，她非得先弄清楚那栋楼的地基，才肯拿咱们的性命冒险，去看琪琪演最后一幕晕倒的那场名戏。因为多萝西说，要是那楼的地基跟那股味儿一样古董，琪琪一倒地，那儿就要出一场大灾难。于是连斯波福德先生都相当泄气，不过他心里倒是高兴，因为他说自己是 100% 的美国人，德国人当初对我们全体美国人发动那样一场战争，这就叫他们活该。

===Original===
May 20th:
Well today Mr. Spoffard is going to take me all around to all of the museums in Munchen, which are full of kunst that I really ought to look at, but Dorothy said she had been punished for all of her sins last night, so now she is going to begin life all over again by going out with her German gentleman friend, who is going to take her to a house called the Half Brow house which is the worlds largest size of a Beer Hall. So Dorothy said I could be a high brow and get full of kunst, but she is satisfide to be a Half brow and get full of beer. But Dorothy will really never be full of anything else but unrefinement.

===Chinese===
5月20日：
哎，今天斯波福德先生要带我逛遍明兴所有的博物馆，那里头满是我实在该看的 kunst，可多萝西说，她昨晚已经把一辈子的罪都受完了，如今她要跟她那位德国绅士朋友出去，重新做人，那位朋友要带她去一栋叫「海布劳府」（Half Brow house）的楼，那是世界上最大号的啤酒馆。于是多萝西说，我可以去当个「高眉」，装一肚子 kunst；她呢，甘心当个「半眉」（Half Brow），装一肚子啤酒。不过多萝西这肚子啊，实在除了不文雅，别的什么也装不满。

===Original===
May 21st:
Well Mr. Spoffard and I and Dorothy are on the train again and we are all going to Vienna. I mean Mr. Spoffard and I spent one whole day going through all of the museums in Munchen, but I am really not even going to think about it. Because when something terrible happens to me, I always try to be a Christian science and I simply do not even think about it, but I deny that it ever happened even if my feet do seem to hurt quite a lot. So even Dorothy had quite a hard day in Munchen because her German gentleman friend, who is called Rudolf, came for her at 11 o’clock to take her to breakfast. But Dorothy told him that she had had her breakfast. But her gentleman friend said that he had had his first breakfast to, but it was time for his second. So he took Dorothy to the Half Brow house where everybody eats white sausages and pretzels and beer at 11 o’clock. So after they had their white sausages and beer he wanted to take her for a ride but they could only go a few blocks because by then it was time for luncheon. So they ate quite a lot of luncheon and then he bought her a large size box of chocolates that were full of liqueurs, and took her to the matinee. So after the first act Rudolf got hungry and they had to go and stand in the lobby and have some sandwitches and beer. But Dorothy did not enjoy the show very much and so after the second act Rudolf said they would leave because it was time for tea anyway. So after quite a heavy tea, Rudolph asked her to dinner and Dorothy was to overcome to say No. So after dinner they went to a beer garden for beer and pretzels. But finally Dorothy began to come to, and she asked him to take her back to the hotel. So Rudolf said he would, but they had better have a bite to eat first. So today Dorothy really feels just as discouradged as I seem to feel, only Dorothy is not a Christian science and all she can do is suffer.
But in spite of all of my Christian science, I am really beginning to feel quite discouradged about Vienna. I mean Mr. Eisman is in Vienna, and I do not see how I can spend quite a lot of time with Mr. Eisman and quite a lot of time with Mr. Spoffard and keep them from meeting one another. Because Mr. Spoffard might not seem to understand why Mr. Eisman seems to spend quite a lot of money to get me educated. And Dorothy keeps trying to depress me about Miss Chapman because she says she thinks that when Miss Chapman sees I and Mr. Spoffard together she thinks that Miss Chapman will cable for the familys favorite lunacy expert. So I have got to be as full of Christian science as I can and always hope for the best.

===Chinese===
5月21日：
哎，斯波福德先生和我、多萝西又在火车上了，我们全都去维也纳。我是说，斯波福德先生和我花了一整天逛遍明兴所有的博物馆，可我实在连想都不想去想它。因为每当我碰上可怕的事，我总试着当个基督教科学派（Christian science），干脆连想都不去想它，一口咬定它压根儿没发生过，哪怕我的两只脚实在疼得厉害。连多萝西在明兴的日子也不好过，因为她那位德国绅士朋友——他叫鲁道夫（Rudolf）——11 点钟来接她去吃早饭。可多萝西对他说，她已经吃过早饭了。可她那位绅士朋友说，他的头一顿早饭也吃过了，可这会儿到他吃第二顿的时候了。于是他带多萝西去了海布劳府，那儿人人 11 点钟都吃白香肠、脆面包圈，喝啤酒。他们吃过白香肠喝了啤酒之后，他要带她出去兜风，可只兜得了几条街，因为到吃午饭的时候了。于是他们吃了好多好多午饭，然后他给她买了一盒大号的、满是利口酒芯的巧克力，带她去看日场戏。第一幕过后，鲁道夫饿了，他们只好到前厅去站着吃了些三明治，喝了啤酒。可多萝西不大看得进那出戏，于是第二幕过后，鲁道夫说咱们走吧，反正到喝下午茶的时候了。于是喝过一顿相当扎实的下午茶，鲁道夫又请她吃晚饭，多萝西实在招架不住，只好说好。晚饭后他们去了一家啤酒园，喝啤酒，吃脆面包圈。可多萝西总算渐渐回过神来了，她求他送她回旅馆。鲁道夫说好，不过他们最好先随便吃点儿。于是今天多萝西心里的泄气劲儿，实在跟我的一模一样，只不过多萝西不是基督教科学派，她只能干受着。
可尽管我把基督教科学派使了个够，我对维也纳实在渐渐觉得相当泄气。我是说，艾斯曼先生在维也纳，我看不出我怎么能既花大把时间陪艾斯曼先生、又花大把时间陪斯波福德先生，还不叫他们俩撞上。因为斯波福德先生也许不明白，艾斯曼先生为什么肯花那么多钱让我受教育。多萝西还一个劲儿拿查普曼小姐来气我，因为她说她觉得，查普曼小姐一看见我和斯波福德先生在一起，准会打电报去请他们家最爱用的疯病专家。所以我只有尽量把基督教科学派装满全身，永远往最好的那边想。

===Original===
May 25th:
So far everything has really worked out for the best. Because Mr. Eisman is very very busy all day with the button profession, and he tells me to run around with Dorothy all day. So I and Mr. Spoffard run around all day. So then I tell Mr. Spoffard that I really do not care to go to all of the places that you go to at night, but I will go to bed and get ready for tomorrow instead. So then Dorothy and I go to dinner with Mr. Eisman and then we go to a show, and we stay up quite late at a cabaret called the Chapeau Rouge and I am able to keep it all up with the aid of champagne. So if we keep our eye out for Mr. Spoffard and do not all bump into one another when he is out looking at things that we Americans really should not look at, it will all work out for the best. I mean I have even stopped Mr. Spoffard looking at museums because I tell him that I like nature better, and when you look at nature you look at it in a horse and buggy in the park and it is much easier on the feet. So now he is beginning to talk about how he would like me to meet his mother, so everything really seems for the best after all.
But I have quite a hard time with Mr. Eisman at night. I mean at night Mr. Eisman is in quite a state, because every time he makes an engagement about the button factory, it is time for all the gentlemen in Vienna to go to the coffee house and sit. Or else every time he makes an engagement about the button factory, some Viennese gentleman gets the idea to have a picknick and they all put on short pants and bare knees and they all put a feather in their hat, and they all walk to the Tyrol. So it really discouradges Mr. Eisman quite a lot. But if anyone ought to get discouradged I think that I ought to get discouradged because after all when a girl has had no sleep for a week a girl can not help it if she seems to get discouradged.

===Chinese===
5月25日：
到现在为止，一切实在都往最好的方向走了。因为艾斯曼先生整天忙着纽扣那一行，叫我整天带着多萝西到处跑。于是我和斯波福德先生就整天到处跑。回头我对斯波福德先生说，你们晚上去的那些地方我实在不想去，我宁可上床睡觉，好歇出精神来对付明天。然后多萝西和我跟艾斯曼先生去吃晚饭，再去看戏，还在一家叫「红帽」（Chapeau Rouge）的卡巴莱待到好晚，全靠香槟我才撑得住。这么着，只要咱们眼睛放亮些，别趁他出去看那些我们美国人实在不该看的东西的时候，跟他们大家撞个正着，一切就都会往最好的方向走。我是说，我连博物馆都劝得斯波福德先生不看了，因为我对他说，我更喜欢大自然，看大自然是坐在公园的马车里看的，脚上省力得多。于是如今他开始念叨，说他想让我见见他的母亲了，这么看一切实在到底都往最好的方向走。
可是晚上我陪艾斯曼先生实在辛苦。我是说，艾斯曼先生一到晚上就没了辙，因为每回他约好谈纽扣厂的事，就到了维也纳全体绅士上咖啡馆去坐着的时辰。要不然就是每回他约好谈纽扣厂的事，哪位维也纳绅士就起了兴要办野餐，于是大家全体穿上短裤，露出膝盖，帽子上插根羽毛，全体步行去蒂罗尔（Tyrol）。这实在让艾斯曼先生相当泄气。可要论谁该泄气，我看该泄气的是我，因为说到底，一个姑娘一个礼拜没合过眼，她要是显得有点泄气，实在怪不得她。

===Original===
May 27th:
Well finaly I broke down and Mr. Spoffard said that he thought a little girl like I, who was trying to reform the whole world was trying to do to much, especially beginning on a girl like Dorothy. So he said there was a famous doctor in Vienna called Dr. Froyd who could stop all of my worrying because he does not give a girl medicine but he talks you out of it by psychoanalysis. So yesterday he took me to Dr. Froyd. So Dr. Froyd and I had quite a long talk in the english landguage. So it seems that everybody seems to have a thing called inhibitions, which is when you want to do a thing and you do not do it. So then you dream about it instead. So Dr. Froyd asked me, what I seemed to dream about. So I told him that I never really dream about anything. I mean I use my brains so much in the day time that at night they do not seem to do anything else but rest. So Dr. Froyd was very very surprized at a girl who did not dream about anything. So then he asked me all about my life. I mean he is very very sympathetic, and he seems to know how to draw a girl out quite a lot. I mean I told him things that I really would not even put in my diary. So then he seemed very very intreeged at a girl who always seemed to do everything she wanted to do. So he asked me if I really never wanted to do a thing that I did not do. For instance did I ever want to do a thing that was really vialent, for instance, did I ever want to shoot someone for instance. So then I said I had, but the bullet only went in Mr. Jennings lung and came right out again. So then Dr. Froyd looked at me and looked at me and he said he did not really think it was possible. So then he called in his assistance and he pointed at me and talked to his assistance quite a lot in the Viennese landguage. So then his assistance looked at me and looked at me and it really seems as if I was quite a famous case. So then Dr. Froyd said that all I needed was to cultivate a few inhibitions and get some sleep.

===Chinese===
5月27日：
哎，我到底还是撑不住了，斯波福德先生说，他觉得像我这样的一个小姑娘，一心要改造全世界，实在是想干得太多了，尤其还从多萝西这样的姑娘下手。于是他说，维也纳有一位有名的医生，叫弗洛伊德医生（Dr. Froyd），能把我这些操心统统治住，因为他不给姑娘开药，他靠精神分析把你说通。于是昨天他带我去了弗洛伊德医生那儿。于是弗洛伊德医生和我用英语谈了好长一场。原来人人都好像有一件叫「压抑」（inhibitions）的东西，就是你想要做一件事、你却不做。于是你改在梦里做。于是弗洛伊德医生问我，我好像都梦见些什么。我对他说，我实在从来不梦见什么。我是说，我白天把脑子使得太狠，到了夜里，它们除了歇着什么也不干了。弗洛伊德医生对一个什么都不梦见的姑娘，实在非常非常吃惊。于是他问了我一整套我的身世。我是说，他非常非常富于同情心，好像顶懂得怎么把一个姑娘的话引出来。我是说，我连真不愿写进日记里的事都告诉他了。于是他好像对一个想做什么就总做成了的姑娘，非常非常来劲。于是他问我，我是不是真的从来没有过想做而没做的事。比如说，我是不是有过想做一件真正凶暴的事的时候，比如说，我是不是想过要开枪打什么人，比如说。于是我说是有过，不过那颗子弹只打进了詹宁斯先生（Mr. Jennings）的肺里，马上又出来就是了。于是弗洛伊德医生瞧着我，瞧了又瞧，说他实在觉得这不可能。于是他把他的助手叫进来，指着我，用维也纳话对助手讲了好多好多。于是他的助手瞧着我，瞧了又瞧，看来我实在是个挺有名的病例。于是弗洛伊德医生说，我缺的就是养成几样「压抑」，再好好睡睡觉。

===Original===
May 29th:
Things are really getting to be quite a strain. Because yesterday Mr. Spoffard and Mr. Eisman were both in the lobby of the Bristol hotel and I had to pretend not to see both of them. I mean it is quite an easy thing to pretend not to see one gentleman, but it is a quite hard thing to pretend not to see two gentlemen. So something has really got to happen soon, or I will have to admit that things seem to be happening that are not for the best.
So this afternoon Dorothy and I had an engagement to meet Count Salm for tea at four o’clock, only you do not call it tea at Vienna but you seem to call it “yowzer” and you do not drink tea at Vienna but you drink coffee instead. I mean it is quite unusual to see all of the gentlemen at Vienna stop work, to go to yowzer about one hour after they have all finished their luncheon, but time really does not seem to mean so much to Viennese gentlemen except time to get to the coffee house, which they all seem to know by instincts, or else they really do not seem to mind if they make a mistake and get there to early. Because Mr. Eisman says that when it is time to attend to the button profession, they really seem to lose all of their interest until Mr. Eisman is getting so nervous he could scream.
So we went to Deimels and met Count Salm. But while we were having yowzer with Count Salm, we saw Mr. Spoffard’s mother come in with her companion Miss Chapman, and Miss Chapman seemed to look at me quite a lot and talk to Mr. Spoffards mother about me quite a lot. So I became quite nervous, because I really wished that we were not with Count Salm. I mean it has been quite a hard thing to make Mr. Spoffard think that I am trying to reform Dorothy, but if I had to try to make him think that I was trying to reform Count Salm, he might begin to think that there is a limit to almost everything. So Mr. Spoffards mother seems to be deaf, because she seems to use an ear trumpet and I really could not help over hearing quite a lot of words that Miss Chapman was using on me, even if it is not such good etiquet to overhear people. So Miss Chapman seemed to be telling Mr. Spoffards mother that I was a “creature,” and she seemed to be telling her that I was the real reason why her son seemed to be so full of nothing but neglect lately. So then Mr. Spoffards mother looked at me and looked at me, even if it was not such good etiquet to look at a person. And Miss Chapman kept right on talking to Mr. Spoffards mother and I heard her mention Willie Gwynn and I think that Miss Chapman has been making some inquiries about me and I really think that she has heard about the time when all of the family of Willie Gwynn had quite a long talk with me and persuaded me not to marry Willie Gwynn for $10,000. So I really wish Mr. Spoffard would introduce me to his mother before she gets to be full of quite a lot of prejudice. Because one thing seems to be piling up on top of another thing, until I am almost on the verge of getting nervous and I have not had any time yet to do what Dr. Froyd said a girl ought to do.
So tonight I am going to tell Mr. Eisman that I have got to go to bed early, so then I can take quite a long ride with Mr. Spoffard and look at nature, and he may say something definite, because nothing makes gentlemen get so definite as looking at nature when it is moonlight.

===Chinese===
5月29日：
事情实在渐渐变得相当折磨人了。因为昨天，斯波福德先生和艾斯曼先生双双坐在布里斯托尔旅馆（Bristol hotel）的大堂里，我只好装作他们两位都没看见。我是说，装作没看见一位绅士还容易，装作没看见两位绅士就相当难了。所以事情实在得快点儿见分晓，不然我就只好承认，眼下发生的事不是往最好处走的了。
于是今天下午多萝西和我约好四点钟同萨尔姆伯爵（Count Salm）喝茶——不过在维也纳你不叫它喝茶，你好像管它叫「姚泽」（yowzer），而且在维也纳你不喝茶，你喝的是咖啡。我是说，看见维也纳全体绅士在午饭吃完大约一个钟头之后，就撂下工作去姚泽，实在相当稀奇；不过时间对维也纳的绅士实在不算什么，除了上咖啡馆的时辰——那他们好像全凭本能就知道，再不然，走岔了到得太早，他们好像也实在不在乎。因为艾斯曼先生说，一到该打理纽扣行业的时辰，他们实在就把兴致全丢了，弄得艾斯曼先生越来越紧张，简直要尖叫出来。
于是我们去了戴梅尔斯（Deimels），见到了萨尔姆伯爵。可我们正跟萨尔姆伯爵姚泽着，看见斯波福德先生的母亲带着她的女伴查普曼小姐进来了，查普曼小姐好像频频打量我，又跟斯波福德先生的母亲讲了好些关于我的话。于是我紧张起来，因为我实在巴不得我们没跟萨尔姆伯爵坐在一起。我是说，让斯波福德先生相信我是在改造多萝西，已经够难的了，可要让我再让他相信我在改造萨尔姆伯爵，他该开始觉得，差不多什么事情都有个限度了。斯波福德先生的母亲好像是聋的，因为她好像用助听喇叭，我实在忍不住偷听到了好些查普曼小姐用来讲我的字眼，虽然偷听别人实在不算有礼貌。于是查普曼小姐好像正在告诉斯波福德先生的母亲，说我是个「害人精」，还好像告诉她，她儿子近来什么都不上心、一门心思怠慢，我就是真正的缘故。于是斯波福德先生的母亲瞧着我，瞧了又瞧，尽管盯着人瞧也不算有礼貌。查普曼小姐还接着跟斯波福德先生的母亲讲，我听见她提到了威利·格温（Willie Gwynn），我想查普曼小姐准是打听过我，我实在觉得，她一定听说了那一回，威利·格温全家人跟我长谈了一回，花了 10,000 美元才劝得我别嫁给威利·格温。所以我实在希望斯波福德先生趁他母亲还没满脑子成见，赶快把我引见给他母亲。因为一件事好像正压在另一件事上头，压得我几乎要犯神经了，而弗洛伊德医生说姑娘该做的事，我还一样都没来得及做。
所以今晚我要告诉艾斯曼先生，我得早点上床睡觉，这样我就能跟斯波福德先生乘车兜个好远的风，看看大自然，他也许会说出一句准话来，因为月光底下看大自然，最能叫绅士们说出准话了。

===Original===
May 30th:
Well last night Mr. Spoffard and I took quite a long ride in the park, but they do not call it a park in the Viennese landguage but they call it the Prater. So a prater is really devine because it is just like Coney Island but at the same time it is in the woods and it is practically full of trees and it has quite a long road for people to take rides on in a horse and buggy. So I found out that Miss Chapman had been talking against me quite a lot. So it seems that she has been making inquiries about me, and I was really surprised to hear all of the things that Miss Chapman seemed to find out about me except that she did not find out about Mr. Eisman educating me. So then I had to tell Mr. Spoffard that I was not always so reformed as I am now, because the world was full of gentlemen who were nothing but wolfs in sheeps clothes, that did nothing but take advantadge of all we girls. So I really cried quite a lot. So then I told him how I was just a little girl from Little Rock when I first left Little Rock and by that time even Mr. Spoffard had tears in his eyes. So I told him how I came from a very very good family because papa was very intelectual, and he was a very very prominent Elk, and everybody always said that he was a very intelectual Elk. So I told Mr. Spoffard that when I left Little Rock I thought that all of the gentlemen did not want to do anything but protect we girls and by the time I found out that they did not want to protect us so much, it was to late. So then he cried quite a lot. So then I told him how I finaly got reformed by reading all about him in the newspapers and when I saw him in the oriental express it really seemed to be nothing but the result of fate. So I told Mr. Spoffard that I thought a girl was really more reformed if she knew what it was to be unreformed than if she was born reformed and never really knew that was the matter with her. So then Mr. Spoffard reached over and he kissed me on the forehead in a way that was full of reverance and he said I seemed to remind him quite a lot of a girl who got quite a write-up in the bible who was called Magdellen. So then he said that he used to be a member of the choir himself, so who was he to cast the first rock at a girl like I.
So we rode around in the Prater until it was quite late and it really was devine because it was moonlight and we talked quite a lot about morals, and all the bands in the prater were all playing in the distants “Mama love Papa.” Because “Mama love Papa” has just reached Vienna and they all seem to be crazy about “Mama love Papa” even if it is not so new in America. So then he took me home to the hotel.
So everything always works out for the best, because this morning Mr. Spoffard called up and told me he wanted me to meet his mother. So I told him I would like to have luncheon alone with his mother because we could have quite a little tatatate if there was only two of us. So I told him to bring his mother to our room for luncheon because I thought that Miss Chapman could not walk into our room and spoil everything.
So he brought his mother down to our sitting room and I put on quite a simple little organdy gown that I had ripped all of the trimming off of, and I had a pair of black lace mitts that Dorothy used to wear in the Follies and I had a pair of shoes that did not have any heels on them. So when he introduced us to each other I dropped her a courtesy because I always think it is quite quaint when a girl drops quite a lot of courtesys. So then he left us alone and we had quite a little talk and I told her that I did not seem to like all of the flappers that we seem to have nowadays, because I was brought up to be more old fashioned. So then Mr. Spoffards mother told me that Miss Chapman said that she had heard that I was not so old fashioned. But I told her that I was so old fashioned that I was always full of respect for all of my elders and I would not dare to tell them everything they ought to do, like Miss Chapman seems to tell her everything she ought to do, for instants.

===Chinese===
5月30日：
哎，昨晚斯波福德先生和我在公园里兜了好远一程风，不过在维也纳话里他们不叫它公园，他们管它叫普拉特（Prater）。普拉特实在妙极了，因为它就跟科尼艾兰一个样，可它同时又坐落在树林子里，简直满眼都是树，还有一条好长的路，让人们坐着马车兜风。于是我弄明白了，查普曼小姐一直说了我好多坏话。原来她一直打听着我的事，查普曼小姐打听到关于我的那些事，真叫我吃惊，只有艾斯曼先生受教育我这件事她没打听出来。于是我只好告诉斯波福德先生，我从前并不总像如今这么有改造，因为这世界满世界都是绅士，个个不过是披着羊皮的狼，干的不外是占尽我们姑娘的便宜。于是我真是哭了好多好多。接着我对他说，我当初离开小石城的时候，还只是小石城来的一个小姑娘，讲到这儿，连斯波福德先生的眼睛里都有了眼泪。于是我对他说，我出身非常非常好的人家，因为爸爸非常学问，还是麋鹿会里非常非常显要的会员，人人总说他是位非常有学问的麋鹿。于是我告诉斯波福德先生，我离开小石城的时候，还以为天下的绅士们一心只想保护我们姑娘们，等我发觉他们并不那么想保护我们的时候，已经来不及了。于是他哭了好多好多。接着我告诉他，我后来是怎么读着报纸上他的事迹改过自新的，我在东方快车上看见他，实在好像什么都不是，纯粹是命运的安排。于是我对斯波福德先生说，我觉得一个姑娘，要是懂得不改造是什么滋味再改过自新，那才比生来就改造、自己还不知道毛病在哪儿的人改造得更彻底。于是斯波福德先生伸过手来，满是敬畏地在我额头上亲了一下，他说我好像让他想起了圣经里一位被大书特书的姑娘，叫抹大拉（Magdellen）。接着他说他自己从前还唱过诗班呢，他算老几，哪能朝我这样的姑娘先扔石头。
于是我们在普拉特里兜来兜去，一直兜到好晚，实在妙极了，因为那晚有月光，我们谈了好多好多道德，普拉特里所有的乐队都在远处奏着《妈妈爱爸爸》。因为《妈妈爱爸爸》刚刚传到维也纳，他们个个都为《妈妈爱爸爸》疯了，哪怕这歌在美国已经不算新了。后来他送我回旅馆。
于是事情总是往最好的方向走，因为今天早上斯波福德先生打来电话，说他要让我见他的母亲。我对他说，我倒愿意单独跟他母亲吃午饭，因为要是只我们两个人，我们还可以好好来一场小小的促膝私谈。于是我叫他把他母亲带到我们房间来吃午饭，因为我想这样一来，查普曼小姐总不能闯进我们房间来搅局。
于是他把他母亲带到了我们的起居室，我穿上了一条挺素净的小纱裙——上头的花边全让我撕掉了——戴了一双多萝西从前在《福利斯》歌舞团戴的黑蕾丝露指手套，脚上一双没有跟的鞋。他给我们互相引见的时候，我给她行了一个屈膝礼，因为我总认为一个姑娘多行几个屈膝礼，实在相当有古风。然后他留下我们单独待着，我们小小谈了一场，我对她说，如今我们到处见的这些轻佻姑娘，我实在都不喜欢，因为我从小受的就是更老派的教养。于是斯波福德先生的母亲对我说，查普曼小姐说，她听人家讲，我并不怎么老派。可我对她说，我老派得很，我一向满心敬重长辈，绝不敢什么都指挥长辈该做什么——像查普曼小姐好像什么都指挥她该做什么那样，比如说。

===Original===
So then I ordered luncheon and I thought some champagne would make her feel quite good for luncheon so I asked her if she liked champagne. So she really likes champagne very very much but Miss Chapman thinks it is not so nice for a person to drink liquor. But I told her that I was a Christian science, and all of we Christian science seem to believe that there can not really be any harm in anything, so how can there be any harm in a small size bottle of champagne? So she never seemed to look at it in that kind of a light before, because she said that Miss Chapman believed in Christian science also, but what Miss Chapman believed about things that were good for you to drink seemed to apply more towards water. So then we had luncheon and she began to feel very very good. So I thought that we had better have another bottle of champagne because I told her that I was such an ardent Christian science that I did not even believe there could be any harm in two bottles of champagne. So we had another bottle of champagne and she became very intreeged about Christian science because she said that she really thought it was a better religion than Prespyterians. So she said Miss Chapman used to try to get her to use it on things, but Miss Chapman never seemed to have such a large size grasp of the Christian science religion as I seem to have.
So then I told her that I thought Miss Chapman was jealous of her good looks. So then she said that that was true, because Miss Chapman would always make her wear hats that were made out of black horses hair because horses hair does not weigh so much on a persons brain. So I told her I was going to give her one of my hats that has got quite large size roses on it. So then I got it out, but we could not get it on her head because hats are quite small on account of hair being bobbed. So I thought I would get the sissors and bob her head, but then I thought I had done enough to her for one day.
So Henry’s mother said that I was really the most sunshine that she ever had in all her life and when Henry came back to take his Mother up to her room, she did not want to go. But after he got her away he called me up on the telephone and he was qiute excited and he said he wanted to ask me something that was very very important. So I said I would see him tonight.
But now I have got to see Mr. Eisman because I have an idea about doing something that is really very very important that has got to be done at once.

===Chinese===
接着我点午饭，我想来点香槟她午饭一定受用，就问她喜欢不喜欢香槟。她实在非常喜欢非常喜欢香槟，只是查普曼小姐认为一个人喝酒不大好。可我对她说，我是基督教科学派，我们基督教科学派好像全都相信，任何东西里实在都不可能有什么害处，那一小瓶香槟里又怎么会有害处呢？她从前好像从来没这么看过这件事，因为她说查普曼小姐也信基督教科学派，不过查普曼小姐关于喝什么都讲究的这套，好像更偏向于水。于是我们吃了午饭，她开始觉得非常非常舒坦。于是我想我们最好再来一瓶香槟，因为我对她说，我是个非常热诚的基督教科学派，热诚到连两瓶香槟里会有什么害处我都不信。于是我们又喝了一瓶香槟，她开始对基督教科学派非常来劲，因为她实在觉得这比长老会是更好的宗教。于是她说查普曼小姐从前总想劝她拿这宗教对付点什么事，不过查普曼小姐对基督教科学派这门宗教的领会，好像从来没有我这么大号的格局。
接着我对她说，我觉得查普曼小姐是嫉妒她的美貌。于是她说这倒是真的，因为查普曼小姐老让她戴黑马鬃做的帽子，因为马鬃压在人的脑子上分量轻。我对她说，我要送她一顶我的帽子，上头有大号大号的玫瑰。于是我把它取了出来，可我们怎么也没法把它扣到她头上，因为如今姑娘们都剪波波头，帽子都做得挺小。于是我想拿剪子把她的头发也剪了，可转念一想，我一天里为她做的已经够多了。
于是亨利的母亲说，我实在是一辈子照进她生命里最多的阳光，亨利回来接他母亲回房的时候，她都不想走了。可等他把她带走之后，他打电话给我，相当激动，说他要问我一件非常非常重要的事。于是我说我今晚见他。
可这会儿我得去见艾斯曼先生了，因为我起了一个念头，要办一件实在非常非常重要、非得马上办的事。

===Original===
May 31st:
Well I and Dorothy and Mr. Eisman are on a train going to a place called Buda Pest. So I did not see Henry again before I left, but I left him a letter. Because I thought it would be a quite good thing if what he wanted to ask me he would have to write down, instead of asking me, and he could not write it to me if I was in the same city that he is in. So I told him in my letter that I had to leave in five minute’s time because I found out that Dorothy was just on the verge of getting very unreformed, and if I did not get her away, all I had done for her would really go for nothing. So I told him to write down what he had to say to me, and mail it to me at the Ritz hotel in Buda Pest. Because I always seem to believe in the old addage, Say it in writing.
So it was really very easy to get Mr. Eisman to leave Vienna, because yesterday he went out to see the button factory but it seems that all of the people at the button factory were not at work but they were giving a birthday party to some saint. So it seems that every time some saint has a birthday they all stop work so they can give it a birthday party. So Mr. Eisman looked at their calendar, and found out that some saint or other was born practically every week in the year. So he has decided that America is good enough for him.
So Henry will not be able to follow me to Buda Pest because his mother is having treatments by Dr. Froyd and she seems to be a much more difficult case than I seem to be. I mean it is quite hard for Dr. Froyd, because she cannot seem to remember which is a dream and which really happened to her. So she tells him everything, and he has to use his judgement. I mean when she tells him that a very very handsome young gentleman tried to flirt with her on Fifth Avenue, he uses his judgement.
So we will soon be at a Ritz hotel again and I must say it will be delightful to find a Ritz hotel right in the central of Europe.

===Chinese===
5月31日：
哎，我和多萝西、艾斯曼先生在火车上了，去一个叫布达佩斯（Buda Pest）的地方。所以我临走没再见到亨利，不过我给他留了一封信。因为我想，他要问我的事，要是非得写下来不可、不能当面问，那才好呢，而我要是跟他待在同一座城里，他就没法子写给我了。于是我在信里告诉他，我五分钟之内就得动身，因为我发现多萝西正到了非常不改造的关口上，我要是不把她带走，我为她做的一切实在就全白费了。于是我告诉他，把他要跟我说的话写下来，寄到布达佩斯的丽兹酒店给我。因为我总好像信那句老话：有事写信说。
劝艾斯曼先生离开维也纳实在容易得很，因为昨天他去纽扣厂视察，可纽扣厂里的人好像全不上工，全在给哪位圣徒开生日会。原来每回哪位圣徒过生日，他们就全体停工，好给他开生日会。于是艾斯曼先生看了他们的日历，发现一年里几乎每个礼拜都有哪位圣徒或别的什么人出生。于是他认定，美国对他来说就够好了。
亨利没法跟着我来布达佩斯，因为他母亲正在弗洛伊德医生那儿接受治疗，她的病例好像比我难治得多。我是说，弗洛伊德医生实在辛苦，因为她好像记不清哪件是梦、哪件是真发生在她身上的。于是她什么都告诉他，只好由他来判断。我是说，她跟他说有一位非常非常英俊的年轻绅士在第五大道上跟她调情的时候，就得由他来判断。
这么着，我们很快又要住进一家丽兹酒店了，我得说，在中欧的正当中一抬头就找着一家丽兹酒店，实在叫人愉快。

===Original===
June 1st:
Well yesterday Henrys letter came and it says in black and white that he and his mother have never met such a girl as I and he wants me to marry him. So I took Henrys letter to the photographers and I had quite a lot of photographs taken of it because a girl might lose Henrys letter and she would not have anything left to remember him by. But Dorothy says to hang on to Henry’s letter, because she really does not think the photographs do it justice.
So this afternoon I got a telegram from Henry and the telegram says that Henry’s father is very, very ill in New York and they have got to leave for New York immediately and his heart is broken not to see me again and to send him my answer by telegraph so that his mind will be rested while he is going back to New York. So I sent him a telegram and I accepted his proposal. So tonight I got another telegram and Henry says that he and his mother are very very happy and Henrys mother can hardly bear Miss Chapman any more and Henry says he hopes I will decide to come right back to New York and keep his mother quite a lot of company, because he thinks I can reform Dorothy more in New York anyway, where there is prohibition and nobody can get anything to drink.
So now I have got to make up my mind whether I really want to marry Henry after all. Because I know to much to get married to any gentleman like Henry without thinking it all over. Because Henry is the kind of a gentleman who gets on a girls nerves quite a lot and when a gentleman has nothing else to do but get on a girls nerves, there really seems to be a limit to almost everything. Because when a gentleman has a business, he has an office and he has to be there, but when a gentlemans business is only looking into other peoples business, a gentleman is always on the verge of coming in and out of the house. And a girl could not really say that her time was her own. And when Henry was not in and out of the house, his mother would always be in and out of the house because she seems to think that I am so full of nothing but sunshine. So it is quite a problem and I seem to be in quite a quarandary, because it might really be better if Henry should happen to decide that he should not get married, and he should change his mind, and desert a girl, and then it would only be right if a girl should sue him for a breach of promise.
But I really think, whatever happens, that Dorothy and I had better get back to New York. So I will see if Mr. Eisman will send us back. I mean I really do not think that Mr. Eisman will mind us going back because if he does, I will start shopping again and that always seems to bring him to terms. But all the time I am going back to New York, I will have to try to make up my mind one way or another. Because we girls really can not help it, if we have ideals, and sometimes my mind seems to get to running on things that are romantic, and I seem to think that maybe there is some place in the world where there is a gentleman who knows how to look and act like Count Salm and who has got money besides. And when a girls mind gets to thinking about such a romantic thing, a girls mind really does not seem to know whether to marry Henry or not.

===Chinese===
6月1日：
哎，昨天亨利的信到了，白纸黑字写着，他和他母亲从来没见过我这样的姑娘，他要我嫁给他。于是我拿着亨利的信去照相馆，把它照了好多好多张相片，因为一个姑娘兴许会把亨利的信弄丢，那样她就什么念想也剩不下了。可多萝西说，还是把亨利的信攥牢，因为她实在觉得那些照片照不出它的神采。
今天下午我收到亨利的电报，电报说亨利的父亲在纽约病得非常非常重，他们得马上动身回纽约，他心里难过极了，不能再看我一面，叫我打电报把答复给他，好让他在回纽约的路上心里安定。于是我给他回了电报，接受了他的求婚。今晚我又收到一封电报，亨利说他和他母亲非常非常高兴，亨利的母亲简直再也不能容忍查普曼小姐了，亨利还说，他希望我下决心马上回纽约，多多给他母亲作伴，因为反正他觉得，我在纽约能把多萝西改造得更好，那儿有禁酒令，谁也弄不着酒喝。
于是这会儿我得拿定主意，到底还想不想嫁亨利。因为我懂得太多了，嫁亨利这样的绅士，不能不好好想个透。因为亨利是那种很磨姑娘神经的绅士，而一位绅士要是没事可干、专会磨姑娘的神经，那差不多什么事可就都有个限度了。因为一位绅士要有正业，他就有个办事处，总得到那儿去；可一位绅士的正业要是专管别人的事，那他就成天在进进出出家门。一个姑娘实在没法说她的时间是自己的。亨利不进出家门的时候，他母亲又要成天进进出出家门，因为她好像觉得我浑身满是阳光。这实在是个难题，我实在陷在不知怎么好里，因为万一亨利自己倒是决定不该结婚、变了卦、把一个姑娘甩了呢，那一个姑娘就该告他一个悔婚，只有这样才算公道。
可我实在觉得，不管怎么样，多萝西和我最好还是回纽约。我要看看艾斯曼先生肯不肯送我们回去。我是说，我实在觉得艾斯曼先生不会不愿意我们回去，他要是不愿意，我就再开始买东西，那一招总能叫他服软。不过回纽约的一路上，我得设法把主意拿定，不管怎么拿。因为我们姑娘实在没法子，谁让我们有理想呢；有时候我的脑子好像净往浪漫的事情上跑，我好像觉得，世上说不定真有个地方，有那么一位绅士，长得、做派都跟萨尔姆伯爵一样，另外还有钱。一个姑娘的脑子一旦想上这么浪漫的事，实在就不知道该不该嫁亨利了。

## VI Brains Are Really Everything / 六 头脑实在才是一切

===Original===
June 14th:
Well, Dorothy and I arrived at New York yesterday because Mr. Eisman finally decided to send us home because he said that all of his button profession would not stand the strain of educating me much more in Europe. So we separated from Mr. Eisman in Buda Pest because Mr. Eisman had to go to Berlin to look up all of his starving relatives in Berlin, who have done nothing but starve since the War, so he wrote me just before we sailed and he said that he had dug up all his starving relatives and he had looked them all over, and decided not to bring them to America because there was not one of his starving relatives who could travel on a railroad ticket without paying excess fare for overweight.
So Dorothy and I took the boat and all the way over on the boat I had to make up my mind whether I really wanted to marry the famous Henry H. Spoffard, or not, because he was waiting for me to arrive at New York and he was so impatient that he could hardly wait for me to arrive at New York. But I have not wasted all of my time on Henry, even if I do not marry him, because I have some letters from Henry which would come in very, very handy if I did not marry Henry. So Dorothy seems to agree with me quite a lot, because Dorothy says the only thing she could stand being to Henry, would be to be his widow at the age of 18.
So coming over on the boat I decided not to bother to meet any gentleman, because what good does it do to meet gentlemen when there is nothing to do on a boat but go shopping at a little shop where they do not have anything that costs more than five dollars. And besides if I did meet any gentleman on the boat, he would want to see me off the boat, and then we would bump into Henry. But then I heard that there was a gentleman on the boat who was quite a dealer in unset diamonds from a town called Amsterdam. So I met the gentleman, and we went around together quite a lot, but we had quite a quarrel the night before we landed, so I did not even bother to look at him when I came down the gangplank, and I put the unset diamonds in my handbag so I did not have to declare them at the customs.
So Henry was waiting for me at the customs, because he had come up from Pennsylvania to meet me, because their country estate is at Pennsylvania, and Henry’s father is very, very ill at Pennsylvania, so Henry has to stay there practically all of the time. So all of the reporters were at the customs and they all heard about how Henry and I were engaged to one another and they wanted to know what I was before I became engaged to Henry, so I told them that I was nothing but a society girl from Little Rock, Arkansas. So then I became quite angry with Dorothy because one of the reporters asked Dorothy when I made my debut in society at Little Rock and Dorothy said I made my debut at the Elks annual street fair and carnival at the age of 15, I mean Dorothy never overlooks any chance to be unrefined, even when she is talking to literary gentlemen like reporters.

===Chinese===
6月14日：
嗯，多萝西（Dorothy）和我昨天到纽约了，因为艾斯曼先生（Mr. Eisman）终于决定送我们回家，因为他说，他那一整个纽扣行业实在经不起再在欧洲教育我的折腾了。于是我们和艾斯曼先生在布达佩斯（Buda Pest）分了手，因为艾斯曼先生得去柏林，寻访他在柏林的所有挨饿的亲戚——那些亲戚自打大战以来什么也没干，光顾着挨饿了。开船前他给我来了封信，说他把挨饿的亲戚全都刨了出来，挨个儿端详了一遍，决定不带他们去美国，因为他那些挨饿的亲戚里，没有一个能坐火车不因为超重补票的。
于是多萝西和我上了船。一路航行，我一直得拿定主意，到底要不要嫁给那位大名鼎鼎的亨利·H·斯波福德（Henry H. Spoffard），因为他正等着我到纽约，等得那样心急，简直等不及我到纽约。不过就算不嫁他，我在亨利身上也没白费工夫，因为我手里有亨利的几封信，万一我不嫁亨利，这些信往后会非常非常好使。多萝西在这点上倒相当赞成我，因为多萝西说，她对亨利唯一能忍受做的，就是 18 岁上做他的寡妇。
于是航行途中我决定懒得结识任何绅士，因为在船上结识绅士有什么好处呢，船上又没事可做，只有一家小铺子可以逛，铺子里没有一件东西超过五块钱。再说，我要真在船上结识了什么绅士，他就要送我下船，那就会撞上亨利。可是后来我听说船上有一位先生，是个相当有来头的未镶钻石商人，来自一个叫阿姆斯特丹（Amsterdam）的城市。于是我就结识了这位先生，我们同行了好些地方，可是着陆前一晚我们大吵了一架，所以下舷梯的时候我连看都懒得看他一眼，还把那些未镶钻石放进了手提包，这样就不用在海关申报了。
于是亨利在海关等着我，因为他是从宾夕法尼亚（Pennsylvania）赶上来接我的，他们家的乡间宅邸就在宾夕法尼亚，亨利的父亲在宾夕法尼亚病得很重很重，所以亨利几乎得一直待在那儿。海关那儿全是记者，他们全听说了我和亨利订婚的事，想知道我跟亨利订婚之前是干什么的，我就告诉他们，我不过是阿肯色州小石城（Little Rock）的一位社交界姑娘。接着我相当生多萝西的气，因为有位记者问多萝西，我当年在小石城几时初入社交界的，多萝西竟说我是 15 岁上在麋鹿会一年一度的街头庙会和嘉年华上初入社交界的。我是说，多萝西从不放过任何一个没教养的机会，哪怕是跟记者这号的文人雅士说话。

===Original===
So Henry brought me to the apartment in his Rolls Royce, and while we were coming to the apartment he said he wanted to give me my engagement ring and I really became all thrills. So he said that he had gone to Cartiers and he had looked over all the engagement rings in Cartiers and after he had looked them all over he had decided that they were not half good enough for me. So then he took a box out of his pocket and I really became intreeged. So then Henry said that when he looked at all of those large size diamonds he really felt that they did not have any sentiment, so he was going to give me his class ring from Amherst College insted. So then I looked at him and looked at him, but I am to full of self controle to say anything at this stage of the game, so I said it was really very sweet of him to be so full of nothing but sentiment.
So then Henry said that he would have to go back to Pennsylvania to talk to his father about us getting married, because his father has really got his heart set on us not getting married. So I told Henry that perhaps if I would meet his father, I would win him over, because I always seem to win gentlemen over. But Henry says that that is just the trouble, because some girl is always winning his father over, and they hardly dare to let him go out of their sight, and they hardly dare let him go to church alone. Because the last time he went to church alone some girl won him over on the street corner and he arrived back home with all of his pocket money gone, and they could not believe him when he said that he had put it in the plate, because he has not put more than a dime in the plate for the last fifty years.
So it seems that the real reason why his father does not want Henry to marry me, is because his father says that Henry always has all of the fun, and every time Henry’s father wants to have some fun of his own, Henry always stops him and Henry will not even let him be sick at a hospital where he could have some fun of his own, but he keeps him at home where he has to have a nurse Henry picked out for him who is a male nurse. So all of his objections seem to be nothing but the spirit of resiprosity. But Henry says that all his objections cannot last much longer because he is nearly 90 years of age after all, and Nature must take its course sooner or later.
So Dorothy says what a fool I am to waste my time on Henry, when I might manage to meet Henry’s father and the whole thing would be over in a few months and I would practically own the state of Pennsylvania. But I do not think I ought to take Dorothy’s advise because Henry’s father is watched like a hawk and Henry himself is his Power of Attorney, so no good could really come of it after all. And, after all, why should I listen to the advise of a girl like Dorothy who travelled all over Europe and all she came home with was a bangle!

===Chinese===
于是亨利用他的劳斯莱斯送我回公寓，路上他说要给我订婚戒指，我真是浑身上下都激动起来。他说他去过了卡地亚（Cartier），把卡地亚的订婚戒指看了一个遍，看完之后断定它们连一半都配不上我。接着他从口袋里掏出一只盒子，我真是好奇心大起。然后亨利说，他看着那些大颗大颗的钻石，实在觉得它们毫无情感可言，所以他打算改送我他的阿默斯特学院（Amherst College）的班级戒指。于是我看了看他，又看了看他，可我这个人太有自我克制力了，事到这一步什么也没说，只说满心满眼只装得下情感二字，实在难得他这样贴心。
接着亨利说，他得回宾夕法尼亚去跟他父亲谈我们结婚的事，因为他父亲实在是一心一意巴望着我们结不成。我对亨利说，兴许让我见见他父亲，我就能把他争取过来，因为我好像总能把绅士们争取过来。可亨利说，毛病恰恰出在这儿，因为总有哪个姑娘在把他父亲往手里争取，家里人几乎不敢让他离开视线，几乎不敢让他一个人上教堂。因为上一回他独自上教堂，就在街角让哪个姑娘给争取过去了，回到家零用钱花了个精光，他说他把钱都放进奉献盘里了，谁也不信，因为这 50 年来他往盘里放的钱就没超过一毛。
原来他父亲不肯让亨利娶我的真正缘由，是他父亲说亨利总是把乐子全占了，每逢他父亲想自己找点乐子，亨利总拦着他，甚至连生病都不让他上医院——在医院他本可以自己找点乐子——偏把他留在家里，由亨利替他挑了个护士照顾，还是个男护士。所以他那些反对，看来全不过是一种「礼尚往来」的精神。不过亨利说，他父亲的反对长不了了，因为他毕竟快 90 岁的人了，大自然迟早要走完它的历程。
多萝西却说我真是个傻瓜，在亨利身上浪费时间，我要是想法子结识亨利的父亲，不出几个月就全完事了，差不多整个宾夕法尼亚州都归了我。可我觉得不该听多萝西的建议，因为亨利的父亲被人盯得像只鹰似的，亨利本人又是他的法定代理人，说到底落不着什么好。再说了，我干嘛要听多萝西这种姑娘的建议呢——她游遍了整个欧洲，带回家的不过是一只镯子！

===Original===
So Henry spent the evening at the apartment and then he had to go back to Pennsylvania to be there Thursday morning, because every Thursday morning he belongs to a society who do nothing but senshure all of the photoplays. So they cut out all of the pieces out of all of the photoplays that show things that are riskay, that people ought not to look at. So then they put all of the riskay pieces together and they run them over and over again. So it would really be quite a hard thing to drag Henry away from one of his Thursday mornings and he can hardly wait from one Thursday morning to another. Because he really does not seem to enjoy anything so much as senshuring photoplays and after a photoplay has once been senshured he seems to lose all of his interest in it.
So after Henry left I held quite a conversation with Lulu, who is my maid who looked out for my apartment while I was away. So Lulu really thinks I ought to marry Mr. Spoffard after all, because Lulu says that she kept studying Mr. Spoffard all of the time she was unpacking my trunks, and Lulu says she is sure that any time I feel as if I had to get away from Mr. Spoffard I could just set him down on the floor, and give him a packet of riskay french postcards to senshure and stay away as long as I like.
So Henry is going to arrange for me to come down to Pennsylvania for a weekend and meet all of his family. But if all of Henry’s family are as full of reforms as Henry seems to be, it will be quite an ordeal even for a girl like I.

===Chinese===
于是亨利在公寓过了傍晚，然后就得回宾夕法尼亚去赶星期四早上，因为每星期四早上他都属于一个协会，那协会什么也不干，专审查所有的影片。他们把所有影片里有伤风化、人们不该看的段落统统剪掉，然后再把所有有伤风化的段落拼到一块儿，翻来覆去地放。所以想把亨利从他哪个星期四早上拽走，实在相当难，他从一个星期四早上熬到下一个星期四早上，简直度日如年。因为他看上去实在再没有比审查影片更让他乐在其中了，一部影片一旦审查过了，他好像就对它兴味索然了。
亨利走后，我和露露（Lulu）好好谈了一阵，她是我的女仆，我不在的时候替我照看公寓。露露真心觉得我到底还是该嫁斯波福德先生（Mr. Spoffard），因为露露说，她替我开箱子理行李的那阵子，把斯波福德先生研究了个透，她敢肯定，往后不论什么时候，我只要觉得非躲开斯波福德先生不可，只消把他往地板上一放，塞给他一沓有伤风化的法国明信片让他审查，爱走多久走多久。
于是亨利打算安排我去宾夕法尼亚过一个周末，见见他全家。可要是亨利全家都像亨利这样满脑子改革，那对我这样一个姑娘来说，也实在是一场考验。

===Original===
June 15th:
Yesterday morning was quite an ordeal for a refined girl because all of the newspapers all printed the story of how Henry and I are engaged to one another, but they all seemed to leave out the part about me being a society girl except one newspaper, and that was the newspaper that quoted what Dorothy said about me being a debutant at the Elk’s Carnival. So I called up Dorothy at the Ritz and I told Dorothy that a girl like she ought to keep her mouth closed in the presents of reporters.
So it seems that quite a lot of reporters kept calling Dorothy up but Dorothy said she really did not say anything to any of them except one reporter asked her what I used for money and she told him buttons. But Dorothy really should not have said such a thing, because quite a few people seem to know that Mr. Eisman is educating me and that he is known all over Chicago as Gus Eisman the Button King, so one thing might suggest another until people’s minds might begin to think something.
But Dorothy said that she did not say anything more about me being a debutant at Little Rock, because after all Dorothy knows that I really did not make any debut in Little Rock, because just when it was time to make my debut, my gentleman friend Mr. Jennings became shot, and after the trial was over and all of the Jury had let me off, I was really much to fatigued to make any debut.
So then Dorothy said, why don’t we throw a party now and you can become a debutant now and put them all in their place, because it seems that Dorothy is dying for a party. So that is really the first sensible suggestion that Dorothy has made yet, because I think that every girl who is engaged to a gentleman who has a fine old family like Henry, had really ought to be a debutant. So I told her to come right over and we would plan my debut but we would keep it very, very quiet and give it tomorrow night, because if Henry heard I was making my debut he would come up from Pennsylvania and he would practically spoil the party, because all Henry has to do to spoil a party is to arrive at it.

===Chinese===
6月15日：
昨天上午，对一个有教养的姑娘来说实在是一场考验，因为所有的报纸全登了我和亨利订婚的新闻，可是关于我是社交界姑娘的那一段，他们好像全略去了，只有一家报纸没略——偏偏就是引用多萝西的话、说我是麋鹿会嘉年华上的初入社交界少女的那家。于是我打电话到丽兹酒店（the Ritz）训多萝西，对她说，像她这样的姑娘，在记者面前就该把嘴闭严实。
看来好些记者一直给多萝西打电话，可多萝西说她实在没对他们说什么，只有一位记者问她，我平时拿什么当钱花，她告诉人家：纽扣。这种话多萝西实在不该说，因为不少人好像都知道艾斯曼先生正在供我受教育，还知道他全芝加哥闻名，是「纽扣大王」格斯·艾斯曼（Gus Eisman），这样一桩牵着另一桩，人们的脑子迟早要开始想出点什么来。
不过多萝西说，关于我在小石城初入社交界的事，她没再多说一个字，因为说到底多萝西知道，我在小石城实在没搞过什么初入社交界，因为正轮到我初入社交界的当口，我的绅士朋友詹宁斯先生（Mr. Jennings）让人给开了枪，等审判完、陪审团全体放我出来之后，我实在累得太很，什么初入社交界也搞不成了。
接着多萝西说，咱们现在干嘛不办个聚会，你现在就当一回初入社交界的少女，把他们的威风全压下去——看得出多萝西想聚会想得要命。这实在是多萝西至今提出的头一个明白建议，因为我觉得，凡是跟亨利这样家世高贵的绅士订了婚的姑娘，实在都该初入一回社交界。于是我叫她马上过来，我们一起筹划我的初入社交界，不过要办得非常非常低调，明晚就办，因为要是让亨利听见我初入社交界，他会从宾夕法尼亚赶上来，把聚会整个搅了——要搅一场聚会，亨利只消到场就够了。

===Original===
So Dorothy came over and we planned my debut. So first we decided to have some engraved invitations engraved, but it always takes quite a little time to have invitations engraved, and it would really be foolish because all of the gentlemen we were going to invite to my debut were all members of the Racquet Club, so I could just write out a notice that I was having a debut and give it to Willie Gwynn and have Willie Gwynn post it on the Racquet Club board.
So Willie Gwynn posted it on the club board and then he called me up and he told me that he had never seen so much enthusiasm since the Dempsey-Firpo fight, and he said that the whole Racquet Club would be there in a body. So then we had to plan about what girls we would ask to my debut. Because I have not seemed to meet so many society women yet because of course a girl does not meet society women until her debut is all over, and then all the society women all come and call on a debutant. But I know practically all of the society men, because practically all of the society men belong to the Racquet club, so after I have the Racquet Club at my debut, all I have to do to take my real place in society is to meet their mothers and sisters, because I know practically all of their sweethearts now.
But I always seem to think that it is delightful to have quite a lot of girls at a party, if a girl has quite a lot of gentlemen at a party, and it is quite delightful to have all the girls from the Follies, but I really could not invite them because, after all, they are not in my set. So then I thought it all over and I thought that even if it was not etiquette to invite them to a party, it really would be etiquette to hire them to come to a party and be entertainers, and after they were entertainers they could mix in to the party and it really would not be a social error.
So then the telephone rang and Dorothy answered it and it seems that it was Joe Sanguinetti, who is almost the official bootlegger for the whole Racquet Club, and Joe said he had heard about my debut and if he could come to my debut and bring his club which is the Silver Spray Social Club of Brooklyn, he would supply all of the liquor and he would guarantee to practically run the rum fleet up to the front door.

===Chinese===
于是多萝西过来了，我们一起筹划我的初入社交界。起先我们决定雕印一批雕版请帖，可是雕印请帖总要费不少工夫，而且实在犯不着，因为我们要请来我初入社交界聚会的绅士，全都是拉奎特俱乐部（Racquet Club）的会员，我只消写一张告示，说我将举办初入社交界聚会，交给威利·格温（Willie Gwynn），让威利·格温把它贴到拉奎特俱乐部的布告板上就成。
威利·格温把告示贴上了俱乐部的布告板，然后打电话告诉我，说自打登普西对菲尔波那场拳赛以来，他再没见过那么高涨的热情，还说全拉奎特俱乐部要集体到场。接下来我们就得筹划请哪些姑娘来我的初入社交界聚会了。因为到现在我好像还没结识多少社交界的太太们——当然了，一个姑娘不把初入社交界办完，是见不着社交界的太太们的，办完了，太太们才会全体登门拜访初入社交界的少女。可社交界的先生们我差不多全认识，因为社交界的先生们差不多全都是拉奎特俱乐部的，所以等我的初入社交界聚会上有了拉奎特俱乐部，我要想在社交界占到我真正的位置，只差认识他们的母亲和姐妹了，因为他们那些情人，我现在差不多全认识了。
不过我一向觉得，一个姑娘的聚会上要是有相当多的绅士，那再有很多姑娘也实在叫人愉快，《福利斯》歌舞团（the Follies）的姑娘们全来就再愉快不过了，可我实在不能请她们，因为说到底，她们不在我这个圈子里。后来我把这事前后一想，觉得请她们来参加聚会虽然不合礼数，雇她们来聚会当助兴演出倒是合礼数的，她们一旦当了助兴演出，就可以混进聚会里来，实在算不上社交失礼。
这时电话响了，多萝西接的，原来是乔·桑吉内蒂（Joe Sanguinetti），他差不多算整个拉奎特俱乐部的官家私酒贩子。乔说他听说了我的初入社交界，问他能不能来，还带上他的会——布鲁克林「银浪社交会」（Silver Spray Social Club）——他愿意供应全部酒水，还担保差不多能把私酒船队一直开到家门口来。

===Original===
So Dorothy told him he could come, and she hung up the telephone before she told me his proposition, and I became quite angry with Dorothy because, after all, the Silver Spray Social Club is not even mentioned in the Social Register and it has no place at a girl’s debut. But Dorothy said by the time the party got into swing, anyone would have to be a genius if he could tell whether he belonged to the Racquet Club, the Silver Spray Social Club, or the Knights of Pythias. But I really was almost sorry that I asked Dorothy to help plan my debut, except that Dorothy is very good to have at a party if the police come in, because Dorothy always knows how to manage the police, and I never knew a policeman yet who did not finish up by being madly in love with Dorothy. So then Dorothy called up all of the reporters on all of the newspapers and invited them all to my debut, so they could see it with their own eyes.
So Dorothy says that she is going to see to it that my debut lands on the front page of all of the newspapers, if we have to commit a murder to do it.

===Chinese===
多萝西跟他说能来，然后没跟我商量他的提议就把电话挂了，我相当生多萝西的气，因为说到底，「银浪社交会」连《社交名人录》里都没提过一笔，在一个姑娘的初入社交界聚会上根本没有位置。可多萝西说，等聚会热闹起来，谁要还能分清自己属于拉奎特俱乐部、「银浪社交会」还是派西亚骑士会（Knights of Pythias），那他准得是个天才。我真是后悔叫多萝西帮我筹划初入社交界——只有一点例外：聚会上要是警察闯进来，有多萝西在场非常管用，因为多萝西永远有办法对付警察，而且我还没见过哪个警察到头来不疯疯地爱上多萝西的。接着多萝西又给各家报纸的记者全打了电话，把他们全请到我的初入社交界聚会上来，好让他们亲眼瞧瞧。
多萝西还说，她一定设法让我的初入社交界登上所有报纸的头版，非得干一桩凶案才能办到的话，那就干一桩。

===Original===
June 19th:
Well, it has been three days since my debut party started but I finally got tired and left the party last night and went to bed because I always seem to lose all of my interest in a party after a few days, but Dorothy never loses her interest in a party and when I woke up this morning Dorothy was just saying goodbye to some of the guests. I mean Dorothy seems to have quite a lot of vitality, because the last guests of the party were guests we picked up when the party went to take a swim at Long Beach the day before yesterday, and they were practically fresh, but Dorothy had gone clear through the party from beginning to end without even stopping to go to a Turkish bath as most of the gentlemen had to do. So my debut has really been very novel, because quite a lot of the guests who finished up at my debut were not the same guests that started out at it, and it is really quite novel for a girl to have so many different kinds of gentlemen at her debut. So it has really been a very great success because all of the newspapers have quite a lot of write-ups about my debut and I really felt quite proud when I saw the front page of the *Daily Views* and it said in large size headlines, "**Lorelei’s Debut a Wow!**" And *Zits’ Weekly* came right out and said that if this party marks my entrance into society, they only hope that they can live to see what I will spring once I have overcome my debutant reserve and taken my place in the world.
So I really had to apologise to Dorothy about asking Joe Sanguinetti to my debut because it was wonderful the way he got all of the liquor to the party and he more than kept his word. I mean he had his bootleggers run up from the wharf in taxis, right to the apartment, and the only trouble he had was, that once the bootleggers delivered the liquor, he could not get them to leave the party. So finally there was quite a little quarrel because Willie Gwynn claimed that Joe’s bootleggers were snubbing the members of his club because they would not let the boys from the Racquet club sing in their quartet. But Joe’s bootleggers said that the Racquet club boys wanted to sing songs that were unrefined, while they wanted to sing songs about Mother. So then everybody started to take sides, but the girls from the Follies were all with Joe’s bootleggers from the start because practically all we girls were listening to them with tears steaming from our eyes. So that made the Racquet club jealous and one thing led to another until somebody rang for an ambulants and then the police came in.

===Chinese===
6月19日：
嗯，我的初入社交界聚会开场到现在已经三天了，不过昨晚我终于乏了，离席睡觉去了，因为我好像总是一过几天就对聚会兴味全失，多萝西却永远不失对聚会的兴致，今早我醒来的时候，多萝西刚送走几位客人。我是说，多萝西的精力实在相当旺盛，因为聚会最后的一批客人，是我们前天全体去长滩（Long Beach）游泳时半道捡的，还几乎算新鲜面孔，多萝西却从头到尾撑完了整场聚会，中途连土耳其浴都没歇一脚——大多数绅士可都非歇不可。所以我的初入社交界实在非常新奇，因为在我初入社交界聚会上收尾的许多客人，并不是开场的那批客人，一个姑娘的初入社交界聚会上能来这么多五花八门的绅士，实在新奇。所以这实在是一场大大的成功，因为所有报纸对我的初入社交界都登了好些报道，我看到《每日见闻报》（*Daily Views*）头版用大号标题写着「**罗蕾莱初入社交界，火爆全场！**」的时候，实在相当得意。《青春痘周刊》（*Zits’ Weekly*）更干脆直言：要是这场聚会标志着我的社交界入场式，他们只盼自己能活着看到，等我克服了初入社交界少女的矜持、在世上占定位置之后，还会放出什么来。
所以关于把乔·桑吉内蒂请来初入社交界聚会这件事，我实在得向多萝西道歉，因为他把酒水全弄到聚会上来的路数实在妙极了，说到做到还绰绰有余。我是说，他让他的私酒贩子们坐出租车从码头一路赶到公寓，唯一的麻烦是，私酒贩子们一交完酒，他就没法让他们离席了。到后来还小小吵了一场，因为威利·格温咬定乔的私酒贩子们怠慢他们俱乐部的会员——他们不肯让拉奎特俱乐部的少爷们参加他们的四重唱。可乔的私酒贩子们说，拉奎特俱乐部的少爷们要唱的歌没教养，他们要唱的是歌颂母亲的歌。于是人人都开始站队，不过《福利斯》的姑娘们从一开始就全站在乔的私酒贩子们那边，因为我们这些姑娘听他们唱得眼泪热气腾腾地往下淌，几乎无一例外。这就惹得拉奎特俱乐部醋意大发，一桩接着一桩，直到有人按铃叫了救护车，警察这才闯了进来。

===Original===
So Dorothy, as usual, won over all of the police. So it seems that the police all have orders from Judge Schultzmeyer, who is the famous judge who tries all of the prohibition cases, that any time they break into a party that looks like it was going to be a good party, to call him up no matter what time of the day or night it is, because Judge Schultzmeyer dearly loves a party. So the Police called up Judge Schultzmeyer and he was down in less than no time. So during the party both Joe Sanguinetti and Judge Schultzmeyer fell madly in love with Dorothy. So Joe and the Judge had quite a little quarrel and the Judge told Joe that if his stuff was fit to drink he would set the Law after him and confiscate it, but his stuff was not worth the while of any gentleman to confiscate who had any respect for his stomach, and he would not lower himself to confiscate it. So along about nine o’clock in the morning Judge Schultzmeyer had to leave the party and go to court to try all of the criminals who break all of the laws, so he had to leave Dorothy and Joe together and he was very very angry. And I really felt quite sorry for any person who went up before Judge Schultzmeyer that morning, because he gave everybody 90 days and was back at the party by twelve o’clock. So then he stuck to the party until we were all going down to Long Beach to take a swim day before yesterday when he seemed to become unconscious, so we dropped him off at a sanitorium in Garden City.
So my debut party was really the greatest success of the social season, because the second night of my debut party was the night when Willie Gwynn’s sister was having a dance at the Gwynn estate on Long Island, and Willie Gwynn said that all of the eligible gentlemen in New York were conspicuous by their absents at his sister’s party, because they were all at my party. So it seems as if I am really going to be quite a famous hostess if I can just bring my mind to the point of being Mrs. Henry Spoffard Jr.
Well Henry called up this morning and Henry said he had finally got his father’s mind so that he thought it was safe for me to meet him and he was coming up to get me this afternoon so that I can meet his family and see his famous old historical home at Pennsylvania. So then he asked about my debut party which some of the Philadelphia papers seemed to mention. But I told him that my debut was really not so much planned, as it was spontaneous, and I did not have the heart to call him up at a moments notice and take him away from his father at such a time for reasons which were nothing but social.
So now I am getting ready to visit Henry’s family and I feel as if my whole future depends on it. Because if I can not stand Henry’s family any more than I can stand Henry the whole thing will probly come to an end in the law court.

===Chinese===
于是多萝西照例把警察全体争取了过来。原来警察们全得过舒尔茨迈耶法官（Judge Schultzmeyer）的吩咐——他就是专审所有禁酒案的那位有名法官——但凡他们闯进一场看着有前途的聚会，无论白天黑夜什么钟点，都要打电话给他，因为舒尔茨迈耶法官深爱聚会。警察们便打给了舒尔茨迈耶法官，他一眨眼就到了。聚会期间，乔·桑吉内蒂和舒尔茨迈耶法官双双疯狂地爱上了多萝西。乔和法官小小斗了一场，法官对乔说，他那货色要真够格入口，他就派法律去追缴充公，可惜他那货色还不值任何一个爱惜自己肠胃的绅士费神去充公，他才不屑降尊纡贵去充公呢。到早上九点来钟，舒尔茨迈耶法官不得不离席上法庭，去审判所有犯下所有罪行的罪犯，只好把多萝西和乔留在一处，气得非常非常厉害。那天早晨凡是站到舒尔茨迈耶法官面前的人，我实在都替他们难过，因为他判了人人 90 天，十二点就回到聚会上来了。此后他钉在聚会上不肯走，直到前天我们全体要去长滩游泳，他好像昏了过去，我们便把他卸在了加登城（Garden City）的一家疗养院门口。
所以我的初入社交界聚会实在是这个社交季最大的成功，因为我聚会办的第二晚，正赶上威利·格温的妹妹在长岛（Long Island）的格温家宅开舞会，威利·格温说，纽约所有够格的绅士在他妹妹的舞会上全体缺席、缺席得格外扎眼，因为他们全在我的聚会上。这样看来，只要我能把心思拿定到做小亨利·斯波福德夫人（Mrs. Henry Spoffard Jr.）这一步，我实在要成为一位相当有名的女主人了。
嗯，亨利今早来了电话，说他终于把他父亲的工作做到觉得我见他不会有闪失了，今天下午就来接我，好让我见见他的家人，看看宾夕法尼亚那座出了名的古老历史宅邸。接着他问起我的初入社交界聚会——好像有几家费城报纸提了提。可我对他说，我的初入社交界实在说不上筹划，全属一时兴起，我不忍心说叫就叫地打电话，在那样的时候把他从他父亲身边拽走，为的又不过是些纯社交的缘由。
这会儿我正收拾着去见亨利全家，只觉得我的整个前程全系在这一遭上。因为要是我对他家的忍耐连对他本人的忍耐都及不上，这整桩事恐怕就要在法庭上收场了。

===Original===
June 21st:
Well, I am now spending the weekend with Henry’s family at his old family mansion outside of Philadelphia, and I am beginning to think, after all, that there is something else in the world besides family. And I am beginning to think that family life is only fit for those who can stand it. For instants, they always seem to get up very early in Henry’s family. I mean it really is not so bad to get up early when there is something to get up early about, but when a girl gets up early and there is nothing to get up early about, it really begins to seem as if there was no sense to it.
So yesterday we all got up early and that was when I met all of Henry’s family, because Henry and I motored down to Pennsylvania and everybody was in bed when we arrived because it was after nine o’clock. So in the morning Henry’s mother came to my room to get me up in time for breakfast because Henry’s mother is very very fond of me, and she always wants to copy all of my gowns and she always loves to look through all of my things to see what I have got. So she found a box of liqueur candies that are full of liqueurs and she was really very delighted. So I finally got dressed and she threw the empty box away and I helped her downstairs to the Dining room.
So Henry was waiting in the dining room with his sister and that was when I met his sister. So it seems that Henry’s sister has never been the same since the war, because she never had on a man’s collar and a necktie until she drove an ambulants in the war, and now they cannot get her to take them off. Because ever since the armistice Henry’s sister seems to have the idea that regular womens clothes are effiminate. So Henry’s sister seems to think of nothing but either horses or automobiles and when she is not in a garage the only other place she is happy in is a stable. I mean she really pays very little attention to all of her family and she seems to pay less attention to Henry than anybody else because she seems to have the idea that Henry’s brains are not so viril. So then we all waited for Henry’s father to come in so that he could read the Bible out loud before breakfast.
So then something happened that really was a miracle. Because it seems that Henry’s father has practically lived in a wheel chair for months and months and his male nurse has to wheel him everywhere. So his male nurse wheeled him into the dining room in his wheel chair and then Henry said “Father, this is going to be your little daughter in law,” and Henry’s father took one good look at me and got right out of his wheel chair and walked! So then everybody was very very surprised, but Henry was not so surprised because Henry knows his father like a book. So then they all tried to calm his father down, and his father tried to read out of the Bible but he could hardly keep his mind on the Bible and he could hardly eat a bite because when a gentleman is as feeble as Henry’s father is, he cannot keep one eye on a girl and the other eye on his cereal and cream without coming to grief. So Henry finally became quite discouradged and he told his father he would have to get back to his room or he would have a relapse. So then the male nurse wheeled him back to his room and it really was pathetic because he cried like a baby. So I got to thinking over what Dorothy advised me about Henry’s father and I really got to thinking that if Henry’s father could only get away from everybody and have some time of his own, Dorothy’s advise might not be so bad after all.

===Chinese===
6月21日：
嗯，我现在正在费城（Philadelphia）城外亨利家的老宅里，跟他的全家过周末，我开始觉得，这世上毕竟还有家庭以外的别的什么东西。我还开始觉得，家庭生活只配受得住它的人过。比如说，亨利家的人好像总起得非常早。我是说，要是有件什么值得早起的正事，早起倒也不算太糟，可一个姑娘起了个大早，又没有什么值得早起的正事，那实在开始显得毫无道理了。
于是昨天我们全都起了早，我就是在那时见全亨利全家的，因为亨利和我坐汽车下去的宾夕法尼亚，我们到的时候人人都还在床上，因为已经过了九点钟。早上，亨利的母亲到我房里来，赶我起来吃早饭，因为亨利的母亲非常非常喜欢我，总想照着我所有的裙子仿做，还总爱把我所有的东西翻个遍，看我都有什么。于是她翻到了一盒酒心糖——满盒子都是酒心的糖——她真是高兴极了。我好容易穿好衣服，她把空盒子扔了，我搀着她下楼进了饭厅。
亨利和他妹妹在饭厅里等着，我就是在那时认识他妹妹的。原来亨利的妹妹自打大战以后就完全变了个人，因为她直到大战里开救护车，才头一回穿上男人的硬领和领带，如今他们再没法让她摘下来了。因为自停战以来，亨利的妹妹好像认定正经的女人衣裳都娘们儿气。亨利的妹妹好像除了马就是汽车，别的一概不想，她不在车库里的唯一另一个快活去处是马厩。我是说，她对全家人实在没多少心思，对亨利的心思好像比谁都少，因为她好像认定亨利的头脑不够阳刚。于是我们全体等着亨利的父亲进来好念《圣经》，饭前要大声念的。
接着发生了一件真真是奇迹的事。原来亨利的父亲一连好几个月几乎算住在了轮椅里，他的男护士得推着他到处走。男护士把他连人带轮椅推进饭厅，亨利说：「父亲，这位就要做您的小儿媳妇了。」亨利的父亲好好看了我一眼，当场从轮椅里站了起来，自己走了！人人全都非常非常吃惊，亨利倒没那么吃惊，因为亨利对他父亲了如指掌。于是大家全设法让老先生定下神来，他也试着念《圣经》，可他实在难以把心思放在《圣经》上，也实在咽不下一口，因为一位绅士衰弱到亨利父亲这个地步，一只眼睛盯着姑娘、另一只眼睛盯着奶油麦片粥，不出乱子是办不到的。亨利终于相当灰心，对他父亲说，他得回房去了，不然要复发的。男护士就把他推回了房，那场面真真可怜，因为他哭得像个婴孩。我开始琢磨多萝西给我出的关于亨利父亲的主意，还真心琢磨开了：要是亨利的父亲能躲开所有人、有点自己支配的时光，多萝西的主意说不定到底不算坏。

===Original===
So after breakfast we all got ready to go to church, but Henry’s sister does not go to church because Henry’s sister always likes to spend every Sunday in the garage taking their Ford farm truck apart and putting it back together again, and Henry says that what the war did to a girl like his sister is really worse than the war itself.
So then Henry and his mother and I all went to church. So we came home from church and we had luncheon and it seems that luncheon is practically the same as breakfast except that Henry’s father could not come down to luncheon because after he met me he contracted such a vialent fever that they had to send for the Doctor.
So in the afternoon Henry went to prayer meeting and I was left alone with Henry’s mother so that we could rest up so that we could go to church again after supper. So Henry’s mother thinks I am nothing but sunshine and she will hardly let me get out of her sight, because she hates to be by herself because, when she is by herself, her brains hardly seem to work at all. So she loves to try on all of my hats and she loves to tell me how all the boys in the choir can hardly keep their eyes off her. So of course a girl has to agree with her, and it is quite difficult to agree with a person when you have to do it through an ear trumpet because sooner or later your voice has to give out.
So then supper turned out to be practically the same thing as luncheon only by supper time all of the novelty seemed to wear off. So then I told Henry that I had to much of a headache to go to church again, so Henry and his mother went to church and I went to my room and I sat down and thought and I decided that life was really to short to spend it in being proud of your family, even if they did have a great deal of money. So the best thing for me to do is to think up some scheme to make Henry decide not to marry me and take what I can get out of it and be satisfied.

===Chinese===
早饭后我们全体准备上教堂，可亨利的妹妹不上教堂，因为她总喜欢每个礼拜天泡在车库里，把他们那辆福特农用卡车拆了装、装了拆，亨利说，大战对他妹妹这样的姑娘干下的事，实在比大战本身还糟。
于是亨利、他母亲和我一同上了教堂。从教堂回来我们吃了午饭，午饭跟早饭实在差不多一模一样，只除了亨利的父亲下不来吃午饭，因为自打见了我，他就发起了那样一场凶猛的高烧，他们只好去请大夫。
下午亨利去了祷告会，留下我单独陪着亨利的母亲歇息，好养足精神晚饭后再上一次教堂。亨利的母亲觉得我纯粹是一团阳光，几乎不让我离开她的视线，因为她恨独自待着——她一独自待着，她的头脑几乎就一点也不转了。她喜欢把我所有的帽子挨个试戴，还爱告诉我，唱诗班的男孩们眼睛简直没法从她身上挪开。当然了，一个姑娘只好附和她，可隔着助听筒附和一个人实在相当费劲，因为你的嗓子迟早要哑。
到了晚饭，实在又是跟午饭差不离的一套，只是到了晚饭时分，所有的新鲜劲好像全磨没了。于是我对亨利说，我头疼得厉害，没法再上教堂，亨利和他母亲上了教堂，我回到自己房里坐下琢磨，我断定，人生实在过于短促，犯不着把工夫花在为你的家族自豪上，哪怕他们真有偌大一份家财。所以我最好的一条路，就是想出个什么妙计，让亨利自己决定不娶我，然后把我能到手的拿到手，心满意足。

===Original===
June 22nd:
Well, yesterday I made Henry put me on the train at Philadelphia and I made him stay at Philadelphia so he could be near his father if his father seemed to take any more relapses. So I sat in my drawing room on the train and I decided that the time had come to get rid of Henry at any cost. So I decided that the thing that discouradges gentlemen more than anything else is shopping. Because even Mr. Eisman, who was practically born for we girls to shop on, and who knows just what to expect, often gets quite discouradged over all of my shopping. So I decided I would get to New York and I would go to Cartiers and run up quite a large size bill on Henry’s credit, because after all our engagement has been announced in all of the newspapers, and Henry’s credit is really my credit.
So while I was thinking it all over there was a knock on the drawing room door, so I told him to come in and it was a gentleman who said he had seen me quite a lot in New York and he had always wanted to have an introduction to me, because we had quite a lot of friends who were common. So then he gave me his card and his name was on his card and it was Mr. Gilbertson Montrose and his profession is a senario writer. So then I asked him to sit down and we held a literary conversation.
So I really feel as if yesterday was a turning point in my life, because at last I have met a gentleman who is not only an artist but who has got brains besides. I mean he is the kind of a gentleman that a girl could sit at his feet and listen to for days and days and nearly always learn something or other. Because, after all, there is nothing that gives a girl more of a thrill than brains in a gentleman, especially after a girl has been spending the week end with Henry. So Mr. Montrose talked and talked all of the way to New York and I sat there and did nothing else but listen. So according to Mr. Montrose’s opinion Shakespear is a very great playwrite, and he thinks that Hamlet is quite a famous tragedy and as far as novels are concerned he believes that nearly everybody had ought to read Dickens. And when we got on the subject of poetry he recited “The Shooting of Dan McGrew” until you could almost hear the gun go off.

===Chinese===
6月22日：
嗯，昨天我让亨利在费城把我送上了火车，还让他留在费城，好守着他父亲，万一他父亲再犯什么复发。我坐在火车的包厢里，断定甩掉亨利的时辰到了，不惜任何代价。我断定，比什么都更叫绅士灰心的，是买东西。因为连艾斯曼先生——他简直可以说就是生来供我们姑娘买东西花的，又有十足的心理准备——见了我买的东西都常常相当灰心。于是我打定主意，一到纽约就去卡地亚，用亨利的信用大大地刷上一笔账，因为说到底，我们的订婚所有报纸都登过了，亨利的信用实在就是我的信用。
我正前后琢磨着，包厢门上响起敲门声，我叫他进来，进来的是位先生，说他在纽约常常见到我，一直盼着有人引见我，因为我们俩有不少朋友是共通的。接着他递上名片，名片上印着他的名字：吉尔伯森·蒙特罗斯先生（Mr. Gilbertson Montrose），他的职业是写剧本的。于是我请他坐下，我们进行了一场文学谈话。
我实在觉得，昨天是我人生的一个转折点，因为我终于遇到了一位不光是艺术家、而且还兼有头脑的绅士。我是说，他是那种姑娘可以坐在他脚边一听好些天、几乎总能学到点什么的绅士。因为说到底，再没有什么比一位绅士的头脑更让姑娘心潮澎湃的了，尤其在一个姑娘刚跟亨利过完一个周末之后。蒙特罗斯先生一路讲到纽约，讲啊讲啊，我坐在那儿什么也不干，光听。按照蒙特罗斯先生的高见，莎士比亚是一位非常伟大的剧作家；他认为《哈姆雷特》是相当有名的悲剧；至于小说嘛，他相信几乎人人都该读一读狄更斯。等谈到诗歌，他又朗诵起《射杀丹·麦格鲁》（“The Shooting of Dan McGrew”）来，朗诵得你几乎能听见枪响。

===Original===
And then I asked Mr. Montrose to tell me all about himself. So it seems that Mr. Montrose was on his way home from Washington D. C., where he went to see the Bulgarian Ambassadore to see if he could get Bulgaria to finance a senario he has written which is a great historical subject which is founded on the sex life of Dolly Madison. So it seems that Mr. Montrose has met quite a lot of Bulgarians in a Bulgarian restaurant on Lexington Avenue and that was what gave him the idea to get the money from Bulgaria. Because Mr. Montrose said that he could fill his senario full of Bulgarian propoganda, and he told the Bulgarian Ambassadore that every time he realised how ignorant all of the American film fans were on the subject of Bulgaria, it made him flinch.
So I told Mr. Montrose that it made me feel very very small to talk to a gentleman like he, who knew so much about Bulgaria, because practically all I knew about Bulgaria was Zoolack. So Mr. Montrose said that the Bulgarian Ambassadore did not seem to think that Dolly Madison had so much about her that was pertinent to present day Bulgaria, but Mr. Montrose explained to him that that was because he knew practically nothing about dramatic construction. Because Mr. Montrose said he could fix his senario so that Dolly Madison would have one lover who was a Bulgarian, who wanted to marry her. So then Dolly Madison would get to wondering what her great, great grandchildren would be like if she married a Bulgarian, and then she could sit down and have a vision of Bulgaria in 1925. So that was when Mr. Montrose would take a trip to Bulgaria to photograph the vision. But the Bulgarian Ambassadore turned down the whole proposition, but he gave Mr. Montrose quite a large size bottle of the Bulgarian national drink. So the Bulgarian national drink looks like nothing so much as water, and it really does not taste so strong, but about five minutes afterwards you begin to realise your mistake. But I thought to myself that if realizing my mistake could make me forget what I went through in Pennsylvania, I really owed it to myself to forget everything. So then we had another drink.
So then Mr. Montrose told me that he had quite a hard time getting along in the motion picture profession, because all of his senarios are all over their head. Because when Mr. Montrose writes about sex, it is full of sychology, but when everybody else writes about it, it is full of nothing but transparent negligays and ornamental bath tubs. And Mr. Montrose says that there is no future in the motion pictures until the motion pictures get their sex motives straightened out, and realize that a woman of 25 can have just as many sex problems as a flapper of 16. Because Mr. Montrose likes to write about women of the world, and he refuses to have women of the world played by small size girls of 15 who know nothing about life and who have not even been in the detention home.

===Chinese===
接着我请蒙特罗斯先生把他的事讲给我听。原来蒙特罗斯先生是刚从华盛顿特区回来，他去那儿见了保加利亚大使，看能不能让保加利亚出资拍他写的一个剧本，那是个宏大的历史题材，取材于多莉·麦迪逊（Dolly Madison）的性生活。据说蒙特罗斯先生在列克星敦大道（Lexington Avenue）上一家保加利亚餐馆里结识了相当多保加利亚人，这才萌生了去保加利亚弄钱的念头。因为蒙特罗斯先生说，他可以把他的剧本塞满保加利亚宣传，他还对保加利亚大使说，每当他意识到美国的影迷们对保加利亚这个题目何等无知，他就心疼得直抽抽。
于是我对蒙特罗斯先生说，跟他这样一位对保加利亚无所不晓的绅士谈话，我觉得自己非常非常渺小，因为我对保加利亚几乎全部的了解，就是祖拉克（Zoolack）。蒙特罗斯先生说，保加利亚大使似乎并不认为多莉·麦迪逊身上有多少跟当今保加利亚相干的东西，可蒙特罗斯先生向他解释，那是因为他对戏剧结构几乎一窍不通。因为蒙特罗斯先生说，他可以把剧本安排成让多莉·麦迪逊有一位情人是保加利亚人，一心想娶她。这样多莉·麦迪逊就会开始琢磨，她要是嫁了个保加利亚人，她的曾曾孙辈会是什么模样，然后她就可以坐下来，幻见 1925 年的保加利亚。到那时蒙特罗斯先生就启程去保加利亚，把那幻象拍下来。可保加利亚大使把整个提议驳回了，只送了蒙特罗斯先生好大一瓶保加利亚国酒。保加利亚国酒看上去最像不过白水，喝着也实在不算烈，可大约五分钟之后，你就开始意识到自己错了。可我心里盘算，要是意识到自己错了能让我忘掉在宾夕法尼亚受的那些罪，那我实在欠自己一个把什么都忘掉。于是我们又喝了一杯。
接着蒙特罗斯先生告诉我，他在电影界混得相当艰难，因为他所有的剧本全都高深得越过众人的头顶。因为蒙特罗斯先生写性的时候，字里行间全是心理学，可换别人写，就净是透明晨衣和装饰性浴缸，别的什么也没有。蒙特罗斯先生说，电影不把性的动机理顺、不认识到一个 25 岁的女人能有一个 16 岁的毛头丫头同样多的性苦闷之前，电影是没有前途的。因为蒙特罗斯先生爱写饱谙世故的女人，而且他拒绝让那些 15 岁的小个子姑娘来演饱谙世故的女人——她们对人生一无所知，连感化院都没进去过。

===Original===
So we both arrived in New York before we realized it, and I got to thinking how the same trip with Henry in his Rolls Royce seemed like about 24 hours, and that was what gave me the idea that money was not everything, because after all, it is only brains that count. So Mr. Montrose took me home and we are going to have luncheon together at the Primrose Tea room practically every day and keep right on holding literary conversations.
So then I had to figure out how to get rid of Henry and at the same time not do anything that would make me any trouble later. So I sent for Dorothy because Dorothy is not so good at intreeging a gentleman with money, but she ought to be full of ideas on how to get rid of one.
So at first Dorothy said, Why didn’t I take a chance and marry Henry because she had an idea that if Henry married me he would commit suicide about two weeks later. But I told her about my plan to do quite a lot of shopping, and I told her that I would send for Henry and I would manage it so that I would not be in the apartment when he came, but she could be there and start a conversation with him and she could tell him about all of my shopping and how extravagant I seem to be and he would be in the poor house in less than a year if he married me.
So Dorothy said for me to take one farewell look at Henry and leave him to her, because the next time I saw him would be in the witness box and I might not even recognize him because she would throw a scare into him that might change his whole physical appearance. So I decided to leave him in the hands of Dorothy and hope for the best.

===Chinese===
不知不觉我们俩就到了纽约，我想起同样一段路，跟亨利坐劳斯莱斯走，简直像要走 24 个钟头，这让我悟出一个道理：钱不是一切，因为说到底，管用的只有头脑。蒙特罗斯先生送我回了家，我们打算差不多每天在报春花茶室（Primrose Tea Room）一起吃午饭，把文学谈话一直进行下去。
接下来我就得琢磨怎么甩掉亨利，同时又不做任何往后给我惹麻烦的事。我派人去叫多萝西，因为多萝西勾住有钱绅士的本事不算高明，可说到怎么甩掉一个，她该满肚子都是主意。
起初多萝西说，我干嘛不碰碰运气嫁了亨利，因为她有种感觉，亨利要是娶了我，不出两礼拜就得自杀。可我把大买特买的计划讲给了她，还说我去叫亨利来，我会安排好，他来的时候我不在公寓，她在场跟他开个话头，把我买东西的事讲给他听，讲我显得多么挥霍无度，他要是娶了我，不出一年就得进贫民院。
多萝西说，让我跟亨利诀别似的看上最后一眼，把他交给她，因为下回我再见到他，就是在证人席上了，而且我可能都认不出他来，因为她要吓唬他一顿，兴许把他整个外貌都吓变了。于是我决定把亨利交到多萝西手里，往最好处指望。

===Original===
July 10th:
Well, last month was really almost a diary in itself, and I have to begin to realize that I am one of the kind of girls that things happen to. And I have to admit, after all, that life is really wonderful. Because so much has happened in the last few weeks that it almost makes a girl’s brains whirl.
I mean in the first place I went shopping at Cartiers and bought quite a delightful square cut emerald and quite a long rope of pearls on Henry’s credit. So then I called up Henry on the long distants telephone and told him that I wanted to see him quite a lot, so he was very very pleased and he said that he would come right up to New York.
So then I told Dorothy to come to the apartment and be there when Henry came, and to show Henry what I bought on his credit, and to tell him how extravagant I seem to be, and how I seem to keep on getting worse. So I told Dorothy to go as far as she liked, so long as she did not insinuate anything against my character, because the more spotless my character seems to be, the better things might turn out later.
